# Literature check of 2026-11-12 (second pass): record

This file records the prior-art search made for the configurations of [`findings.md`](findings.md) after the adversarial review of the study. It was produced by automated search agents and is kept as a record: Section 3 of `findings.md` gives the conclusions. A first check (15 to 30 queries per candidate configuration, before the experiments) is summarized there as well.

**Method.** Five search agents, one per group of configurations (110 web searches), each asked to find prior art for the configurations of its group and to open the candidate papers (abstract page, then the algorithm box or the theorem). Then four agents (69 web searches) were given the reports of the first five and asked to refute their verdicts from another vocabulary (Frank-Wolfe, preconditioning, sign / normalized steps and compression, the 2025-2026 Muon literature). A last agent opened the newly cited identifiers again and compared them with what was claimed. Verdicts: *published (same)*: the same algorithmic configuration or statement was found; *close work published*: the closest work shares the main idea and differs in a way that matters; *not found*: these queries found nothing closer than what the study already cited.

**Limits.** A web search engine returning about ten links per query, not a full-text or citation search of arXiv; workshop listings and OpenReview could not be read; code hosts, theses and non-English sources were not searched. Long papers were often read through a summarizing fetch tool (first 100,000 characters): section, theorem and equation numbers quoted below should be re-read in the papers before being quoted elsewhere. 'Not found' means 'not found by these queries on 2026-11-12'. Remarks marked as the agent's own derivation come from no paper.

## Reports of the search agents

### Frozen polar metric, steps on exact gradients

**1. one-shot polar-metric SAGA (and its damped version)**: close work published.

*Closest work.* No work was found that builds the frozen preconditioner of SAGA / SVRG from the polar decomposition of one full gradient. Closest on the variance-reduction side (a preconditioner computed once at the start, then SAGA / SVRG on the reparametrized problem): (a) Kereta, Twyman, Arridge, Thielemans, Jin, 'Stochastic EM methods with Variance Reduction for Penalised PET Reconstructions', 2021, arXiv:2106.03172 [opened; PDF text read: Section 4, Eq. (4.3)-(4.4), preconditioned SAGA and SVRG; 'A simple remedy is to freeze the preconditioner, d_t(f^(k)) = d, where d is constant and given as d = d(f^(0))'; Theorem 4.1, almost sure convergence with a constant diagonal preconditioner]. (b) PROMISE, Frangella, Rathore, Zhao, Udell, arXiv:2309.02014 [already in the table; opened; PDF text read: Section 3.2, 'Default hyperparameters' of SketchySVRG: update times U = {0, u, 2u, ...} with u = infinity 'for problems with a constant Hessian (i.e., we only update the preconditioner once in total), like least squares/ridge regression'; Section 3.3, Algorithm 4 SketchySAGA: 'We recommend setting b_g, b_H, and U similar to SketchySVRG'; Theorem 20 (local convergence) is stated with U = {0}]. (c) Gonen, Orabona, Shalev-Shwartz, 'Solving Ridge Regression using Sketched Preconditioned SVRG', 2016, arXiv:1602.02350 [opened; PDF text read: introduction and Algorithm 3: preconditioner formed once from a block-Lanczos low-rank approximation, then SVRG; 'the pd matrix that minimizes the average condition number is P = C + lambda I, and the corresponding average condition number is d']. (d) Sadiev et al., 'Stochastic Gradient Methods with Preconditioned Updates', arXiv:2206.00285 [opened; HTML first 100k characters via the fetch tool's summary: Scaled SARAH / Scaled L-SVRG, Algorithms 1-2; with beta = 1 the diagonal Hutchinson preconditioner D_0 built at the starting point is kept for all iterations; linear rate under PL, Theorems 2 and 4]. (e) Liu, Feng, Yin, arXiv:1905.09734 [abstract page only: 'fixed preconditioners']. Closest on the polar side (a polar transform computed at one step and applied linearly to later stochastic directions): CacheMuon, Dev, Bohara, Takac, Horvath, arXiv:2606.16371 [in the table for another row; opened; PDF text read: Sections 3.2-3.3: FreshGNS returns a left transform Q_t with polar(X_t) ~ Q_t X_t and is run at the first step to seed the cache; on reuse steps X_t = M_t / (||M_t||_F + eps) and the direction is Q_{a_t} X_t].

*What differs.* The structure is published: SAGA / SVRG with a fixed preconditioner is the Euclidean method on a linearly reparametrized problem, and freezing a state-dependent preconditioner at its value at the starting point is exactly what Kereta et al. do. What was not found is the source of the metric: C = (G0^T G0)^(-1/2) read off the polar decomposition of the single full gradient at W_0 (right-sided, floored, normalized), which makes the first exact step the nuclear-scaled polar step. The published one-shot preconditioners are curvature objects (Hessian sketches in PROMISE, a low-rank approximation of the covariance in Gonen et al., a Hutchinson diagonal in Sadiev et al.) or an iterate-dependent EM scaling (Kereta et al.); CacheMuon reuses a polar transform but has no finite-sum estimator, normalizes by the Frobenius norm of the momentum and refreshes on a residual test. closer_than_table is set to true for two reasons, neither of which is a single paper containing the method: the table lists 'one-shot version' as a difference although one-shot preconditioned SAGA / SVRG is published (Kereta et al.; Gonen et al.; PROMISE's own setting for least squares), and CacheMuon is closer than one-sided Shampoo on the polar side. Second point for the report: on the instance where the method helps (ls-orth, K = d, error proportional to an orthogonal matrix, no active floor) C = tr(H) H^(-1) by the identity of item 5, which is published, so the iteration coincides (up to the label noise and the floor) with SAGA preconditioned by the exact Hessian, which Gonen et al. call the optimal but impractical preconditioner; the new element is only that the metric is obtained from one gradient without forming H. The damped (theta < 1) version in a frozen metric was not found; by the same reparametrization it is the damped SAGA estimator on the reparametrized problem.

*Queries.* Q1 (extended): variance reduction SVRG OR SAGA with Shampoo OR 'Kronecker-factored' OR 'one-sided' matrix preconditioner, finite-sum, preconditioner built from the full gradient at the snapshot. Q3 (extended): preconditioned SAGA OR SVRG with a fixed preconditioner computed once from the initial full gradient, 'polar decomposition' OR '(G^T G)^{-1/2}' OR 'one-sided Shampoo' OR 'orthogonalized gradient', linear convergence finite sum. Q4 (extended): Muon OR PolarGrad stale OR frozen OR cached preconditioner (G G^T)^{-1/2} reused across steps, update linear in the current gradient, 'Shampoo without accumulation' lazy refresh every k steps. Q11 (extended): 'polar preconditioner' OR 'Muon preconditioner' OR 'linearized Muon' OR 'frozen Muon' fixed metric from the polar decomposition of one gradient applied linearly to later stochastic gradients, preconditioned SGD matrix weights. Q13 (standard): K-FAC OR 'Kronecker-factored' OR Shampoo OR 'natural gradient' combined with SVRG OR SAGA variance reduction, preconditioned variance-reduced gradient, matrix-structured preconditioner. Q19 (extended): variance reduction SVRG OR SAGA OR SARAH with gradient-based (AdaGrad-type, not Hessian) preconditioner computed from the full gradient at the first snapshot and then kept frozen, 'fixed preconditioner' full-matrix. Q21 (extended): SOAP OR ASGO OR 'one-sided Shampoo' OR 'whitening optimizer' combined with SVRG OR SAGA OR finite-sum variance-reduced gradient estimator, convex finite sum, preconditioner from gradients frozen between snapshots.

**2. lazily refreshed polar-metric SAGA / L-SVRG**: close work published.

*Closest work.* (a) OSGM-SVRG: Zhang, Gao, Ye, Udell, 'Stochastic Gradient Methods with Online Scaling', arXiv:2609.11751 (v1 10 Sep 2026) [opened; abs page, and HTML v2 first 100k of 191k characters via the fetch tool's summary, asked twice: Algorithm 3: inner step x_{t+1} = x_t - c P_k g_t + beta_k (x_t - x_{t-1}) with g_t the SVRG estimator; P_k is updated once per epoch (line 10) by a projected online-gradient step on a ratio or hypergradient feedback evaluated at the snapshot with the full gradient, and stays fixed in the inner loop; Theorem 4.8: linear rate for strongly convex finite sums, covering a scalar, a diagonal and a full symmetric matrix P]. (b) CacheMuon, arXiv:2606.16371 [opened; PDF text read, Sections 3.2-3.3, Theorem 4.3: cached orthogonalizing transform, refreshed when a residual test fails and applied linearly to the normalized momentum in between; stochastic nonconvex bound of order N^(-1/4)]. (c) AdaSVRG: Dubois-Taine, Vaswani, Babanezhad, Schmidt, Lacoste-Julien, 'SVRG meets AdaGrad: painless variance reduction', arXiv:2102.09645 [opened; abs page and ar5iv HTML via the fetch tool's summary: Algorithm 1: G_0 = 0 and x_1 = w_k at each outer loop, A_t = G_t^(1/2), step x_t - eta_k A_t^(-1) g_t; scalar variant in the main text, diagonal and full-matrix variants in the appendix; O(1/K) rate, Theorem 1]. (d) SVRG-BB: Tan, Ma, Dai, Qian, 'Barzilai-Borwein Step Size for Stochastic Gradient Descent', arXiv:1605.04131 [opened; abs page and ar5iv HTML via the fetch tool's summary: Algorithm 2, Eq. (3.2), Theorem 3.8: step size computed once per epoch from the full gradients at two consecutive snapshots, fixed in the inner loop, linear rate]. (e) PROMISE, arXiv:2309.02014 [in the table; opened; PDF text read] remains the closest for the SAGA version with a refresh every n iterations: its recommended refresh period for losses with a non-constant Hessian is one pass (u = n / b_g, Section 3.2).

*What differs.* Same skeleton (a variance-reduced estimator multiplied by a metric that is refreshed with the reference point, or once per pass, and frozen in between), different metric. OSGM-SVRG learns P by one online-gradient step per epoch on a feedback that needs f at the trial point x - P grad f(x); PROMISE sketches the Hessian. The study sets C in closed form to the polar metric (D^T D)^(-1/2) of the exact gradient at the reference point (L-SVRG) or of the SAGA predictable part every n iterations, for a matrix parameter, so that the step at the anchor is the nuclear-scaled polar step. CacheMuon has the lazily refreshed polar transform applied linearly, but to a momentum of minibatch gradients, with Frobenius normalization and a residual-triggered refresh, without finite-sum estimator and without convex analysis. AdaSVRG builds an AdaGrad preconditioner from gradients inside SVRG and restarts it at each snapshot (its first inner step uses the exact snapshot gradient), but the preconditioner keeps accumulating in the inner loop, so the step is not linear in the estimator, and no one-sided matrix structure is used. Not found: a polar or Shampoo-type metric refreshed at the reference point of SVRG, or built from a SAGA table. closer_than_table is true because OSGM-SVRG (refresh at the snapshot from first-order information only, frozen in the inner loop, linear rate) and CacheMuon (frozen polar transform) are each closer than the two works of the row, PROMISE (curvature sketches) and one-sided Shampoo (accumulated, never frozen, no variance reduction).

*Queries.* Shared with item 1: Q1, Q4, Q13, Q21. Own queries: Q5 (standard): 'SVRG' 'Shampoo' preconditioner variance reduction matrix parameters snapshot full gradient Kronecker preconditioner updated once per epoch. Q8 (extended): SVRG preconditioner recomputed at each snapshot from the full gradient only (no Hessian), frozen during the inner loop: full-matrix AdaGrad OR Shampoo OR 'Barzilai-Borwein' diagonal OR matrix step size, linear convergence.

**3. anchor-PolarGrad L-SVRG**: not found.

*Closest work.* Nothing closer than the table. Checked: SARAH, Nguyen, Liu, Scheinberg, Takac, arXiv:1703.00102 [opened; ar5iv HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Algorithm 1: at each outer loop v_0 is the full gradient at w_0 and the iterate takes w_1 = w_0 - eta v_0, the same Euclidean step as in the inner loop]. Neighbours opened: SPIDER, Fang et al., arXiv:1807.01695 [ar5iv HTML first 100k characters, Algorithm 1: Option I takes a normalized step of fixed length at every iteration, with the exact gradient at the checkpoints in the finite-sum case]; AdaSVRG, arXiv:2102.09645 [ar5iv HTML, Algorithm 1: the accumulator is reset at each outer loop, so the first inner step is eta_k grad f(w_k) / ||grad f(w_k)||, a normalized exact-gradient step; this is a reading of the pseudocode, the paper does not comment on it]; MuonBP, Khaled, Ozkara, Yu, Hong, Park, arXiv:2510.16981 [HTML first 100k characters, Algorithm 1: full orthogonalization of the momentum every P steps with its own step size, blockwise orthogonalization otherwise; stochastic gradients only]; SVRN, Derezinski, arXiv:2206.02702 [ar5iv HTML first 100k characters, Algorithm 1 and Section 4: a first phase of Newton-type steps with full gradients, then one switch to variance-reduced Newton steps]; OSGM-SVRG, arXiv:2609.11751 [HTML, Algorithm 3: the full-gradient trial point x - P grad f(x) is evaluated at each snapshot for the feedback, but the iterate does not move there].

*What differs.* In SARAH (and PAGE, as cited in the table) the full-gradient step at a refresh is the ordinary Euclidean step of the method with an exact estimator. In SPIDER Option I, and in the variance-reduced LMO methods cited in findings.md (Li-Tang-Zou, Qian et al.; not re-opened here), the nonlinear map is applied at every iteration, to stochastic estimators as well. In MuonBP the periodic full orthogonalization acts on a stochastic momentum. SVRN separates a full-gradient second-order phase from a variance-reduced phase but switches once and keeps the same metric in both. A Euclidean variance-reduced method whose only non-Euclidean step (spectral, nuclear-scaled) is taken on the exact gradient at each refresh of the reference point was not found. The one-step decrease of that anchor step is published on quadratics: Theorem 3, Eq. (17) of arXiv:2610.30280 [PDF text read] gives f(X+)/f(X) = 1 - alpha (2 - alpha) p(X) for the step gamma ||G||_* polar(G) with alpha = gamma tr(H), next to PolarGrad's Theorem 3.2 already cited.

*Queries.* Q6 (extended): loopless SVRG OR SARAH OR PAGE with a spectral OR orthogonalized OR 'steepest descent' step using the exact full gradient at each snapshot refresh, Euclidean stochastic steps in between, Muon hybrid 'full gradient'. Q12 (extended): hybrid optimizer alternating Muon OR spectral OR orthogonalized steps on full-batch (exact) gradients with plain SGD or variance-reduced steps on small batches, periodic full-gradient polar step, checkpoint. Q14 (extended): SARAH OR SPIDER OR SVRG where the linear minimization oracle or normalized/steepest-descent step is applied only at the checkpoint with the full gradient, ordinary gradient steps in the inner loop, non-Euclidean step at snapshot. Q22 (extended): SVRG OR 'loopless SVRG' variant that takes an extra deterministic step with the full gradient at each snapshot (full gradient step, line search, Newton, normalized or spectral step at the anchor point) before the stochastic inner loop. Shared with item 4: Q20.

**4. lazy split L-SVRG-Muon**: not found.

*Closest work.* Nothing closer than the works of the table: CacheMuon, arXiv:2606.16371 [opened; PDF text read, Sections 3.2-3.3]; MuonBP, arXiv:2510.16981 [opened; HTML first 100k characters via the fetch tool's summary, Algorithm 1]; PROMISE, arXiv:2309.02014 [opened; PDF text read]. Neighbours found and opened: 'FedMuon: Accelerating Federated Learning with Matrix Orthogonalization', arXiv:2510.27403 [HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Algorithm 1: local step x <- x - eta ((1 - alpha) U V^T + alpha Delta_G), U V^T the polar factor of the local momentum, Delta_G a non-orthogonalized global direction kept fixed for the K local steps; authors not noted]; FedPAC, 'Taming Preconditioner Drift: Unlocking the Potential of Second-Order Optimizers for Federated Learning on Non-IID Data', arXiv:2602.19271 [HTML first 100k characters, Algorithm 2: x <- x - eta ((1 - beta) P_Theta(g) + beta g_G), instances SOAP, Sophia, Muon]; Che, 'Second-Order Muon Done Right', arXiv:2608.09763 [HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Section 3.5: curvature factors refreshed every K = 4 updates and reused, polar map recomputed at every step]; Du and Su, 'The Newton-Muon Optimizer', arXiv:2604.01472 [PDF text read: the inverse of the input second moment is recomputed periodically and 'the cached inverse is reused' between refreshes, the matrix sign is recomputed every step]; MiMuon, Huang, Luo, Chen, arXiv:2605.19619 [abs page and HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Algorithm 2: hard switch between an orthogonalized step and a momentum-SGD step according to a singular-value-gap test].

*What differs.* CacheMuon passes the whole fresh momentum through a stale linear transform (no separate linear correction); MuonBP alternates blockwise and full orthogonalization; 'Second-Order Muon Done Right' and Newton-Muon freeze curvature factors for several steps but recompute the polar factor at every step; FedMuon (arXiv:2510.27403) and FedPAC add a per-round frozen, non-orthogonalized global direction linearly to an orthogonalized local direction, which is the mirror placement (map on the stochastic part, frozen part linear). The lazy split step, gamma sharp(grad F(w_ref)) computed once per reference point plus the linear correction gamma_c (SVRG innovation + lam (W - w_ref)), was not found. With the map removed, the step gamma grad F(w_ref) + gamma_c c is an SVRG estimator with unequal weights on the anchor gradient and on the correction, i.e. the SVRG counterpart of the damped estimator of the B-SAGA row (my derivation, not checked against a source in this run).

*Queries.* Q7 (extended): SVRG variant where only the snapshot full gradient is passed through a nonlinear map (sign, normalization, clipping, orthogonalization) and the stochastic gradient-difference correction is added linearly without the map. Q9 (extended): 'Muon' 'SVRG' OR 'SAGA' OR 'snapshot' 2026 arXiv orthogonalization computed once per epoch at the reference point, reused in the inner loop, 'variance reduction' polar factor lazy. Q10 (extended): variance-reduced stochastic method matrix optimization 'one SVD per epoch' OR 'SVD only at the snapshot' OR 'full SVD once per epoch' SVRG nuclear norm low-rank inner iterations without SVD. Q16 (extended): Muon amortized orthogonalization: cached or stale orthogonalized direction reused for several steps plus a linear correction with the fresh gradient difference between refreshes. Q20 (extended): federated or local-update Muon: orthogonalization applied only at the server to the aggregated full gradient once per round, clients take plain SGD local steps with SCAFFOLD or SVRG control-variate correction, reuse of the orthogonalized global direction.

**5. structural fact F10: the polar metric of one gradient is the Newton metric at an orthogonal error**: published (same).

*Closest work.* Shulgin, Laing, Orvieto, Richtarik, 'A Quadratic Lens on Muon's Polar Update: Benefits, Pitfalls, and the Role of Newton-Schulz', arXiv:2610.30280v1, 21 Oct 2026 [opened; abs page, HTML, and PDF text read directly: Theorem 1(iii) and the paragraph after it, Section 4.1 Eq. (10)-(13) with Proposition 2, Theorem 3 Eq. (14)-(17), proof of Theorem 1 in Appendix B.3]. Its shorter workshop version, 'A Quadratic Lens on Muon: Orthogonalization, Invariance, and Implicit Preconditioning', ICML 2026 HiLD workshop, https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/72378 [listing opened, abstract only; OpenReview forum and PDF TVTwgZp0qe not readable: browser-verification page]. Same mechanism in a Kronecker model: Du and Su, 'The Newton-Muon Optimizer', arXiv:2604.01472, 1 Apr 2026 [opened; PDF text read: Assumption 1, Proposition 1 with its proof, Theorem 1, Corollary 1, Section 1.2]. Checked and not containing the identity: Ma, Huang, Chi, Chen, arXiv:2601.13474 [HTML characters 0-200k via the fetch tool's summary: Eq. (17) and Appendix A give the right-preconditioner form and a heuristic link with ScaledGD only]; PolarGrad, arXiv:2505.21799 [HTML characters 0-200k: Section 4.4 bounds the gradient condition number by kappa(A) kappa(B) kappa(E) for matrix quadratic regression, no Newton statement]; Su, arXiv:2511.00674, Davis and Drusvyatskiy, arXiv:2512.04299, Gonon, Musat, Boumal, arXiv:2602.11948 [HTML, first 100k characters each]; Xie et al., arXiv:2503.10537 [HTML, three chunks]; Morwani et al., arXiv:2406.17748 [abstract page only]. For the condition number: Gonen, Orabona, Shalev-Shwartz, arXiv:1602.02350 [PDF text read].

*What differs.* Same identity. The paper's objective is f(X) = (1/2) tr((X - X*)^T H (X - X*)), X in R^{d x m}, with H the d x d Hessian acting on the row side; the paper itself instantiates it as multi-output least squares (1/2n)||Z X - Y||_F^2. It is the lab's problem with W = X^T (K = m outputs, H on the feature side). Theorem 1(iii): assuming rank(G) = d <= m, the polar step with exact line search reaches the minimizer if and only if E E^T = c I_d (E = X - X*), 'a scaled orthogonal matrix if m = d', and the text adds that this requires no condition on the Hessian. The proof (Appendix B.3) writes G G^T = H E E^T H = c H^2 and Q = (G G^T)^(-1/2) G = (c H^2)^(-1/2) H E = c^(-1/2) H^(-1) H E = E / sqrt(c): this is F10's statement that the polar preconditioner of the gradient is the inverse Hessian up to scale, in transposed notation. It is stronger than F10 on two points: it is an equivalence, and it holds for K >= d (error with orthonormal columns up to scale, in the lab's orientation), not only for K = d. The paper also contains F10's 'when it is not': the implicit preconditioner A(X) = (H Sigma H)^(-1/2) H, Sigma = X X^T, has the condition number of the error (Proposition 2), and the exact progress coefficient p(X) = ||H X||_*^2 / (tr(H) tr(H X X^T)) equals 1 iff X X^T = c I and can be as small as 4 kappa(X) / (1 + kappa(X))^2 (Theorem 3), which is the published form of 'later anchors give the largest gains to the directions already converged'. Newton-Muon has the same mechanism for the model G = H (W - W*) Z Z^T / N: if (W - W*)(W - W*)^T is proportional to the identity, the Newton step is proportional to msgn(G (Z Z^T)^(-1)) (Theorem 1), and to msgn(G) if moreover Z Z^T is proportional to the identity (Corollary 1). Not found in either paper: the use of this metric as a frozen preconditioner of a stochastic method, and the finite-sum condition number in that metric, kappa_P = d_inf(lam) + lam / lambda_min(H). The nearest published statement is in Gonen et al.: with the exact preconditioner P = C + lam I the average (trace over smallest eigenvalue) condition number that governs their preconditioned SVRG equals d; kappa_P is the uniform-sampling, maximum-leverage counterpart.

*Queries.* Q2 (standard): 'A Quadratic Lens on Muon' Orthogonalization, Invariance, and Implicit Preconditioning Shulgin Laing Orvieto Richtarik. Q15 (extended): Shampoo OR AdaGrad square root justified: gradient Gram matrix G^T G equals Hessian squared when the error (iterate minus optimum) is isotropic or orthogonal, so (G^T G)^{-1/2} is the inverse Hessian, quadratic least squares single gradient. Q17 (standard): preconditioned SVRG OR SAGA ridge regression Hessian preconditioner condition number becomes maximum ridge leverage score 'leverage' statistical preconditioning finite sum. Q18 (extended): orthogonalized gradient OR 'polar factor of the gradient' OR 'matrix sign of the gradient' equals the Newton direction when the error matrix is orthogonal or has orthonormal rows, matrix least squares, one-step convergence regardless of conditioning. The arXiv identifier of the paper was not returned by Q2; it was found on the author page richtarik.org/i_papers.html (entry 330), then confirmed by Q18.

*Other papers reported by this agent.*

- arXiv:2610.30280 (v1, 21 Oct 2026), Shulgin, Laing, Orvieto, Richtarik, 'A Quadratic Lens on Muon's Polar Update: Benefits, Pitfalls, and the Role of Newton-Schulz' [opened: abs, HTML, PDF text read directly]. This is the workshop paper that findings.md (bounded-gain row) says could not be opened. To cite (i) at F10 and in Section 2 for the ls-orth instance: Theorem 1(iii) and Appendix B.3 are the Newton-metric identity, and the paper's remark that orthogonal initialization is special for PolarGD is the planted='orthogonal' construction; (ii) at F10 'when it is not': Proposition 2 and Theorem 3 (the implicit preconditioner has the conditioning of the error; slow regime with tiny singular values); (iii) for the anchor step and the PolarGrad baseline on least squares: Theorem 3, Eq. (17); (iv) in the bounded-gain row: Section 4.2 and Appendices F-G define the damped polar map Q_eps = (G G^T + eps I)^(-1/2) G, sigma -> sigma / sqrt(sigma^2 + eps), and show that finite Newton-Schulz behaves like it (definition read in the PDF text, Appendix G).
- arXiv:2604.01472, Du and Su, 'The Newton-Muon Optimizer' (1 Apr 2026) [opened: abs, HTML, PDF text read: Assumption 1, Proposition 1 and proof, Theorem 1, Corollary 1, Section 1.2]. Same mechanism as F10 for the Kronecker model G = H (W - W*) Z Z^T / N; Section 1.2 states that 'Muon's matrix sign can be interpreted as an implicit left preconditioner that approximates the effect of H^(-1)'. Its right preconditioner, the damped inverse input second moment recomputed periodically with the cached inverse reused in between, is the feature-side statistical metric that the one-shot polar metric reproduces on ls-orth. To cite at F10 and in the polar-metric row.
- arXiv:2601.13474, Ma, Huang, Chi, Chen, 'Preconditioning Benefits of Spectral Orthogonalization in Muon' (20 Jan 2026) [opened: HTML characters 0-200k via the fetch tool's summary]. Eq. (17) and Eq. (79) write the Muon step as the gradient times the right preconditioner (grad f^T grad f)^(-1/2), the same right-sided form as the polar metric C; Appendix A (Eq. 79-83) relates it heuristically to the ScaledGD preconditioner (U^T U)^(-1). To cite where the table attributes D (D^T D)^(-1/2) = polar(D) to one-sided Shampoo.
- arXiv:2503.20762, An, Liu, Pan, Ren, Ma, Goldfarb, Zhang, 'ASGO: Adaptive Structured Gradient Optimization' [opened: HTML first 100k characters via the fetch tool's summary]. One-sided accumulation V_t = V_{t-1} + G_t G_t^T (Algorithm 1, left side) or G_t^T G_t (right-sided variant, preferred in its Section 5), power -1/2; the paper states that without momentum in the gradient and in the preconditioner 'ASGO is equivalent to Muon'. It is the right-sided one-sided Shampoo that actually matches C. Xie et al. (arXiv:2503.10537) define one-sided Shampoo on the left side with accumulation (Algorithm 4) and, in the HTML scanned here, never mention the polar factor.
- arXiv:2609.11751, Zhang, Gao, Ye, Udell, 'Stochastic Gradient Methods with Online Scaling' (Sep 2026) [opened: abs, HTML first 100k characters via the fetch tool's summary]. OSGM-SVRG (Algorithm 3, Theorem 4.8): matrix stepsize multiplying the SVRG estimator, updated once per epoch at the snapshot from full-gradient feedback, frozen in the inner loop, linear rate. Closest published structure to the lazily refreshed polar-metric L-SVRG; to add to the polar-metric row.
- arXiv:2102.09645, Dubois-Taine, Vaswani, Babanezhad, Schmidt, Lacoste-Julien, 'SVRG meets AdaGrad: painless variance reduction' [opened: abs, ar5iv HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Algorithm 1]. A gradient-built AdaGrad preconditioner (scalar, diagonal, full-matrix) inside SVRG, restarted at each snapshot, whose first inner step is a normalized step on the exact snapshot gradient (my reading of the pseudocode). Neighbour of the polar-metric L-SVRG and of the anchor step.
- arXiv:1602.02350, Gonen, Orabona, Shalev-Shwartz, 'Solving Ridge Regression using Sketched Preconditioned SVRG' [opened: abs, PDF text read]. SVRG with a preconditioner computed once; states that the optimal preconditioner is P = C + lambda I, with average condition number d. To cite for the one-shot version and next to kappa_P = d_inf(lam) + lam / lambda_min(H) in F10.
- arXiv:2106.03172, Kereta, Twyman, Arridge, Thielemans, Jin, 'Stochastic EM methods with Variance Reduction for Penalised PET Reconstructions' [opened: abs, PDF text read, Section 4 and Theorem 4.1]. Preconditioned SAGA / SVRG in which a state-dependent preconditioner is frozen at its value at the starting point, for the reason that motivates the metric placement (with iteration-dependent preconditioners the limit does not satisfy the optimality condition). Published precedent of the one-shot construction, with a diagonal EM preconditioner.
- arXiv:2206.00285, Sadiev, Beznosikov, Almansoori, Kamzolov, Tappenden, Takac, 'Stochastic Gradient Methods with Preconditioned Updates' [opened: HTML first 100k characters via the fetch tool's summary] and arXiv:1905.09734, Liu, Feng, Yin, 'Acceleration of SVRG and Katyusha X by Inexact Preconditioning' [abstract page only]. Scaled L-SVRG / SARAH with a diagonal preconditioner kept at its initial value when beta = 1; SVRG and Katyusha X with fixed preconditioners. Further precedents of a frozen metric inside (loopless) SVRG.
- arXiv:1605.04131, Tan, Ma, Dai, Qian, 'Barzilai-Borwein Step Size for Stochastic Gradient Descent' [opened: abs, ar5iv HTML via the fetch tool's summary: Algorithm 2, Eq. (3.2), Theorem 3.8]. SVRG-BB: a scalar metric computed once per epoch from the full gradients at two snapshots and frozen in the inner loop, with a linear rate; scalar ancestor of refreshing the metric with the reference point.
- arXiv:2605.20756, Nayak et al., 'Correcting Stochastic Update Bias in Preconditioned Language Model Optimizers' (20 May 2026) [opened: abs, HTML first 100k characters via the fetch tool's summary]. Names the 'gradient-preconditioner coupling bias' of estimating the gradient and the preconditioner from the same minibatch and removes it by cross-fitting (preconditioner from an independent microbatch group) for AdamW, Sophia and Shampoo; its Theorem 1 gives a conditional mean that is linear in the gradient. Same rationale as taking C known before the index is drawn (metric placement, F10); also relevant to F5 and the joint placement.
- arXiv:2510.27403, 'FedMuon: Accelerating Federated Learning with Matrix Orthogonalization' [opened: HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Algorithm 1; authors not noted] and arXiv:2602.19271, FedPAC, 'Taming Preconditioner Drift: Unlocking the Potential of Second-Order Optimizers for Federated Learning on Non-IID Data' [opened: HTML first 100k characters, Algorithm 2]. Local step (1 - alpha) msgn(local momentum) + alpha Delta_G with a per-round frozen, non-orthogonalized global direction: mirror image of the lazy split step; to add to the split and lazy-split rows. This FedMuon is a different paper from the FedMuon already cited (arXiv:2509.26337).
- arXiv:2608.09763, Che, 'Second-Order Muon Done Right: A Principled Marriage of Spectral Geometry and Curvature' (10 Aug 2026) [opened: HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Section 3.5] and arXiv:2605.19619, Huang, Luo, Chen, 'MiMuon: Mixed Muon Optimizer with Improved Generalization for Large Models' (19 May 2026) [opened: abs and HTML via the fetch tool's summary, Algorithm 2]. Curvature factors refreshed every K = 4 steps and reused while the polar map is recomputed (stale geometry, fresh orthogonalization); hard switch between an orthogonalized step and a momentum-SGD step. Neighbours of the lazy and split rows.
- arXiv:1703.00102, Nguyen, Liu, Scheinberg, Takac, SARAH [opened: ar5iv HTML, Algorithm 1]; arXiv:1807.01695, SPIDER [opened: ar5iv HTML first 100k characters, Algorithm 1]; arXiv:2206.02702, Derezinski, 'Stochastic Variance-Reduced Newton' [opened: ar5iv HTML first 100k characters, Algorithm 1 and Section 4]. Identifiers and algorithm boxes for the anchor row, which cites the full-gradient step of SARAH / PAGE by name only.
- arXiv:2406.17748, Morwani, Shapira, Vyas, Malach, Kakade, Janson, 'A New Perspective on Shampoo's Preconditioner' [abstract page only]. Background, not prior art for F10: the square of Shampoo's approximation is one power-iteration step towards the optimal Kronecker approximation of the Gauss-Newton matrix or of the gradient covariance; this concerns accumulated statistics, not the single-gradient identity.

*Limits stated by this agent.*

- Scope of the negative results: 22 WebSearch calls (the cap), 18 in extended mode and 4 in standard mode. The task suggested about half in extended mode; I used it more because almost every query was a niche combination. 'Not found' means that these 22 queries, whose wording is listed per configuration, returned nothing closer; it is not a proof of absence. The engine's own summaries of the result lists were used as leads only; every paper cited above was then opened.
- How documents were read. WebFetch returns the answer of a small model that reads the page, usually only the first 100k characters. 10-17, Proposition 2, Theorem 3, Appendix B.3, damped polar in Appendix G), arXiv:2604.01472 (Assumption 1, Proposition 1, Theorem 1, Corollary 1, Section 1.2), arXiv:2606.16371 (Sections 3.2-3.3, Theorem 4.3), arXiv:2309.02014 (Sections 3.2-3.3, Theorem 20, related work), arXiv:1602.02350 (introduction, Algorithm 3), arXiv:2106.03172 (Section 4, Theorem 4.1). Everything else rests on the fetch tool's summary: the theorem, equation and algorithm numbers taken from those summaries (OSGM-SVRG, AdaSVRG, SVRG-BB, Sadiev et al., Ma et al., ASGO, Xie et al., PolarGrad, MuonBP, both federated papers, Che, MiMuon, SARAH, SPIDER, SVRN, Nayak et al.) should be re-read before being quoted.
- Item 5, dating and coverage. The arXiv version of the Quadratic Lens paper is dated 21 Oct 2026 and states that a shorter version was presented at the ICML 2026 HiLD workshop. Of the workshop version only the abstract on icml.cc was read (it already states the row-isotropic exactness regime and the implicit iterate-dependent preconditioner); OpenReview (forum and PDF, id TVTwgZp0qe) returned a browser-verification page. The second half of F10, kappa_P = d_inf(lam) + lam / lambda_min(H), was not found stated anywhere, with one standard query only (Q17); it belongs to the statistical-preconditioning note B1 of the main project and was not searched further.
- Corrections to the positioning table, polar-metric row. (i) 'one-shot version' is listed as a difference, but PROMISE itself recommends updating the preconditioner once in total for least squares (Section 3.2 for SketchySVRG, same setting recommended for SketchySAGA in Section 3.3), and Gonen et al. 2016 and Kereta et al. 2021 are one-shot too. (ii) The identity D (D^T D)^(-1/2) = polar(D) is attached to Xie et al.; in the HTML of arXiv:2503.10537 (three chunks via the fetch tool; the last chunk ended with a truncation marker) one-sided Shampoo is left-sided with accumulation and the polar factor is never mentioned. The right-sided form is explicit in Ma et al. (Eq. 17 and 79) and in ASGO; the left-sided form Q = (G G^T)^(-1/2) G is Eq. (10) of arXiv:2610.30280. (iii) CacheMuon, cited only in the lazy-split row, is the published lazily refreshed polar transform and belongs in this row as well.
- Consequence for the claims about ls-orth. Where the one-shot polar metric helps (K = d, error proportional to an orthogonal matrix, no active floor), C is the exact Newton metric by a published identity, so the method coincides, up to the label noise, the floor and the scale normalization, with SAGA preconditioned by the exact Hessian (the optimal preconditioner of Gonen et al.; the statistical preconditioning of the note B1). This follows from F10 as written in notes_theory.md; I ran no code to check it numerically (no Python allowed in this task).
- Not covered. GitHub and other code hosts, theses, blog posts and non-English sources were not searched specifically. Vocabularies suggested by the task that did not get a query of their own: stochastic L-BFGS with SVRG (seen only through PROMISE's related-work paragraph: Moritz et al. 2016, Gower et al. 2016 and 2018, not opened), DANE / SPAG statistical preconditioning, spectral initialization followed by SVRG in low-rank matrix recovery, SPIDER- or SARAH-type Frank-Wolfe. Search hits not opened: arXiv:2011.01718, 2611.05075, 1806.02958, 2506.03595, 2606.02365, 2604.09967, 2605.07067, 2608.05088, 2609.09676, 2605.17806, 2605.07815, 2610.37225, 2608.12710, 2607.24518, 2502.14648, 1802.06232, 1705.09059, and the SVRG-AS report on optimization-online (2019/04/7170). Opened and found irrelevant to this group (abstract or HTML): arXiv:2611.11277, 2606.00371, 2607.13246, 2607.01124, 2603.21230, 2510.03866.
- Files and commands. No Python, no git command, nothing else in the repository touched. In the 'queries' fields the exact-phrase double quotes of the real queries are written as single quotes.

### Placement of the nonlinear map

**1. split placement: step = gamma sharp(m) + gamma_c theta c (map on the predictable part, sampled correction added linearly)**: not found.

*Closest work.* No work found with the nonlinear map on the predictable part of a variance-reduced estimator and the sampled correction kept linear. The table entries stay the closest: PMRC (arXiv:2610.19844; New neighbours at a comparable distance, all opened in this run: (a) 'FedMuon: Accelerating Federated Learning with Matrix Orthogonalization', Junkang Liu, Fanhua Shang, Junchao Zhou, Hongying Liu, Yuanyuan Liu, Jin Liu, 2025, arXiv:2510.27403 (HTML opened; Eq. (3), Algorithm 1 and the definition of Delta_G read through the fetch tool): local step x <- x - eta [(1 - alpha) U V^T + alpha Delta_G], with U V^T the polar factor of the local momentum and Delta_G the averaged displacement of the previous round, added linearly OUTSIDE the orthogonalization. (b) MAGD in 'Nonsmooth Optimization via Orthogonalized Momentum', Lexiao Lai, Tianyi Lin, Jiayu Zhang, 2026, arXiv:2609.13677 (abstract + Algorithm 1 read): convex combination, with a sigmoid weight adapted online, of a Muon branch and a gradient-descent branch. (c) 'MiMuon: Mixed Muon Optimizer with Improved Generalization for Large Models', Feihu Huang, Yuning Luo, Songcan Chen, 2026, arXiv:2605.19619 (abstract + Algorithm 2 read): switches between U V^T and the raw momentum according to a singular-gap test. (d) EF-Muon in 'Muon Does Not Converge on Convex Lipschitz Functions', Parshakova, Khaled, Crawshaw, Garrigos, Gower, 2026, arXiv:2605.08980 (abstract + Algorithm 1 read): P_t = E_t + lambda_t M_t, W_{t+1} = W_t - (1/min(m,n)) ||P_t||_* polar(P_t), E_{t+1} = P_t - C(P_t): error feedback around the nuclear-scaled polar step. (e) 'SUDA-Muon: Structural Design Principles and Boundaries for Fully Decentralized Muon', Hengrui Zhang, Boao Kong, Jiahe Geng, Zhengyang Huang, 2026, arXiv:2604.23980 (abstract + main recursion read): X^{k+1} = A (C X^k - alpha msgn(H^{k+1})) - B Y^k, msgn of the tracked signal plus a linear dual term outside.

*What differs.* None of the works found applies the nonlinear map to the predictable part while keeping the sampled correction linear, and none has a closed-form conditional expectation of its step. What is published is the algebraic form 'polar factor of one component + another component added linearly', with other decompositions. PMRC (table) splits by subspace (msign of the in-subspace momentum, out-of-subspace residual gradient linear). MuSGD (table) passes the same gradient stream through both maps. FedMuon of Liu et al. is the mirror image of the split: the polar factor is taken of the SAMPLED local momentum and the PREDICTABLE term (previous-round global direction) is the one added linearly, with convex weights (1 - alpha, alpha); federated, nonconvex, no finite-sum estimator; the paper presents the term as local-global alignment against client drift, not as variance reduction, and (per the fetch reading of its reference list) does not cite the Takezawa et al. FedMuon, which puts its correction inside the LMO. MAGD mixes two iterate branches; MiMuon switches between the two updates; EF-Muon stores what the nuclear-scaled polar step did not transmit in an error buffer; SUDA-Muon and the Takezawa FedMuon put the correction inside the polar map, and SUDA-Muon argues that this ordering is necessary in the decentralized setting. In the compression and clipping literature the nonlinearity is put on the innovation and the stored shift is kept linear (DIANA and EF21-Muon in the table; Clip21, arXiv:2305.18929, verified here: v_k^i = v_{k-1}^i + clip_tau(grad f_i(x_k) - v_{k-1}^i)): the opposite assignment to the split. None of these is closer than PMRC on the axis that defines the configuration, so the row keeps its closest work; FedMuon (Liu et al.), MAGD and EF-Muon should be added to it. Own remark (a derivation, not from a paper): if the map is replaced by a multiple of the identity, the split step is gamma (m + (gamma_c theta / gamma) c), i.e. SVAG / B-SAGA of the damped row; the split is that two-coefficient form with the sharp map applied to the memory mean.

*Queries.* ASCII transcription, quoted phrases shown with single quotes. Q1 [extended] Muon optimizer SVRG OR SAGA variance reduction orthogonalize snapshot full gradient only, stochastic correction added outside the polar factor (not orthogonalized), finite-sum. Q2 [extended] optimizer update = orthogonalized momentum (Muon, matrix sign) plus raw stochastic gradient term added linearly, hybrid spectral + Euclidean step, 'Muon' 'SGD' combination residual gradient not orthogonalized arXiv 2026. Q3 [extended] nonlinear operator (sign, normalization, LMO, clipping) applied only to the predictable / snapshot part of a variance-reduced gradient estimator, stochastic correction (control variate) added linearly outside the nonlinearity so the step has known conditional expectation. Q11 [extended, shared with item 2] Muon orthogonalization bias under gradient noise E[msign(G)] != msign(E[G]) 'bias correction' OR 'debiased' OR 'unbiased' estimator of the polar factor, corrected orthogonalized update, control variate. Q12 [extended] Muon variance reduction ablation: apply the gradient-difference correction 'after orthogonalization' versus before (inside msign); correction term added to the orthogonalized update; 'orthogonalize first' then variance-reduce; MARS-M STORM SVRG placement of Newton-Schulz. Q16 [standard] Clip21 'Error Feedback for Gradient Clipping' clipping applied to the difference between the local gradient and the shift g_i. Q17 [extended] federated Muon OR LMO OR sign local update: orthogonalized local momentum plus a linearly added global correction / control variate term (correction outside the orthogonalization), 'local-global alignment' FedMuon, SCAFFOLD correction after the LMO. Q20 [standard] 'SVRG-Muon' OR 'SAGA-Muon' OR 'Muon-SVRG' OR 'Muon-SAGA' OR 'VR-Muon' OR 'Spectral-SVRG' finite-sum variance reduced orthogonalized gradient. Q22 [extended] federated OR distributed Muon: orthogonalization (msign / polar factor) computed once per round on the global / server gradient or momentum, clients take local steps along that fixed orthogonalized direction plus a linear local gradient-difference correction (SVRG / SCAFFOLD / Mime style), no Newton-Schulz on clients.

**2. tangent placement: step = gamma (sharp(m) + D sharp(m)[c]) (Frechet derivative of the spectral map used as an algorithm)**: not found.

*Closest work.* Nothing closer than the table: Beneventano, Abdelmoneum, Poggio, arXiv:2606.08388 (table; One more analysis-only use was opened: 'MuonEq: Balancing Before Orthogonalization with Lightweight Equilibration', Chang, Shi, Zhang, Li, Zhang, Lu, Liu, Yuan, 2026, arXiv:2603.28254 (abstract + Section 3.2, Proposition 3.2, Corollary 3.3 and Algorithms 1-2 read through the fetch tool, first 100k characters): first-order expansions of whitening maps, with the correction defined by a Sylvester-type equation, used to justify row normalization; the algorithm itself equilibrates then runs Newton-Schulz. No optimizer applying D polar(m) or D sharp(m) to a sampled correction, a gradient difference or a gradient noise was found.

*What differs.* Linearizations of the polar map were found only in proofs and noise analyses (Beneventano et al., Xie, MuonEq, and the noise studies returned by Q11, e.g. arXiv:2606.30509, not opened). The papers that reduce the cost of the factorization (CacheMuon, tiled / hierarchical Newton-Schulz arXiv:2606.27216, randomized inexact polar arXiv:2605.06884, Turbo-Muon arXiv:2512.04632; the last three not opened, described from search snippets) precondition, tile or sketch; none updates the polar factor by a first-order perturbation formula. The control-variate literature returned by Q14 uses Taylor expansions in the iterate or in a latent variable, not in the step map. Own remark (a derivation, not from a paper, not checked numerically since no Python was allowed): for full-rank m, D sharp(m)[c] = <polar(m), c> polar(m) + ||m||_* D polar(m)[c], so at theta = 1 the tangent step equals gamma (<omega, O_m> O_m + ||m||_* D polar(m)[c]) with O_m = polar(m): the length term of the radial placement plus a first-order rotation of the direction. The prior art of item 3 (GLyDER) therefore also covers the length part of the tangent step; the rotation term, the one with gain ||m||_* / s_min (F7), is the part with no published counterpart. In the l_infinity analogue (sign descent) the rotation term vanishes almost everywhere and tangent = radial without its Euclidean term.

*Queries.* ASCII transcription, quoted phrases shown with single quotes. Q4 [extended] Muon optimizer 'Frechet derivative' of the polar factor OR 'matrix sign' used in the update rule: linearized orthogonalization, first-order Taylor expansion of msign around momentum applied to the fresh stochastic gradient, tangent / perturbative update. Q5 [extended] Muon cheaper orthogonalization: update polar factor between iterations by first-order perturbation theory (derivative of polar decomposition / SVD divided differences) instead of recomputing Newton-Schulz; 'perturbation' 'polar factor' momentum change low-rank update optimizer. Q14 [extended] variance reduction for a nonlinear update map (normalization, sign, orthogonalization, LMO): first-order Taylor / delta-method expansion around the snapshot or momentum, T(m) + Jacobian (stochastic gradient difference), 'linearized' control variate, second-order bias, stochastic optimization algorithm. Shared: Q11 (bias of msign, debiased / unbiased estimator of the polar factor) and Q13 (l_infinity analogue, see item 3).

**3. radial placement: step = gamma_c omega + gamma <omega, O> O with O = polar(m) (predictable direction, unbiased scalar length)**: close work published.

*Closest work.* GLyDER: Gilad Yehudai, Alon Cohen, Amit Daniely, Yoel Drori, Tomer Koren, Mariano Schain, 'Locally Optimal Descent for Dynamic Stepsize Scheduling', 2023, arXiv:2311.13877 (PDF opened, text extracted with pdftotext and read directly: Theorem 2.1, Corollary 2.2, Eq. (2), Theorems 2.3-2.5, Section 2.2, Algorithms 1 and 3). Muon-side neighbours, further away: AdaGO, 'AdaGrad Meets Muon: Adaptive Stepsizes for Orthogonal Updates', Minxin Zhang, Yuxuan Liu, Hayden Schaeffer, 2025, arXiv:2509.02981 (HTML opened, Algorithm 2 restated by the fetch tool); NAMO, 'Adam Improves Muon: Adaptive Moment Estimation with Orthogonalized Momentum', same authors, 2026, arXiv:2602.17080 (HTML opened, Algorithms 1-2 restated); OptMuon, arXiv:2606.08783 (already cited in the report as a control; Section 4 re-read in the local text: the scalar coefficient is lagged, hence F_{t-1}-measurable); IAN-SGD, 'Adaptive Gradient Normalization and Independent Sampling for (Stochastic) Generalized-Smooth Optimization', Yang, Tripp, Sun, Zou, Zhou, TMLR 2025, arXiv:2410.14054 (abstract + Eq. (14) read through the fetch tool).

*What differs.* The table has no entry for this row. GLyDER shares the main idea: a step along a direction d with the length <grad f, d> / (L ||d||^2) given by the descent lemma (Corollary 2.2), the pairing being estimated without bias from fresh stochastic gradients drawn independently of the direction ((1/k) sum_i <h_i, g_t>, end of Section 2.1), and the best step in expectation (1/L) ||grad f||^2 / (||grad f||^2 + sigma^2) (Theorem 2.4), which is the Euclidean form of the optimal gamma = a^2 / (L_op (a^2 + v)) of F9. Differences that matter: Euclidean geometry; the direction is the stochastic gradient itself (or the direction of any base optimizer in its Algorithm 3), not the polar factor of a finite-sum memory mean; the pairing estimator is mentioned and then set aside for an estimator of the squared gradient norm, because the pairing cannot be bounded away from zero; no variance-reduced estimator, so the variance of the length does not vanish at the optimum; no added Euclidean variance-reduced step; nonconvex stationarity rates, no linear rate. On the Muon side: AdaGO takes the direction Orth(M_t) and the length max(eps, eta min(||G_t||, gamma) / v_t), a clipped norm of the fresh gradient over an AdaGrad-norm accumulator (a norm, not a signed pairing, and M_t contains the fresh gradient, so the direction is not predictable); NAMO scales Orth(M_t) by ||M_t||_F / sqrt(EMA of ||G_t||_F^2); OptMuon puts a predictable scalar on the sampled direction polar(M_t) (the mirror image: predictable length, sampled direction); IAN-SGD draws the normalization factor from an independent sample so that the numerator stays unbiased up to a scalar (decorrelation of the nonlinear ingredient from the sample, without a fixed direction). No paper found has a predictable polar direction with an unbiased signed length, with or without variance reduction (Q6, Q9, Q13, Q18, Q19 all negative on that exact scheme). Own remark (derivation): the radial step is linear in the estimator, vec(S) = (gamma_c I + gamma vec(O_m) vec(O_m)^T) vec(omega), i.e. SAGA with a predictable identity-plus-rank-one metric; it belongs to the same class as the polar-metric row (PROMISE, already cited there).

*Queries.* ASCII transcription, quoted phrases shown with single quotes. Q6 [extended] Muon variant step size = inner product of the fresh stochastic gradient with the orthogonalized momentum direction, '<G_t, msign(M' OR 'tr(G_t^T' OR '<G_t, O_t>', direction from momentum polar factor, unbiased step length, dual norm estimate. Q9 [extended] normalized OR sign OR LMO stochastic method where the direction is computed from past information (momentum, independent of the current sample) and the step length is the fresh stochastic directional derivative <g_t, d_t>, conditionally unbiased; 'independently normalized' SGD; unbiased normalized gradient. Q13 [extended] scaled sign descent with dual-norm (l1) step length estimated without bias: direction sign(m) from momentum or snapshot gradient, length <g, sign(m)> from a fresh stochastic gradient; steepest descent in l_infinity norm stochastic 'dual norm' estimate predictable direction variance reduction. Q18 [extended] Muon OR 'spectral descent' OR 'orthogonalized momentum' with Polyak step OR line search OR Frank-Wolfe 'short step': step size along the polar factor direction set by the directional derivative <grad f, UV^T> estimated from an independent minibatch, stochastic steepest descent non-Euclidean adaptive step. Q19 [extended] sample splitting OR 'independent minibatch' OR 'two independent batches' for LMO / sign / orthogonalized (Muon) step: one batch (or the momentum) gives the direction, another gives an unbiased scale <g', lmo(g)>; removes bias of normalized / sign / spectral stochastic steepest descent.

**4. mean-scaled length (direction polar(omega), length gamma <m, polar(omega)>_+) and clipped length min(rho, gamma ||omega||_*) with a SAGA estimator**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Clipped half: SPIDER-SFO, Cong Fang, Chris Junchi Li, Zhouchen Lin, Tong Zhang, 'SPIDER: Near-Optimal Non-Convex Optimization via Stochastic Path Integrated Differential Estimator', 2018, arXiv:1807.01695 (abstract opened; Algorithm 1 read through ar5iv and restated by the fetch tool; its step rule is also quoted in arXiv:2303.00883, which I read directly); (L0,L1)-Spider, Reisizadeh, Li, Das, Jadbabaie, 'Variance-reduced Clipping for Non-convex Optimization', 2023, arXiv:2303.00883 (PDF opened, text extracted and read: Algorithm 2 and Section 3.1); EPISODE, Michael Crawshaw, Yajie Bao, Mingrui Liu, 2023, arXiv:2302.07155 (ar5iv opened, algorithm lines 3-13 quoted by the fetch tool; the ICLR 2023 venue comes from the search result, not from the page); CGTVR, Leilei Mei, Junyu Zhang, 'Clipped Stochastic Gradient Tracking For Locally Smooth Functions', 2026, arXiv:2605.17027 (abstract + HTML, algorithms restated). Mean-scaled half: Grams, Yang Cao, Xiaoyu Li, Zhao Song, 'Grams: Gradient Descent with Adaptive Momentum Scaling', 2024, arXiv:2412.17107 (PDF opened, text read directly: Definition 4.1 and Algorithm 1, line 10); GLyDER Eq. (2), arXiv:2311.13877 (read directly, see item 3); AdaGO arXiv:2509.02981, NAMO arXiv:2602.17080 (see item 3); AdaSGDA-VR in 'Orthogonal Updates for the Win: Towards Accelerated Adaptive Minimax Optimization', Zhai, Wang, Yan, Ding, Zhang, 8 Nov 2026, arXiv:2611.11387 (abstract + Section 3.2 through the fetch tool; its Algorithm 2 is in Appendix D, not read).

*What differs.* Clipped half. The rule 'step = min(rho, gamma ||v||) v / ||v||' on a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator is published in the Euclidean norm since SPIDER-SFO: Option II takes eta_k = min(eps / (L n0 ||v_k||), 1 / (2 L n0)) on the recursive estimator v_k = grad f_S2(x_k) - grad f_S2(x_{k-1}) + v_{k-1} with a full gradient every q iterations (Option I is the fixed-length normalized step); (L0,L1)-Spider uses eta_k = min(1 / (2 L0), eps / (L0 ||v_k||), eps / (L1 ||v_k||^2)); CGTVR applies the same trust-region rule (v / beta if ||v|| <= beta delta, else delta v / ||v||) to a tracked SARAH-type direction; EPISODE uses an SVRG / SCAFFOLD-type corrected gradient g = grad F_i(x; xi) - G_r^i + G_r and takes eta g if ||G_r|| <= gamma / eta, gamma g / ||g|| otherwise: the clipping regime is decided by the norm of the anchor gradient, known before the local samples are drawn. What differs here: spectral norm (nuclear-norm length, polar direction) instead of the Euclidean norm; a SAGA table instead of a recursive or resampled-anchor estimator; a strongly convex finite sum at a constant step with the error going to rounding, against nonconvex stationarity rates. The table's reference (generalized gradient norm clipping, arXiv:2506.01913) remains the only one in a non-Euclidean norm, with momentum instead of a finite-sum estimator; no paper combining the two was found (Q21). These works are closer than the table on the estimator axis only: both should be cited. Mean-scaled half. Decoupling direction and length is published in other geometries: Grams takes the direction sign(g_t) from the current gradient and the magnitude |m_hat_t / (sqrt(v_hat_t) + eps)| from the momentum (elementwise, Adam family, no variance reduction); GLyDER's Eq. (2) is the Euclidean form of the same pairing, a step along the stochastic gradient g_t of length <grad f, g_t> / (L ||g_t||^2), the gradient in the pairing being estimated from other samples; AdaGO, NAMO and AdaSGDA-VR take an orthogonalized direction and a scalar from another quantity (clipped norm of the fresh gradient over an accumulator; ratio of momentum norm to gradient RMS), AdaSGDA-VR with a STORM-type variance-reduced momentum (its correction sits inside the orthogonalized matrix according to Section 3.2). For this half AdaGO and AdaSGDA-VR are closer than the table's reference, which takes direction and length from the same momentum estimator. What differs here: the length is the pairing <m, polar(omega)>_+ of the polar direction of the estimator with the predictable part of a SAGA estimator (a low-noise, biased proxy of <grad F, polar(omega)>, with a positive part that cancels the step when the two disagree), in the spectral norm. No Muon variant with this pairing was found (Q15).

*Queries.* ASCII transcription, quoted phrases shown with single quotes. Q7 [standard] gradient clipping with SAGA OR SVRG variance-reduced estimator finite-sum 'clipped SAGA' OR 'clipped SVRG' OR 'normalized SVRG' OR 'normalized SAGA' convergence. Q8 [extended] optimizer decouples update direction and magnitude: direction from the current noisy gradient estimate, step length from the momentum / averaged gradient (inner product of momentum with the direction), 'Grams' OR 'decoupled direction and magnitude' OR 'direction from gradient, magnitude from momentum' spectral OR Muon OR normalized. Q10 [standard] EPISODE 'Episodic Gradient Clipping with Periodic Resampled Corrections' federated clipping decided from the global gradient norm at the start of the round, local SCAFFOLD-type correction (a candidate recalled from memory, searched then opened). Q15 [extended] 'cautious' Muon OR spectral: step length from alignment <momentum, msign(gradient)> positive part, nuclear norm of momentum as scale with direction from the current gradient polar factor; Muon variant mixing momentum magnitude and gradient direction. Q21 [extended] variance reduction (STORM OR SPIDER OR SVRG OR SAGA) with 'generalized gradient norm clipping' OR 'clipped Scion' OR clipped LMO step non-Euclidean spectral norm (L0,L1)-smooth; clipped spectral descent with variance-reduced estimator; nuclear-norm scaled step min(rho, gamma ||d||_*).
