*Limits stated by this agent.*

- Budget and modes: 22 WebSearch calls were made, the cap; all returned results. 18 were in extended mode and 4 in standard mode, more extended calls than the 'about half' the task asked for. No search engine or search endpoint was queried through WebFetch or any other tool.
- What a negative result means: each query returns about ten results and a summary; 'not found' means not in those results for these wordings. The search tool is US-only and returned almost only arXiv pages; theses, blog posts, public code and non-English sources were not reached. Dedicated queries: split 6 plus 3 shared, tangent 3 plus 2 shared, radial 5, mean-scaled / clipped 5.
- Every other paper was read through WebFetch, which passes the page through a small model: algorithm boxes were restated, not copied, and only the first 100000 characters of long HTML pages were read (appendices not reached for MAGD, MiMuon, IAN-SGD, EPISODE, SUDA-Muon, NAMO, Denoise First, Parshakova et al., CGTVR, Sun-Liu-Yuan, AdaSGDA-VR, SPIDER). Formulas quoted from those pages should be re-read in the PDF before being printed in the report. Specific weak points: for AdaSGDA-VR the placement of the correction inside the orthogonalization is inferred from Section 3.2, the algorithm box was not read; for FedMuon (Liu et al.) the fetch tool reported inconsistencies between Algorithm 1 ('(-alpha)') and Eq. (3) ('(1 - alpha)') and in the round index of Delta_G; for SUDA-Muon the two orderings are known from the abstract and contribution list only.
- The verdict 'not_found' for the split is a judgement call. FedMuon (Liu et al., arXiv:2510.27403) does add a drift-correcting term linearly outside the polar factor, which no work of the table does in a correction role; I rated it at the same distance as PMRC and not closer, because the map is applied there to the sampled part and the predictable part is the linear one, the reverse of the split. A reader who weighs 'a correction outside the polar map' more than 'which part is mapped' would call it published_close.
- A sentence of findings.md Section 3 needs to be qualified: 'Not found: ... any study of where the nonlinear map should sit relative to the variance-reduction correction'. Placement studies exist for neighbouring mechanisms: momentum averaging (arXiv:2606.03899; PolarGrad), gradient tracking and averaging in decentralized Muon (arXiv:2604.23980), drift correction inside the LMO (arXiv:2509.26337) versus outside (arXiv:2510.27403) in federated Muon, error feedback around the polar step (arXiv:2605.08980), clipping on differences (arXiv:2305.18929) versus on the whole estimator (arXiv:1807.01695). What was not found is such a study for a finite-sum SAGA / SVRG correction, and any method with the map on the predictable part.
- F9 (radial descent inequality) and the mean-scaled pairing have a Euclidean precedent in GLyDER (arXiv:2311.13877); GLyDER cites earlier work on locally optimal learning rates (Schaul et al. 2013), which I did not open.
- The three 'own remarks' in the difference fields (split with a linear map = SVAG / B-SAGA; tangent step = radial length term + ||m||_* D polar(m)[c]; radial step = SAGA with an identity-plus-rank-one predictable metric) are my derivations from the definitions in muon/lab/vrmuon.py. They come from no paper and were not checked numerically (no Python was allowed in this task).
- Returned by the searches and NOT opened (titles from the result lists only): arXiv:2605.31505 (sketched landing method, SVRG-like structure under orthogonality constraints), arXiv:2606.01720 (stability of orthogonalized matrix momentum with client sampling), arXiv:2608.12710 (FedCoMuon), arXiv:2606.00371 ('How Much Orthogonalization Does Muon Need?'), arXiv:2502.11682 (Clip21-SGD2M), arXiv:2606.30509, arXiv:2606.27216, arXiv:2605.06884, arXiv:2512.04632, arXiv:2510.19933, arXiv:2609.21102, arXiv:2609.17759, arXiv:2609.34915, arXiv:2605.09552, arXiv:2507.11005 (AdaMuon), arXiv:2604.09967 (Muon2), arXiv:2605.10797 (Muown), arXiv:2604.01472 (Newton-Muon), DeMuon (no identifier seen). Opened at abstract level only and judged off-topic for this group: arXiv:2303.16510 (Ablin et al., variance-reduced landing), arXiv:1710.07462 (Gower, Le Roux, Bach), arXiv:2609.06073 (FedSubMuon).
- Not searched: the numerical-linear-algebra literature on low-rank updates of the polar decomposition or of the matrix sign function, and Riemannian optimization on the Stiefel manifold, where differentials of the polar retraction are standard; either could contain a precursor of the tangent step under another name.
- No Python, no git command, nothing else modified.

### Shaping the estimator or the map

**1. damped SAGA-Muon (nuclear-scaled polar step of m + theta c, theta < 1; theta = 1/n: polar factor of the SAG direction), and the claim of F4 / Section 5.2 that the joint polar step of the SAGA estimator at batch size 1 is pathwise a MISO / Finito iteration (dual-free SDCA for the relaxed table)**: not found.

*Closest work.* Nothing closer than the works of the row was found. In-row closest, already cited: Negiar et al., 'Stochastic Frank-Wolfe for Constrained Finite-Sum Minimization', 2020, arXiv:2002.11860 (not re-opened in this run; Lemma 2.1 gives the equivalence with a randomized coordinate mirror descent on the dual). Neighbours met during this search that findings.md cites elsewhere but not in this row: (a) MARS-M, Liu, Yuan, Gu, 2025, arXiv:2510.21800 (opened: saved text, Section 3 and Algorithm 2: 'MARS introduces a scaling parameter for the gradient-correction term', C_t = grad f(X_t, xi_t) + gamma_t (beta/(1-beta)) (grad f(X_t, xi_t) - grad f(X_{t-1}, xi_t)), M_t = beta M_{t-1} + (1-beta) Clip(C_t, 1), O_t = NewtonSchulz(M_t); gamma = 0.025 in the experiments): a scaled-down variance-reduction correction inside Muon's orthogonalized step, the momentum-based counterpart of theta < 1. (b) SSVR-FS, Jiang, Yang, Yang, Zhang, 2024, arXiv:2406.00489 (opened: HTML v3 through the fetch tool, Algorithm 2 / eq. (3)): x_{t+1} = x_t - eta Sign(v_t), v_t = grad f_i(x_t) + (1-beta)(v_{t-1} - grad f_i(x_{t-1})) - beta (grad f_i(x_tau) - grad f(x_tau)) with beta = O(1/m): the l_infinity LMO of an estimator in which the SVRG-corrected sample enters with a weight of order 1/m. (c) EF21-Muon, Gruntkowska, Gaponov, Tovmasyan, Richtarik, 2025, arXiv:2510.00643 (opened: saved text, Algorithm 1): X^{k+1} = LMO(G^k), G_j^{k+1} = G_j^k + C_j(M_j^{k+1} - G_j^k): spectral LMO of the mean of per-worker memories, a biased SAG-like aggregate with no innovation term. For the MISO / Finito claim: no statement was found that a polar or nuclear-scaled step of a SAGA estimator reduces pathwise to MISO, Finito or dual-free SDCA; the published analogue remains Lemma 2.1 of Lu-Freund on the LMO side.

*What differs.* No work found combines a SAGA / SAG table, a damping theta of the sampled correction and an unconstrained nuclear-scaled polar step. Negiar et al. and Lu-Freund: the SAG aggregate (theta = 1/n) is fed to the LMO of a constraint set or regularizer inside a Frank-Wolfe convex-combination step; no polar factor, no intermediate theta. MARS-M: the damped correction is a STORM difference of two minibatch gradients (no finite-sum memory, an error that does not vanish at the minimizer, fixed-length step, nonconvex sublinear rate). SSVR-FS: sign instead of polar factor, SVRG reference point plus STORM recursion instead of a table, fixed-length step, rate m^(1/4) d^(1/2) T^(-1/2). EF21-Muon: all workers participate and compress, fixed radius, nonconvex sublinear rates. Point to make in the report: the device 'scale down the variance-reduction correction before the polar map' is published for the STORM correction (gamma of MARS-M, a family the study already runs with g = 0.025), so the row should cite MARS-M, SSVR-FS and EF21-Muon as cross-references and state the difference as 'damped finite-sum memory estimator inside an unconstrained nuclear-scaled polar step'. The MISO reading of Section 5.2 can keep the wording 'not found by the literature check of 2026-11-12'.

*Queries.* #3 ext: SAGA OR SAG OR SVRG finite-sum variance reduction inside Muon orthogonalized polar factor step, biased or damped estimator, linear convergence strongly convex. #4 ext: Muon OR 'spectral descent' OR 'normalized' SAGA interpreted as MISO OR Finito OR 'majorization-minimization' incremental surrogate equivalence polar factor. #9 ext: 'stochastic average gradient' OR 'SAG' OR 'incremental aggregated gradient' combined with sign OR normalized OR 'steepest descent' OR 'linear minimization oracle' step, memory of stored gradients, biased estimator, unconstrained finite-sum. #10 ext: 'MISO' OR 'Finito' OR 'incremental majorization-minimization' with spectral norm OR Schatten OR non-Euclidean surrogates for matrix parameters, 'stochastic spectral descent' OR Muon. #12 ext: 'stochastic spectral descent' OR 'spectral descent' OR PolarGrad 'variance reduction' SVRG OR SAGA OR SAG finite-sum nuclear norm scaled polar step. #14 ext: Muon OR 'spectral LMO' OR orthogonalized federated 'partial participation' OR 'client sampling' stored client gradients table OR 'SAGA' OR 'SAG' OR 'control variates' memory aggregated then orthogonalized. #16 ext: 'SAGA' OR 'SVRG' with normalized gradient OR gradient clipping step, finite-sum, '(L0,L1)-smooth' OR 'generalized smoothness', nonlinear map applied to variance-reduced estimator, linear convergence strongly convex. #17 std: 'SAGA-Muon' OR 'SVRG-Muon' OR 'Muon-SVRG' OR 'Muon-SAGA' OR 'SAG-Muon' OR 'VR-Muon' OR 'SARAH-Muon' OR 'Muon-SARAH'. #21 ext: Muon weight decay as stochastic Frank-Wolfe on spectral norm ball with finite-sum gradient memory 'SAG' OR 'SAGA' OR 'stochastic average gradient', dual coordinate ascent OR SDCA OR MISO interpretation, linear convergence. None of the nine returned a SAGA / SAG / SVRG estimator inside a Muon or polar step other than the works already in findings.md.

**2. bounded-gain (soft) polar map s -> s / sqrt(s^2 + eps^2) with eps = c ||theta c||_F recomputed at every step from the sampled correction, applied to a variance-reduced estimator ('soft SAGA-Muon')**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Yujie Yang, 'PRISM: Structured Optimization via Anisotropic Spectral Shaping', 3 Feb 2026, arXiv:2602.03096 v1 (opened: TeX source read: Algorithm 1, the sections 'Innovation-Augmented Polar Decomposition' and spectral gain, the Tikhonov ablation). Algorithm 1: M_t = beta M_{t-1} + (1-beta) G_t; D_t = G_t - M_t ('instantaneous innovation'); O_t = top block of polar([M_t; gamma D_t]) = M_t (M_t^T M_t + gamma^2 D_t^T D_t)^(-1/2); theta_t = theta_{t-1} - eta O_t. Stated gain along the eigenvector v_k: rho_k = ||M_t v_k|| / sqrt(||M_t v_k||^2 + gamma^2 ||D_t v_k||^2) = (1 + 1/SNR_k^2)^(-1/2), with ||D_t v_k||^2 called the noise energy. The same paper has the ablation 'Muon with Tikhonov damping', update proportional to M (M^T M + lambda I)^(-1/2) with a constant lambda (final loss 3.325 against 3.285 for Muon and 3.266 for PRISM with gamma = 2, single runs). Other published uses of the same scalar map, all opened: Shulgin, Laing, Orvieto, Richtarik, arXiv:2610.30280 (TeX source; details in other_relevant_papers); Kim, Nichani, Wu, Bietti, Lee, arXiv:2603.26554 (TeX source); Mustafi, Mukherjee, Sriperumbudur, arXiv:2605.23871 (TeX source); Kai Jia, blog post 'Softening Muon for Better Optimization in Certain Applications', 2026-07-18, https://jiakai.xyz/2026/07/18/softening-muon.html (page text read); Jiang, Mhammedi, Mohri, Mokhtari, arXiv:2602.08232 (TeX source, Leon update); Jiang, Semenov, Stich, arXiv:2603.14315 (TeX source, soft spectral clipping).

*What differs.* PRISM is the idea of the row with another estimator and a matrix-valued offset. Since M_t = M_{t-1} + (1-beta)(G_t - M_{t-1}) and D_t = beta (G_t - M_{t-1}) (my rewriting, not in the paper), PRISM applies a bounded-gain polar map to 'prediction + (1-beta) x innovation' with an offset built from that same innovation and recomputed at every step. What differs: (1) the offset is the matrix gamma^2 D^T D (anisotropic; rank one at batch size 1, so it damps only the feature direction of the sampled correction), against the isotropic eps^2 I with eps = c ||theta c||_F of the lab, which keeps every singular direction below the innovation level from being inflated to one; (2) the estimator is an EMA of minibatch gradients, whose innovation does not vanish at the minimizer, against a SAGA / L-SVRG estimator whose innovation vanishes there; (3) fixed-length step eta O_t against the nuclear-scaled length; (4) LLM pretraining, single runs, no convergence statement, against a strongly convex finite sum with exact expectations. The scalar map with other offsets is published several times: fixed eps (Shulgin et al., Appendix F, 'Tikhonov damping'; Mustafi et al., Orth_eps; the PRISM ablation); lambda prescribed by theory as max{(log d)^(2 alpha + 2) / d^alpha, (log d)^2 / B}, hence depending on the batch size B, on minibatch gradients (Kim et al.; the paper calls lambda a 'scale of resolution' and does not call the 1/B term a noise level: that reading is mine); eps_t = K ||M_t||_F / sqrt(r), K = 0.5, recomputed at every step from the mapped momentum itself (Kai Jia: scale-adaptive, not noise-adaptive); matrix offset (1/eta^2)(G^2 beta^(-2) I + sum_s beta^(2(t-s)) G_s G_s^T) made of the discounted second moment of the past stochastic gradients (Leon, Jiang-Mhammedi-Mohri-Mokhtari). Not found: an isotropic offset proportional to the norm of the sampled variance-reduction correction, and any soft polar map applied to a SAGA / SVRG estimator. Suggested row: closest work PRISM (innovation-augmented polar decomposition), then the fixed-eps and scale-adaptive soft polar maps; difference: scalar offset tied to the Frobenius norm of the finite-sum correction, finite-sum estimator, nuclear-scaled length. The row must drop 'a workshop paper we could not open'.

*Queries.* #1 std: 'A Quadratic Lens on Muon' Orthogonalization, Invariance, and Implicit Preconditioning Shulgin Laing Orvieto Richtarik. #2 ext: Shulgin Laing Orvieto Richtarik 'Quadratic Lens on Muon' arXiv PolarGD exact line search implicit preconditioner whitens iterate covariance. #5 ext: Muon noise-adaptive orthogonalization: soft polar factor with damping epsilon set by gradient noise level, signal-to-noise shrinkage of singular values, Wiener filter. #6 std: 'Enhancing LLM training via spectral clipping' Jiang Semenov Stich arXiv. #7 std: PRISM 'Anisotropic Spectral Shaping' innovation-augmented polar decomposition Muon optimizer arXiv (a candidate recalled from memory, then searched and opened). #8 ext: Muon variant orthogonalization with damping from gradient variance: polar of augmented matrix [momentum, innovation], suppress high-variance singular directions, AdaBelief-style spectral optimizer. #13 ext: variance-reduced Muon (MARS-M OR STORM OR SVRG OR SAGA) with regularized OR 'soft' OR damped OR Tikhonov orthogonalization, damping epsilon scaled by the variance-reduction correction or gradient difference norm. #18 ext: Muon orthogonalization 'singular value' shrinkage OR 'soft-thresholding' OR truncation at the gradient noise level, noise-aware threshold from minibatch variance or gradient difference, 'below the noise' directions not amplified. #19 ext: orthogonalized optimizer per-direction signal-to-noise gain, damping matrix built from 'gradient minus momentum' OR 'gradient difference' OR innovation, (M^T M + gamma^2 D^T D)^{-1/2}, successor OR extension of PRISM, variance-aware Muon 2026. #20 ext: 'Soft Muon' OR 'SoftMuon' OR 'regularized Muon' OR 'damped Muon' OR 'smoothed Muon' optimizer regularized polar map epsilon chosen adaptively from gradient noise or variance estimate. Queries #13, #19 and #20 returned no soft polar map combined with a variance-reduced estimator and no successor of PRISM.

**3. relaxed SAGA table inside a polar step (the table entry of the sampled index is moved by a fraction delta towards the fresh gradient, the memory of dual-free SDCA, and the estimator is fed to the polar step)**: not found.

*Closest work.* Nothing closer than the works of the row was found. In-row closest, already cited: Lu and Freund, 2018, arXiv:1807.07680 (opened: Algorithm 1 read in the saved text: 'Update s value: s_j <- (1 - eta_i) s_j + eta_i x_j^T lambda~' for the sampled index j, then the substitute gradient d <- d + (1/n)(l_j'(s_j new) - l_j'(s_j old)) x_j is the matrix given to the generalized LMO; Lemma 2.1: equivalence with randomized coordinate mirror descent on the dual) and Shalev-Shwartz, 'SDCA without Duality', 2015, arXiv:1502.06177 (not re-opened; Neighbours cited elsewhere in findings.md: DIANA, Mishchenko, Gorbunov, Takac, Richtarik, 2019, arXiv:1901.09269 (opened: ar5iv HTML through the fetch tool, algorithm box): h_i <- h_i + alpha Q(g_i - h_i) and estimator g^ = h + mean of the quantized differences, i.e. an unbiased estimator on a relaxed memory, followed by a Euclidean prox step; EF21-Muon, arXiv:2510.00643 (opened: saved text, Algorithm 1): per-worker memories moved by compressed differences, spectral LMO of their mean. New but only loosely related: EF-BV, Condat, Yi, Richtarik, NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2205.04180 (abstract only): one framework 'recovering DIANA and EF21 as particular cases'.

*What differs.* Lu-Freund relax the stored predictions and give the biased SAG-type aggregate (no innovation term) to the LMO of a regularizer or constraint inside a Frank-Wolfe step; the lab gives the unbiased estimator m + c built on a relaxed table to an unconstrained nuclear-scaled polar step. DIANA has the estimator structure of the relaxed-table SAGA estimator (memory mean plus innovation, memory moved by a fraction alpha) but compresses every worker instead of sampling one index, and takes a Euclidean step. EF21-Muon puts a relaxed (compressed-difference) memory under the spectral LMO but uses the memory mean alone, full participation and a fixed radius. No work was found that feeds an SDCA-type or DIANA-type relaxed-table estimator to a polar or spectral-LMO step, and none that relates such a step to dual-free SDCA. Suggested edit of the row: add DIANA (relaxed control variate with an unbiased estimator; it shows that the Euclidean relaxed-table estimator is not specific to dual-free SDCA) and EF21-Muon as cross-references.

*Queries.* #11 ext: 'dual-free SDCA' OR 'SDCA without duality' OR 'dual free' stochastic dual coordinate ascent non-Euclidean OR 'arbitrary norm' OR 'steepest descent' OR 'linear minimization oracle' OR 'spectral norm' OR normalized update. #15 ext: SAGA variant where the stored gradient of the sampled index is moved by a fraction toward the new gradient (relaxed OR partial OR averaged table update, 'control variate' step size) combined with normalized OR sign OR Frank-Wolfe OR LMO step. #22 ext: DIANA OR 'EF-BV' OR 'shifted compression' control variates with relaxed shift update h_i + alpha C(grad f_i - h_i) inside 'linear minimization oracle' OR Muon OR Gluon OR Scion non-Euclidean, distributed variance reduction then orthogonalization. Also relevant: #14 and #21 (listed under item 1). #11 returned only Euclidean dual-free SDCA papers (arXiv:1605.09066, arXiv:1701.07808, not opened); #15 returned SAGA variants with full replacement of the entry; #22 returned EF21-Muon and its follow-ups only.

*Other papers reported by this agent.*

- arXiv:2602.03096 - Yujie Yang, 'PRISM: Structured Optimization via Anisotropic Spectral Shaping' (3 Feb 2026). OPENED (TeX source). O_t = M_t (M_t^T M_t + gamma^2 D_t^T D_t)^(-1/2) with D_t = G_t - M_t the innovation of the current step; per-direction signal-to-noise gain; constant-lambda 'Tikhonov-damped Muon' as ablation. Closest published work of the bounded-gain row; not cited in findings.md.
- arXiv:2610.30280 - Egor Shulgin, Sam Laing, Antonio Orvieto, Peter Richtarik, 'A Quadratic Lens on Muon's Polar Update: Benefits, Pitfalls, and the Role of Newton-Schulz' (v1, 21 Oct 2026, 47 pages). OPENED (TeX source: Section 4.2, Appendices F and G). It is the long version of the workshop paper: the arXiv comment says 'A shorter version was presented at the ICML 2026 Workshop on High-dimensional Learning Dynamics', and the ICML listing https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/72378 (opened) gives the title 'A Quadratic Lens on Muon: Orthogonalization, Invariance, and Implicit Preconditioning', the four authors as believed, a poster of the workshop 'High Dimensional Learning Dynamics', and the OpenReview id TVTwgZp0qe (NOT readable: OpenReview answers with a browser challenge, HTTP 403, which was not circumvented). What it says. (i) Damped polar: Section 4.2 ('Near-Rank-Deficiency and Softened Polar Updates') defines Q_eps := (G G^T + eps I_d)^(-1/2) G, 'which maps each singular value to sigma_i / sqrt(sigma_i^2 + eps)' (eps not squared: it is eps^2 of the lab), next to the rank-truncated polar Q^(s) = U_{:,1:s} V_{:,1:s}^T; 'Up to the factor sqrt(eps), damped polar is the soft spectral clipping (I_d + G G^T / c^2)^(-1/2) G with c = sqrt(eps)', credited to Jiang, Semenov, Stich. Appendix F.2 calls it 'Tikhonov damping' and proves one property: ||(G G^T + eps I_d)^(-1/2)||_2 <= 1/sqrt(eps), with equality when rank(G) < d. No convergence theorem is stated for the damped map. (ii) Newton-Schulz, Appendix G ('Finite Newton-Schulz as Damped Polar'): a finite iteration keeps the singular vectors and applies a polynomial filter phi_q to the normalized singular values; phi_q(sigma) = l_q sigma + O(sigma^3) with l_q the product of the linear coefficients, so 'finite Newton-Schulz matches a damped polar filter with eps_q = l_q^(-2) in normalized singular-value units'; for Muon's coefficients l_q = 3.4445^q (l_5 about 485), 0 < phi_q(s) <= 1.2024 and phi_q(s)/s <= a^q; damping scale tau_q = 1/l_q = 0.29, 8.4e-2, 2.4e-2, 2.1e-3, 4.3e-6 for q = 1, 2, 3, 5, 10; proposition 'Finite Newton-Schulz has bounded singular-mode amplification': Z_q = psi_q(G G^T) G with ||psi_q(G G^T)||_2 <= L_q / nu, whereas the exact polar map applies the unbounded multiplier 1/sigma_i. Main text: 'finite Newton-Schulz acts like GD on singular values below the damping scale 1/l_q and like exact polar above it'; in a 90M-parameter transformer about a quarter of the attention singular values lie below 1/l_5. The words 'bounded spectral filter' are those of the workshop abstract shown by the ICML listing; the arXiv version says 'scalar filter' and 'bounded amplification'. (iii) Noise: none. 'Momentum and stochasticity are left out on purpose, so that the effect of the update direction can be isolated'; eps is a fixed constant and eps_q depends only on the number of Newton-Schulz steps: no offset depends on noise, on a batch or on a variance-reduction correction. Use in the report: bounded-gain row (in place of 'a workshop paper we could not open'), F2 / Section 5.1, and Section 5.7 for 'bounded gain 3.4445^5' of ns5 (their proposition on Muon's Newton-Schulz filter).
- arXiv:2603.26554 - Juno Kim, Eshaan Nichani, Denny Wu, Alberto Bietti, Jason D. Lee, 'Sharp Capacity Scaling of Spectral Optimizers in Learning Associative Memory' (27 Mar 2026, v2 28 Apr 2026). OPENED (TeX source). 'Stabilized approximation to Muon' h_lambda(z) = z / sqrt(z^2 + lambda^2), W_{t+1} = W_t + eta_t G_t (G_t^T G_t + lambda_t^2 I_d)^(-1/2) on minibatch gradients; theorem 'one-step recovery of Muon': lambda of order max{(log d)^(2 alpha + 2) / d^alpha, (log d)^2 / B}; multi-step theorem: lambda_t = Theta~(d_{t+1}^(-alpha) sqrt(d)); the experiments use lambda = 0. The only soft polar map found whose offset is tied to the batch size.
- arXiv:2605.23871 - Aratrika Mustafi, Soumya Mukherjee, Bharath K. Sriperumbudur, 'Move on Muon: A Hamiltonian probability gradient flow perspective of Muon optimizer' (22 May 2026). OPENED (TeX source). Orth_eps(P) = U diag(sigma_i / sqrt(sigma_i^2 + eps^2)) V^T as the gradient of a smoothed nuclear norm, 1/eps-Lipschitz, fixed eps, deterministic gradients: exactly the lab's soft map with an absolute eps.
- Blog post - Kai Jia, 'Softening Muon for Better Optimization in Certain Applications', 2026-07-18, https://jiakai.xyz/2026/07/18/softening-muon.html. OPENED (page text). Q_t = M_t (M_t^T M_t + eps_t^2 I)^(-1/2) with eps_t = K ||M_t||_F / sqrt(r), K = 0.5, recomputed at every step from the momentum being mapped; its section 'What is and is not new' lists Mustafi et al., Kim et al., Jiang-Mhammedi-Mohri-Mokhtari and SPECTRA (not PRISM).
- arXiv:2602.08232 - Ruichen Jiang, Zakaria Mhammedi, Mehryar Mohri, Aryan Mokhtari, 'Adaptive Matrix Online Learning through Smoothing with Guarantees for Nonsmooth Nonconvex Optimization' (9 Feb 2026). OPENED (TeX source). Leon: X_{t+1} = -D (G^_t G^_t^T + (1/eta^2)(G^2 beta^(-2) I + M^_t))^(-1/2) G^_t with M^_t = sum_s beta^(2(t-s)) G_s G_s^T, computed as the leading block of polar([G^_t, L^_t / eta]): a damped polar map of the gradient EMA whose matrix offset is the discounted second moment of the stochastic gradients; Muon is identified as follow-the-leader under a spectral constraint.
- arXiv:2603.14315 - Xiaowen Jiang, Andrei Semenov, Sebastian U. Stich, 'Enhancing LLM Training via Spectral Clipping' (ICML 2026, PMLR 306). OPENED (TeX source). Soft spectral clipping H_c(X) = (I + X X^T / c^2)^(-1/2) X, h_c(x) = x / sqrt(1 + x^2 / c^2), with a constant c (rescaled as c eta / eta_k during warm-up), used as a wrapper on the update of base optimizers, among them the variance-reduced MARS ('Spectra-Mars' in the appendix): a soft spectral map composed with a variance-reduced optimizer, at a fixed scale.

NAMO: Theta_t = Theta_{t-1} - eta alpha_t Orth(M_t), alpha_t = ||M^_t||_F / (sqrt(v^_t) + eps_t), v_t the EMA of ||G_t||_F^2: a scalar signal-to-noise factor on the orthogonalized momentum (NAMO-D: one factor per column, clamped). Scalar counterpart of a noise-dependent bounded gain; also a neighbour of the 'mean-scaled length' row.
- arXiv:2609.32861 - Xie, 'Muon Under Gradient Noise and the Limits of Orthogonalization Near Optima' (already cited in the tangent row; saved text read). It also belongs in the bounded-gain row: 'Gaussian noise smooths the polar map'; Proposition 3.1: near a noise-dominated optimum the expected polar update is the linear response (c / sigma) Y, a gain inversely proportional to the noise scale. An offset proportional to the innovation makes deterministic what this paper computes in expectation.
- arXiv:2305.13187 - Chzhen and Schechtman, 'SignSVRG' (already cited in Section 3; saved text read, Algorithm 1). It also belongs in the bounded-gain row: the step is sign(v_t + G_t U_t) with a uniform dither of amplitude G_t = L ||x_t - x~|| + ||grad f(x~)|| recomputed at every step; in expectation over the dither the direction is v_t / G_t, a bounded-gain map whose scale is an upper bound of the SVRG correction plus the reference gradient. It is the l_infinity, randomized precedent of a smoothing scale tied to the size of the variance-reduction correction.

Theorem 2: momentum before the polar map ('Pre-polar') dominates orthogonalize-then-average ('Post-polar') and no momentum in expected signal alignment under a signal-plus-perturbation model. A published study of where the polar map should sit relative to the noise filter (for momentum, not for a variance-reduction correction): it qualifies the sentence of Section 3 'Not found: any study of where the nonlinear map should sit ...'.

Relevant to Section 5.7 (rounding sensitivity) and F7.

*Limits stated by this agent.*

- Budget: 22 WebSearch calls were made (the cap): 4 in standard mode (#1, #6, #7, #17) and 18 in extended mode, more than the suggested half, because almost every query was a niche combination of keywords. The search tool is US-only and returns about ten links per query.
- 'not_found' means: not found by these 22 queries and by the documents opened from them, on 2026-11-12. No citation-graph search was possible (papers citing PRISM, B-SAGA / SVAG, Lu-Freund or dual-free SDCA were not listed), and no code-hosting search was made (only one README was opened, RhizomeResearch/rollfast, which implements PRISM without any variance-reduced estimator). Public code that puts a SAGA table under a Muon step could exist without a paper.
- OpenReview could not be read (browser challenge, HTTP 403, on the forum, the PDF and the API; not circumvented). The workshop version of Shulgin et al. was therefore not read; the arXiv long version (v1) was, and the two may differ. The phrase 'bounded spectral filter' comes from the workshop abstract as displayed by the ICML listing and by the search summary, not from the arXiv text.
- Depth of reading. Read only through the fetch tool, which summarizes with a small model: 2406.00489 (HTML v3: the SSVR-FS recursion must be re-read before it is quoted), 2606.03899 (first 100,000 characters), 1901.09269 (ar5iv). Abstract only: 2606.01720, 2608.26288, 1705.07774, 2307.12615, 2303.00883, 2511.11466, 2610.10965, 2602.04669, 2205.04180, 2610.31728, 2602.13498. Seen in search results and NOT opened: arXiv:2606.15702 (Schattor), arXiv:2609.39595, arXiv:2611.13137, arXiv:2605.16311, arXiv:2607.29674 beyond its abstract, arXiv:1605.09066, arXiv:1701.07808.
- Interpretations that are mine and not in the sources: the rewriting of PRISM as 'prediction + (1-beta) x innovation with an offset built from the same innovation'; the reading of the (log d)^2 / B term of Kim et al. as a noise level (the paper speaks of a 'scale of resolution' and of items not observed in the minibatch); the statement that SignSVRG is, in expectation over its dither, a bounded-gain map with scale G_t (the paper states E[sign(g_t + G U)] = grad f(x_t) / G for a uniform dither U on [-1, 1]^d); the classification of MARS-M's gamma as the momentum-based counterpart of theta.
- PRISM is a single-author preprint (v1), with single runs and no convergence analysis. It is prior art for the idea of an innovation-dependent damping of the polar map, not for any guarantee, and not for a finite-sum estimator.
- Items 1 and 3 were searched with the vocabularies of finite-sum optimization, Frank-Wolfe, sign and normalized methods, federated control variates and compression (EF21, DIANA). The compression literature was probed by two queries only (#14, #22); it moves fast (several Muon / Gluon papers per month in 2026), so a DIANA-type shift under a spectral LMO may appear or may exist under a name these queries did not match.
- no Python and no git command was run; downloads were limited to single arXiv source archives and one blog page.

### Sampling, batches, recursive estimators, acceleration

**1. importance sampling inside the polar step (IS SAGA-Muon)**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Z. Allen-Zhu, 'Katyusha: The First Direct Acceleration of Stochastic Gradient Methods', JMLR 18 (2018) 1-51 (arXiv:1603.05953), Section 6 and Appendix E, Algorithm 4 'Katyusha2'. read: Section 5.1, Sections 6.1-6.2, the box of Algorithm 4, Lemma E.1, proof of Lemma E.2). Content: each f_i is L_i-smooth with respect to an ARBITRARY norm; the index is drawn with p_i = L_i/(n Lbar) (Algorithm 4, line 9); the SVRG correction carries the weight 1/(n p_i) (written in Section 5.1, 'this is standard, see for instance Prox-SVRG (Xiao and Zhang, 2014)', and in the proof of Lemma E.2; the extracted text of line 10 of the Algorithm 4 box does not show the weight); the estimator is fed to y_{k+1} = argmin_y (9L/2)||y - x_{k+1}||^2 + <estimator, y> + psi(y) (line 12), which for psi = 0 is the sharp (dual-map) step of that norm; linear rate in Theorem 6.1. No matrix norm appears in the paper (no occurrence of Schatten / spectral / nuclear / trace norm) and its experiments are Euclidean. Also opened, all Euclidean or with uniform sampling: Horvath-Richtarik arXiv:1809.04146 (PDF, abstract and introduction: importance sampling for SVRG / SAGA / SARAH, nonconvex); Gower-Richtarik-Bach arXiv:1805.02632 (abstract page: best-known rate of SAGA with importance sampling, resolves a conjecture of Schmidt et al. 2015); Qian-Qu-Richtarik 'SAGA with Arbitrary Sampling', PMLR 97:5190-5199 (abstract page) and 'L-SVRG and L-Katyusha with Arbitrary Sampling', arXiv:1906.01481 (abstract page); Schmidt et al. arXiv:1504.04406 (abstract page: non-uniform sampling for SAG, rate analysis of SAGA with non-uniform sampling); Xiao-Zhang arXiv:1403.4699 (abstract page only; the abstract does not mention the sampling, the non-uniform variant is attested by Katyusha Section 5.1); Nazykov et al. arXiv:2406.06788 (PDF: Algorithms 1-3 L-SVRG FW / SARAH FW / SAGA FW, batches drawn uniformly); arXiv:1607.08254, Hazan-Luo arXiv:1602.02101, Negiar et al. arXiv:2002.11860 (all uniform), FedMuon arXiv:2509.26337 (uniform client sampling), Qian et al. arXiv:2604.10689 (i.i.d. / uniform samples), Li-Tang-Zou arXiv:2602.11557v2 (random reshuffling).

*What differs.* The table row cites only Euclidean non-uniform sampling (Xiao-Zhang 2014, Schmidt et al. 2015) and states the difference as 'sampled index reweighted inside a nonlinear step'. Katyusha2, which the table cites for other rows but not for this one, is the general-norm template of exactly that ingredient: an index drawn with p_i proportional to L_i, a correction reweighted by 1/(n p_i), and the resulting variance-reduced estimator fed to the steepest-descent (sharp) step of a non-Euclidean norm. So the reweighted index inside a nonlinear step is published as a template (a = 1 only). What still differs in the study: (i) SAGA table (entry i refreshed with probability p_i, unweighted table update) instead of an SVRG snapshot; (ii) the mapped step is the whole method, not the y step of an accelerated three-sequence scheme whose z step is linear in the estimator (which is where Katyusha2 uses unbiasedness); (iii) spectral-norm instance and experiments: Katyusha2 is instantiated for no matrix norm and is not tested in a non-Euclidean setting; (iv) the exponent a = 1/2 and the bounded-gain map are not in Katyusha2. On the LMO / Frank-Wolfe, sign and Muon sides no importance sampling of the index was found at all: SagaFw / SVFW, SVRF / STORC, Negiar et al., the Frank-Wolfe zoo of Nazykov et al., Li-Tang-Zou, FedMuon and Compressed Gluon all sample uniformly (checked in the texts), and the queries on signSGD / normalized SGD / Muon / Gluon with importance or arbitrary sampling returned nothing. Remark of mine, not from a source: for losses l(W x_i) the per-sample gradient is rank one, so the smoothness constant of the loss part with respect to the spectral norm equals the Euclidean one (c_l ||x_i||^2); the study's a = 1 sampling is therefore the distribution Katyusha2 prescribes for the spectral norm.

*Queries.* S3 (extended): "importance sampling" OR "non-uniform sampling" OR "arbitrary sampling" Muon OR Gluon OR "linear minimization oracle" OR "spectral descent" variance reduction finite-sum SAGA OR SVRG. S10 (standard): stochastic "Frank-Wolfe" "non-uniform sampling" OR "importance sampling" variance reduced finite-sum SAGA OR SVRG "linear minimization oracle" Lipschitz constants sampling probabilities. S12 (standard): "signSGD" OR "normalized SGD" OR "sign descent" "importance sampling" OR "non-uniform sampling" variance reduction finite-sum SVRG sign reweighted. **2. minibatch SAGA-Muon with a batch of the order of the rank**: close work published.

*Closest work.* (a) On the question 'batch size versus rank inside the polar step': E. Paquette, N. Marshall, L. Benigni, G. Wang, A. Agarwala, C. Paquette, 'Phases of Muon: When Muon Eclipses SignSGD', arXiv:2605.09552v2 (27 Oct 2026; the PDF header says NeurIPS 2026). OPENED (PDF text; read: abstract, Sections 1.1-1.2 with the algorithm, list of contributions, Sections 3.1-3.2, captions of Figures 4-6, related-work appendix). Content: on an N x N matrix least-squares model with rank-one per-sample gradients, 'the minibatch gradient is almost surely a rank-min{N_in, N_out, B} matrix'; Muon 'acts as a square-root preconditioner only on the resolved eigenspaces'; momentum enlarges the effective batch B_eff = B(1+beta)/(1-beta) and 'a critical transition occurs at B_eff ~ N'; without momentum and B_eff << N the speed-up over SignSGD tends to 1 because 'the whitening of the individual per-example gradients can essentially be done per example', with a dip at B = N where the smallest singular values are amplified; Newton-Schulz acts as a ridge and 'beats exact whitening for B_eff < N'. No finite-sum estimator (the PDF text contains no occurrence of variance reduction / SVRG / SAGA / finite-sum). (b) On the axis 'minibatch finite-sum variance-reduced estimator inside a polar step': Li, Tang, Zou, arXiv:2602.11557v2, already in the table for another row. read: Algorithm 1, Section 4.3 with Theorem 4.7, Section 4.4, captions of the VR-Spectral-SGD figures): minibatch SVRG estimator (batch b, random reshuffling, m = n/b) inside the unit-norm LMO with decaying steps; limit proved independent of the batch size, constants in m = n/b; tested with b = 20 (n = 200); no study of the best batch nor of its relation to the rank. (c) H. Naganuma, S. Gupta, Y. Briki, I. Mitliagkas, I. Rish, P. Raman, H.-J. M. Shi, 'Adaptive Batch Sizes Using Non-Euclidean Gradient Noise Scales for Stochastic Sign and Spectral Descent', arXiv:2602.03001v2 (ICML 2026 according to the search result). OPENED (PDF text, Sections 3.2-3.3): critical batch size of stochastic spectral descent defined by a nuclear-norm noise scale B_S1 = ||C_row^(1/2)||_S1^2 / ||grad L||_S1^2; 'when B_k << B_S1 the noise dominates the gradient's spectral structure, causing the unitary factor U V^T to point in a random direction'; the optimal learning rate is proportional to ||grad L||_S1 / r.

*What differs.* The table row cites critical batch sizes of Muon without variance reduction (Sato et al.; Beneventano et al.). Phases of Muon is closer on the precise claim of the row: it locates the transition of the polar step at an (effective) batch equal to the matrix dimension, i.e. the maximal rank, from the rank min(B, N) of the minibatch gradient, and it shows that momentum supplies the missing rank ('effective batch'), which is the streaming analogue of the study's statement that the memory is the only source of spectral structure at batch size one (F1). It differs from the study by: streaming minibatches of a Gaussian least-squares model in a proportional high-dimensional limit; EMA momentum instead of a finite-sum memory; fixed-length Muon / SignSVD step with a constant or oracle-scheduled learning rate instead of the nuclear-scaled step; no linear convergence on a finite sum; comparison with SignSGD instead of minibatch SAGA. Li-Tang-Zou have the minibatch finite-sum estimator inside the polar step (SVRG, fixed length, decaying step, separable data) but no batch-versus-rank study; they should be cited in this row too. What remains specific to the study: a minibatch SAGA table inside the nuclear-scaled step at a constant step on a strongly convex finite sum, and the comparison of the tuned best b with min(K, d) against Euclidean minibatch SAGA at equal gradient budgets. Two published general-norm statements bear on the mechanism: Katyusha Section 6.2 (opened) says that with a minibatch estimator 'the variance with respect to an arbitrary norm may not necessarily go down by a factor of b', so no clean minibatch speed-up is stated for Katyusha2; Xie-Wang-Wu-Li arXiv:2511.20584 Section 4.3 (opened) give the tight extra dimension factor of the dual-norm variance of an average (l1 case) and a dimension-dependent lower bound for normalized steepest descent with momentum (Theorem 4.9).

*Queries.* S6 (extended): Muon orthogonalization "batch size" "rank" per-sample gradient "rank-one" small batch minibatch gradient rank spectral descent noise "batch size" smaller than rank. S13 (extended): Muon OR "spectral descent" OR "normalized steepest descent" minibatch gradient noise "nuclear norm" variance "rank" factor "batch size" must scale with rank dimension-dependent dual norm averaging. S17 (standard): "non-Euclidean" "gradient noise scale" spectral descent Muon adaptive batch size schedule 2026. Plus the general queries S5 and S20 (see item 1).

**3. PAGE-Muon with the nuclear-scaled step**: close work published.

*Closest work.* A. Beznosikov, D. Dobre, G. Gidel, 'Sarah Frank-Wolfe: Methods for Constrained Optimization with Best Rates and Practical Features', arXiv:2304.11737 (v2 Sept 2024; ICML 2024 according to the arXiv page). OPENED (PDF text; read: Algorithm 1, the discussion after Theorem 4.1, Corollary 4.2, experiments, conclusion). Algorithm 1: s_k = argmin_{s in X} <g_k, s>; x_{k+1} = x_k + eta_k (s_k - x_k); g_{k+1} = grad f(x_{k+1}) with probability p, else g_k + (1/b) sum_{i in S_k} [grad f_i(x_{k+1}) - grad f_i(x_k)]: the loopless-SARAH / PAGE estimator with EXACT finite-sum refresh, fed to an LMO, for convex and nonconvex finite sums; experiments on l1 balls (trace norms are only named as a motivation); the conclusion leaves 'fast rates in the strongly convex case' to future work. Same algorithm as Algorithm 2 'SARAH Frank-Wolfe' of Nazykov, Shestakov, Solodkin, Beznosikov, Gidel, Gasnikov, 'Stochastic Frank-Wolfe: Unified Analysis and Zoo of Special Cases', arXiv:2406.06788 (OPENED: PDF text, Algorithms 1-3, Table 1, Corollary 3.5). Earlier member of the family: Yurtsever, Sra, Cevher, 'Conditional Gradient Methods via Stochastic Path-Integrated Differential Estimator', ICML 2019, PMLR 97:7282-7291 (abstract page opened: SPIDER-FW and SPIDER-CGS for finite sums). Algorithm 1 'Compressed Gluon': PAGE / SARAH-type recursion with a large-batch refresh with probability q, EMA momentum, LMO over a ball of radius t_i, nonconvex, federated; 'the full gradient is calculated in SARAH for finite-sum problem ... we use a large batch of stochastic gradients instead'). Fang, Li, Lin, Zhang, SPIDER, arXiv:1807.01695 (abstract page opened: SPIDER combined with normalized gradient descent).

*What differs.* The table row cites Qian et al. only and lists three differences: exact finite-sum refresh, nuclear-scaled step, strongly convex. The first one is published in the LMO family: Sarah Frank-Wolfe is the PAGE estimator with exact full-gradient refresh inside a linear minimization oracle on a (convex) finite sum. On a spectral-norm ball its LMO is -R polar(g_k), so it is the PAGE estimator inside a fixed-length polar step with Frank-Wolfe averaging (Muon with weight decay in the reading of Sfyraki-Wang, arXiv:2506.04192, already cited in findings). It is closer than Qian et al. on the estimator and the problem class (single machine, finite sum, exact refresh, no momentum, convex), and farther on the geometry: the spectral instance is neither written nor tested there, whereas Qian et al. is explicitly Gluon / Muon. What still differs from both: the unconstrained nuclear-scaled (sharp) step instead of a constrained Frank-Wolfe step or a fixed-radius LMO step; a constant step with linear convergence on a strongly convex finite sum (Sarah-FW: decaying steps and sublinear rates). No work with a recursive (SARAH / SPIDER / PAGE) estimator inside a dual-norm-scaled step was found: the queries on PolarGrad / stochastic spectral descent / sharp operator with SVRG / SARAH / PAGE / SAGA returned only deterministic or momentum-based work; SPIDER itself pairs the recursive estimator with a normalized (Euclidean fixed-length) step; SpiderBoost's non-Euclidean variant is a Bregman proximal method according to the search summary (arXiv:1810.10690, not opened).

*Queries.* S4 (extended): PAGE OR SARAH OR SPIDER recursive variance reduction Muon OR "spectral LMO" OR "orthogonalized" OR "non-Euclidean" "nuclear norm" scaled step finite-sum "full gradient" refresh. S7 (standard): SARAH OR SPIDER OR PAGE "Frank-Wolfe" variance reduced finite-sum "linear minimization oracle" loopless SARAH Frank-Wolfe best rates. S11 (extended): "steepest descent" "non-Euclidean" OR "arbitrary norm" variance reduction SPIDER OR SARAH OR PAGE OR "MARINA" "LMO" OR "sharp operator" Muon Gluon Scion nonconvex finite-sum 2026. **4. Katyusha-Muon (loopless Katyusha with the nuclear-scaled polar y step)**: not found.

*Closest work.* Same as the table: Z. Allen-Zhu, Katyusha, JMLR 18 (2018) 1-51 (arXiv:1603.05953), Section 6 and Appendix E, Algorithm 4 'Katyusha2'. read Sections 6.1-6.2, Algorithm 4, Lemmas E.1-E.2): epoch-based (m = n), proximal, index drawn with probability L_i/(n Lbar), z step = Bregman mirror step, y step = argmin (9L/2)||y - x||^2 + <estimator, y> + psi(y) in an arbitrary norm, Theorem 6.1 (linear rate). The text contains no occurrence of Schatten / spectral / nuclear / trace norm; the examples of norms are l_p with p in (1, 2] and l_1 on the simplex (footnote 17); experiments are Euclidean. Nothing closer was found. Related documents opened: Allen-Zhu and Orecchia, 'Linear Coupling', arXiv:1407.1537 (PDF text: the general-norm gradient step Grad(x) = argmin_y (L/2)||y - x||^2 + <grad f(x), y - x> coupled with a mirror step; origin of the template; deterministic; no matrix-norm instance); Wei, Gu, Huang, 'An Accelerated Variance-Reduced Conditional Gradient Sliding Algorithm for First-order and Zeroth-order Optimization' (ARCS), arXiv:2109.08858 (PDF text: Algorithm 2, introduction, nuclear-norm-ball experiments); Yurtsever-Sra-Cevher PMLR 97:7282-7291 (abstract page: SPIDER-CGS); Xie, Wang, Wu, Li, 'A Tale of Two Geometries: Adaptive Optimizers and Non-Euclidean Descent', arXiv:2511.20584 (PDF text: introduction, Sections 4.2-4.3, related work); Lan, Li, Zhou, Varag, arXiv:1905.12412 (PDF text: paragraph comparing Varag with Katyusha); Li et al., MONA, arXiv:2605.26842 (abstract page and text search of the PDF); Bai and Bullins, 'Faster Acceleration for Steepest Descent', arXiv:2409.19200 (abstract page); Qian-Qu-Richtarik arXiv:1906.01481 (abstract page);

*What differs.* No instance of Katyusha (or of any accelerated variance-reduced method) with a spectral-norm steepest-descent step was found, loopless or not. Katyusha2 remains the closest: the study's variant is loopless (coin-flip refresh of Kovalev-Horvath-Richtarik), non-proximal (regularizer inside the estimator), uniformly sampled, and instantiated and tested for the spectral norm with the Frobenius distance on the z sequence. Neighbours that are not the configuration: (i) accelerated variance-reduced projection-free methods (STORC, SPIDER-CGS, ARCS) use an LMO, on a nuclear-norm ball in their experiments, as an inner solver of the proximal subproblem of an accelerated SVRG on a constrained set: this is not a steepest-descent step in the spectral norm; Sarah Frank-Wolfe (arXiv:2304.11737, opened) states that reaching Katyusha's complexity with Frank-Wolfe-type methods is an open question; (ii) Xie-Wang-Wu-Li prove an accelerated O~(T^-2) rate for adaptive optimizers over structured preconditioner sets under 'adaptive smoothness' and recall the Guzman-Nemirovski lower bound Omega(L/(T log T)) under standard l_infinity smoothness: acceleration in a steepest-descent geometry is not available under norm-smoothness alone, which is consistent with F8 (certified complexity of the Euclidean order) and worth citing there; (iii) Varag notes that Katyusha's second proximal mapping cannot use a Bregman distance (it must be the squared norm), and is the Bregman-distance accelerated variance-reduced alternative (no steepest-descent step); (iv) MONA ('Muon Optimizer with Nesterov Acceleration') adds an Adan-type moving average of gradient differences before orthogonalization for LLM training: no finite sum, no Katyusha; (v) Bai-Bullins accelerate l_p steepest descent (no Schatten norm in the abstract, no variance reduction).

*Limits stated by this agent.*

- Hard cap reached: 22 WebSearch calls (15 extended, 7 standard). Per item: importance sampling 5 dedicated queries, batch / rank 3, PAGE 6, Katyusha 6, plus 2 general queries (finite-sum variance-reduced Muon; public code / blog). No further search was possible for the leads that appeared late (for example forward citations of Phases of Muon or of Sarah Frank-Wolfe).
- The search tool returns about ten links per query with an automatic summary. It is not an arXiv full-text or listing search and not a citation-forward search: papers whose abstract uses another vocabulary, ICLR 2027 submissions on OpenReview, theses, and non-English sources may be missed. No query on a search engine or search endpoint was made through WebFetch or curl.
- The statement that Katyusha2 has not been instantiated for the spectral norm rests on six keyword queries and on text searches of the Katyusha, linear-coupling and L-Katyusha papers; the papers citing Katyusha (several thousand) were not enumerated.
- Public code and blog posts: one query only (S20), no result. GitHub code search was not used.
- Documents were read as pdftotext extractions (formulas partly garbled). Algorithm boxes were read for Katyusha2, Sarah Frank-Wolfe, the Frank-Wolfe zoo (Algorithms 1-3), Compressed Gluon, Li-Tang-Zou (Algorithm 1), the Muon recursion of Phases of Muon, and ARCS (structure only). In the extracted text of the Katyusha2 box the weight 1/(n p_i) is not visible on line 10; it is explicit in Section 5.1 and in the proof of Lemma E.2.
- Abstract page only (body not read): SPIDER-FW / SPIDER-CGS, SPIDER, JacSketch, SAGA with arbitrary sampling, L-SVRG and L-Katyusha with arbitrary sampling, Schmidt et al. 2015, Xiao-Zhang 2014, Gazagnadou et al., Kim et al., Bai-Bullins, Kovalev arXiv:2503.12645, Davis-Drusvyatskiy arXiv:2512.04299. Identifiers listed as NOT OPENED in other_relevant_papers come from search snippets or reference lists only.
- A search summary attributed to Negiar et al. (arXiv:2002.11860) a sentence about further gains from non-uniform sampling;
- closer_than_table is true for items 1 to 3 in the sense 'closer than the works cited in that row'. For item 1 the work (Katyusha Section 6) is already cited in other rows of the table; for item 3 Sarah Frank-Wolfe is closer on the estimator and the problem class but farther on the geometry than Qian et al. (it is not a Muon / spectral paper).
- The remark in item 1 that the spectral-norm and Euclidean per-sample smoothness constants of the loss part coincide for rank-one per-sample gradients is my own one-line derivation, not a cited result, and was not checked numerically (no Python was run).
- Phases of Muon v2 is dated 27 October 2026 and Naganuma et al. v2 July 2026: this area moves fast and concurrent work on batch size and rank for spectral optimizers is likely; the check is valid for 2026-11-12 only.
- Nothing else in the repository was modified; no Python and no git command was run.

### Base methods and landscape

**1. Base methods: SAGA-Muon / L-SVRG-Muon (SAGA or loopless-SVRG estimator inside the nuclear-scaled polar step W <- W - gamma ||omega||_* polar(omega), constant step size, linear convergence observed on strongly convex finite sums)**: not found.

*Closest work.* Nothing closer than the positioning table was found. Additional neighbours found by this search, none closer: (a) Fang, Li, Lin, Zhang, 'SPIDER: Near-Optimal Non-Convex Optimization via Stochastic Path Integrated Differential Estimator', 2018, arXiv:1807.01695: OPENED (PDF text extracted, Algorithm 1 and Section 3.2 read); (b) Oikonomidis, Quan, Antonakopoulos, Silveti-Falls, Cevher, Patrinos, 'Constrained Stochastic Spectral Preconditioning Converges for Nonconvex Objectives', May 2026, arXiv:2605.11850: OPENED (arXiv HTML through the fetch tool: base update, Section 4.1 / Theorem 4.5, Theorem 4.7, appendices D and E); (c) Qin, Liu, Xu, 'Convergence of Sign-based Random Reshuffling Algorithms for Nonconvex Optimization', arXiv:2310.15976 (v4 Aug 2026): abstract page only (SignRVR 'signs an SVRG estimator anchored at the beginning of every epoch'); (d) Jiang, Li, Zhang, 'Revisiting Distributed Sign-Based Variance Reduction', Sep 2026, arXiv:2609.18656: abstract page only; (e) Kovalev, Borodich, 'Non-Euclidean SGD for Structured Optimization: Unified Analysis and Improved Rates', arXiv:2511.11466v2: OPENED (HTML through the fetch tool, Algorithm 1, eqs. 12a-12c).

*What differs.* Every finite-sum variance-reduced method found inside a non-Euclidean or LMO-type step uses a FIXED-LENGTH step (unit-norm LMO, sign, or Euclidean normalization) with a decaying or accuracy-tied step size and a sublinear guarantee: Li-Tang-Zou on separable data, the sign methods SignSVRG / SignRVR / arXiv:2609.18656, SPIDER-SFO in the Euclidean norm. The other variance-reduced spectral methods found use momentum-based (STORM-type) estimators: the MARS-M family already cited, arXiv:2605.11850 (STORM estimator inside a bounded-gain spectral map, rate K^(-1/3), decaying steps), arXiv:2511.11466 (LMO step with momentum variance reduction, no finite sum, no linear rate). The general-norm template of Katyusha Section 6 is not instantiated for the spectral norm in anything found. No paper, preprint or public code was found that iterates W <- W - gamma ||omega||_* polar(omega) with omega a SAGA or loopless-SVRG estimator at a constant step, nor any linear-rate statement for it. **2. Claim: with the fixed-length (Muon) step a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator does not remove an error floor of order lr^2 at a constant learning rate, whereas a nuclear-scaled, clipped or bounded-gain length does**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Chzhen and Schechtman, 'SignSVRG: fixing SignSGD via variance reduction', 2023, arXiv:2305.13187: OPENED (PDF downloaded, text extracted; read the description of SignSVRG in Section 3 and Section 4.2 'On the absence of linear rates for strongly convex functions' with its Example 2). Second neighbour: Fang, Li, Lin, Zhang, SPIDER, 2018, arXiv:1807.01695: OPENED (Algorithm 1, Options I and II, and Section 3.2 read). Precedents that are not in the table, all OPENED: Rhee, Simon, Karkada, 'Muon learns balanced solutions in matrix factorization without slow saddle-to-saddle dynamics', arXiv:2606.30509 (HTML through the fetch tool, Sections 1 and 3.1); Ma, Huang, Chi, Chen, 'Preconditioning Benefits of Spectral Orthogonalization in Muon', arXiv:2601.13474 (HTML through the fetch tool, eq. 4, Theorems 1 and 3); Do, Dereich, Jentzen, 'On MUON optimization: From non-convergence to an error analysis with Polar Express and the Newton-Schulz polynomial from implementations', arXiv:2608.04607 (PDF pages 1-14 extracted; Theorems 1.2 and 1.3 and the related-work paragraph read).

*What differs.* Not found for Muon (polar step) with a finite-sum estimator: the statement of the study is not published in that form. For sign + SVRG it exists as an argued remark, not a theorem: SignSVRG Section 4.2 gives the exact-gradient example (sign GD on x^2/2 'does not converge linearly for any fixed step-size') and writes that 'this is not a proof of the absence of linear rates for SignSVRG'; Section 3 adds that the variance of the dithered sign 'is not expected to go to zero', so that the usual properties of variance reduction (linear convergence for strongly convex functions) are not expected. It gives no floor of order lr^2 and compares no length rules. For normalized SGD + SARAH the fact is implicit in the design of SPIDER-SFO: Option I takes steps of fixed length eta = eps/(L n0) and stops when ||v|| <= 2 eps (accuracy tied to the step length), Option II uses the clipped length eta_k = min(eps/(L n0 ||v_k||), 1/(2 L n0)), i.e. fixed length far from stationarity and a plain variance-reduced gradient step near it; Euclidean norm, nonconvex stationarity, no statement of a floor. The table cites, for this floor, only exact-gradient results (Jawanpuria et al., Gonon et al., PolarGrad): SignSVRG Section 4.2 and SPIDER are closer because they concern variance-reduced estimators, but they are sign / Euclidean, not spectral. Two more exact-gradient precedents and a fourth remedy that the study does not test: Rhee-Simon-Karkada Section 3.1 (constant-rate Muon iterates 'oscillate about the solution manifold without converging', 'it is necessary to anneal the learning rate', geometric annealing by 1/2 per step gives linear convergence) and Ma-Huang-Chi-Chen Theorems 1 and 3 (X - eta_t msign(grad f) converges linearly with eta_t = C rho^t). For the other floor (noise), Do-Dereich-Jentzen Theorem 1.3 proves that Muon with decaying learning rates does not converge to the minimizer of a scalar quadratic stochastic problem for almost every batch size when the third central moment of the data is nonzero, with a mean square error bounded by C (gamma_n + 1/M): the bias that a finite-sum estimator removes.

**3. Methodology: exact computation, by enumeration of the sampled index, of the conditional expectation of an orthogonalized stochastic step (bias and alignment of the mean step)**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Chen, Zhao, Xu, Liu, 'Muon Sublates the Edge of Stability in LLM Pretraining', arXiv:2609.34915v2 (Nov 2026): OPENED (arXiv HTML through the fetch tool, first 100k characters: Section 2, Definition 2.1 with eqs. 4-5, Theorem 2.1, Appendix B.3-B.5). Other neighbours, opened the same way: Chen, Li, Wang, Lu, 'When and How Does Muon Precondition? The Role of Structured Gradient Noise', arXiv:2610.31728 (Proposition 3.1, Theorem 4.1, Proposition 4.4, Appendix C.4); Jacobs, Burkholz, 'From Parallel to Sequential: Small-Batch Noise Restores Simplicity Bias of Muon', arXiv:2610.16868 (Theorem 4.2, Lemma 4.5). PDF text read: Do, Dereich, Jentzen, arXiv:2608.04607 (Theorem 1.3). Abstract page only: Roulet, Agarwala, 'Per-example gradients: a new frontier for understanding and improving optimizers', arXiv:2510.00236.

*What differs.* Not found as an exact enumeration. Chen-Zhao-Xu-Liu define the batch-to-population coherence rho_b = E_b<G, P> / ||G||_* (G the full gradient, P the polar factor of the minibatch gradient), which is the lab's alignment of the mean step, and an exact conditional loss identity E[L(W+) - L(W) | W] = (eta^2 ||G||_* / 2)(s_b - 2 rho_b / eta) (Theorem 2.1, an algebraic rearrangement of the definitions); but they evaluate the expectations by Monte Carlo over 16 or 32 probe batches at frozen states (Appendix B.4), for plain minibatch gradients of deep networks with the fixed-length step, without any variance-reduced estimator. Chen-Li-Wang-Lu are analytical under a noise or data model (Proposition 3.1: E[msgn(G + sigma Xi)] = (c_d / sigma) G + O(||G||_F^3 / sigma^3); Theorem 4.1: an exact two-task batch-size threshold from the binomial batch composition; they also state the rank-one identity msgn(a b^T) = a b^T / (||a|| ||b||), the fact F1 of the study). Jacobs-Burkholz give a closed form per singular mode under Gaussian noise (an erf, Theorem 4.2). Do-Dereich-Jentzen obtain the bias of the Newton-Schulz map of a batch mean from the third central moment of the data. Li-Tang-Zou Definition 4.9 aggregates in closed form the normalized per-sample gradients (the mean step at batch size one) on a special dataset. SignSVRG makes the sign step unbiased by dithering instead of computing its bias. None enumerates the n possible next steps of a single-index method at a state of the run, and none treats a SAGA / SVRG estimator. Compared with the references of Section 3 for this point (Beneventano et al. and Xie, linearizations), arXiv:2609.34915 is closer: same statistic, measured at frozen states along trajectories, by sampling instead of enumeration.

**landscape: Oikonomidis, Quan, Antonakopoulos, Silveti-Falls, Cevher, Patrinos, 'Constrained Stochastic Spectral Preconditioning Converges for Nonconvex Objectives' (arXiv:2605.11850) -> soft SAGA-Muon (bounded-gain map applied to a variance-reduced estimator) and the bounded-gain part of claim 2**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Oikonomidis, Quan, Antonakopoulos, Silveti-Falls, Cevher, Patrinos, 'Constrained Stochastic Spectral Preconditioning Converges for Nonconvex Objectives', 12 May 2026, arXiv:2605.11850: OPENED (abstract page, then the arXiv HTML through the fetch tool in two passes covering characters 0-200k of 220k: forward-backward update, spectral maps, Section 4.1 / Theorem 4.5 with the STORM estimator, Theorem 4.7, Remark 3.4, appendices D and E; Appendix G with the experiments was not read).

*What differs.* Same idea: a spectral map with bounded gain that vanishes with its argument (s -> s/(eps + |s|), or s/(eps^k + |s|^k)^(1/k) with k > 2; the matrix sign is the limit eps -> 0) applied to a variance-reduced estimator, jointly (no correction outside the map); the paper notes the bias E[map(stochastic gradient)] != map(gradient) and argues (Remark 3.4) that Muon's polynomial iterations are better modelled by such a map than by the exact sign. What differs: the estimator is STORM (momentum-based, two gradients at the same sample), not a finite-sum SAGA / SVRG memory; eps is a fixed constant, or shrinks with the horizon in Theorem 4.7, and is not tied to the innovation of the current step; the map is not s/sqrt(s^2 + eps^2); the setting is nonconvex, proximal / constrained, with decaying steps and a K^(-1/3) rate on a Bregman gap; no strongly convex finite sum, no linear rate, no constant-step claim. It is closer than the works of the bounded-gain row (arXiv:2609.30546, Jawanpuria et al., Shulgin et al.), none of which feeds a variance-reduced estimator to the map.

*Queries.* No dedicated query: found in the citation lists of MARS-M, Qian et al. arXiv:2512.16598, PolarGrad, Sfyraki-Wang and arXiv:2510.11312 (the last one reached after Q16, which itself returned nothing relevant).

**landscape: Fang, Li, Lin, Zhang, 'SPIDER' (arXiv:1807.01695) -> clipped length with a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator; fixed-length step on a recursive (PAGE / SARAH-type) estimator**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Fang, Li, Lin, Zhang, 'SPIDER: Near-Optimal Non-Convex Optimization via Stochastic Path Integrated Differential Estimator', NeurIPS 2018, arXiv:1807.01695: OPENED (PDF, first 12 pages extracted; Algorithm 1 'Spider-SFO' and Section 3.2 read).

*What differs.* Algorithm 1 uses the recursive estimator v_k = grad f_S(x_k) - grad f_S(x_{k-1}) + v_{k-1} with a full gradient every q iterations in the finite-sum case, and two step rules: Option I, x_{k+1} = x_k - eta v_k/||v_k|| with eta = eps/(L n0) (a fixed-length normalized step; the paper describes it as 'a stochastic variant of NGD'), and Option II, x_{k+1} = x_k - eta_k v_k with eta_k = min(eps/(L n0 ||v_k||), 1/(2 L n0)), which is the clipped length of the lab (fixed length far from stationarity, plain step near it). So 'clipped length x finite-sum variance-reduced estimator' and 'fixed length x finite-sum recursive estimator' are published since 2018 in the Euclidean geometry. What differs: Euclidean norm (normalization of a vector, no polar factor, no spectral norm), SARAH / SPIDER estimator instead of SAGA, nonconvex stationarity with the step length tied to the target accuracy, no strongly convex finite sum and no linear rate. It is closer than the reference of the clipped row (generalized gradient norm clipping with momentum, arXiv:2506.01913) on the estimator axis only, not on the geometry.

*Queries.* Surfaced by Q7 (the result summary recalled that SPIDER uses the SARAH estimator with a normalized update); then opened directly. Related: Q21.

**landscape: Zhang, Kong, Geng, Huang, 'SUDA-Muon' (arXiv:2604.23980) -> the placement question (joint placement versus the map applied before the correction); bullet 'not found: any study of where the nonlinear map should sit relative to the variance-reduction correction'**: close work published.

*Closest work.* Zhang, Kong, Geng, Huang, 'SUDA-Muon: Structural Design Principles and Boundaries for Fully Decentralized Muon', 27 Apr 2026, arXiv:2604.23980: OPENED (PDF text extracted; Algorithm 1, Lemma 1, Proposition 5 in Section 4.5 and Section 4.6 read). Abstract page only, same theme: Li, Zhang, Liu, Bao, 'Denoise First, Orthogonalize Later: Understanding Momentum in Muon via Spectral Filtering', arXiv:2606.03899 (momentum before versus after the orthogonalization); Smirnova, Kravatskiy, 'Sign compression for Muon: SignMuon, MuonSign, and the Limits of Error Feedback', arXiv:2607.29674; Roulet, Agarwala, arXiv:2510.00236 (placement of the sign in the gradient processing chain).

*What differs.* SUDA-Muon states the order between a correction and the polar map as a design principle: 'tracking before polarization' (the iterate moves by alpha msgn(H) with H the gradient-tracked momentum, Algorithm 1 line 11), and Proposition 5 exhibits two smooth heterogeneous objectives for which the variant that applies msgn to the local momentum keeps the averaged iterate at a non-stationary point; Section 4.6 contrasts 'average-then-polarize' (federated) with 'local-polarize-then-average' (decentralized) on a toy example. In the vocabulary of the study this says that the map must act on the corrected estimator (the joint placement). What differs: the correction is decentralized gradient tracking against client heterogeneity, not a finite-sum variance-reduction memory; fixed-length steps, nonconvex rate K^(-1/4); no placement with part of the estimator kept outside the map (split, tangent, radial) is considered. It is of the same nature as FedMuon (already cited, which shows that local Muon without control variates stalls), hence not closer than the table; but together with arXiv:2606.03899 it means that the bullet of Section 3 should read 'relative to a finite-sum variance-reduction correction' and cite these placement studies for momentum, tracking and compression.

*Queries.* No dedicated query: found in the citation lists of FedMuon and Gluon. arXiv:2606.03899 was found in the citation lists of Kovalev arXiv:2503.12645 and Gluon; arXiv:2607.29674 in those of FedMuon and Qian et al. arXiv:2604.10689; arXiv:2510.00236 was returned by Q19.
