Orthogonalized updates discard the residual scale, overshoot and oscillate near the solution; proposes a switch to GD-like refinement in a final phase. arXiv:2605.19619, Huang, Luo, Chen, 'MiMuon: Mixed Muon Optimizer with Improved Generalization for Large Models' (May 2026): OPENED (HTML through the fetch tool, Algorithm 2): hard switch between the orthogonalized momentum and the raw momentum (in practice a gate on ||M||_F), i.e. a hybrid length rule; neighbour of the MuSGD reference of the split row. arXiv:2605.18999, Demidovich, Chakraborty, Malinovsky, Nedic, Richtarik, 'Distance-Aware Muon: Adaptive Step Scaling for Normalized Optimization' (May 2026): abstract page only; three adaptive rules for the length of the Muon step. All three concern the length rule of Section 5.1, without variance reduction.
- arXiv:2311.05589, Yin, Xu, Li, Darrell, Liu, 'A Coefficient Makes SVRG Effective' (ICLR 2025): abstract page only. alpha-SVRG multiplies the SVRG variance-reduction term by a decaying coefficient (an unbiased partial control variate, unlike the biased damping m + theta c of B-SAGA): a neighbour of the damped estimators. Its 9 citing papers (Semantic Scholar) contain no Muon combination other than MARS (arXiv:2411.10438, not opened) and MARS-M.
- arXiv:2605.30648, Vaswani, Sun, Babanezhad, 'Convergence of Steepest Descent and Adam under Non-Uniform Smoothness' (ICML 2026): abstract page only. Sign GD converges linearly on logistic regression with separable data: the fixed-length floor F2 needs an attained minimizer (as in the study); worth one sentence, since Li-Tang-Zou work on separable data.
- arXiv:2601.14603, Li, Fan, Li, 'Variance-Adaptive Muon: Pre-Orthogonalization Variance Modulation for Efficient Language Model Pretraining' (Jan 2026, v2 Sep 2026): abstract page only. Noise-to-signal modulation of the momentum before the Newton-Schulz step, with one variance buffer: a published way of damping, before the map, what is at the noise level; possible neighbour of the bounded-gain map with an offset proportional to the innovation (to be opened by whoever owns that row).

*Limits stated by this agent.*

- WebSearch worked but was weak on this topic: boolean operators and exact phrases were visibly not honoured (the exact-name query Q6 returned SAGA and Langevin papers) and no query returned a finite-sum variance-reduced Muon paper. 19 of the 22 calls were in extended mode and 3 in standard mode, i.e. more extended calls than the suggested half.
- Citation lists were obtained with WebFetch on the Semantic Scholar citation lookup of a given arXiv identifier (a per-paper listing, not a keyword search). They were screened on titles only, and the index may lag for October-November 2026 papers (no citing paper is listed for Xie arXiv:2609.32861; Jawanpuria et al. arXiv:2610.27747 was not queried). The list of the 228 papers citing Pethick et al. arXiv:2502.07529 was screened by the fetch tool with a keyword filter that visibly missed titles (it did not return MARS-M nor arXiv:2512.16598): treat that list as not checked.
- Workshop and conference listings could not be checked: the OPT 2026 page (opt-ml.org) lists no accepted paper yet; the OpenReview venue listings redirect to a bot challenge, which I did not try to pass; no HiLD 2026 nor NeurIPS 2026 listing was opened. Google Scholar, GitHub code search and non-English blogs were not consulted.
- Long arXiv HTML pages were read through the WebFetch summarizer, usually on the first 100k characters: the section, theorem and equation numbers quoted from those pages are as reported by that tool. I read the text myself (pdftotext) only for SignSVRG (Section 4.2), SPIDER (Algorithm 1), SUDA-Muon (Algorithm 1, Proposition 5, Section 4.6) and Do-Dereich-Jentzen (pages 1-14).
- Katyusha Section 6, SSVR, Jawanpuria et al., Gonon et al. and PolarGrad were not re-opened; Li-Tang-Zou, FedMuon and the appendix of Jawanpuria et al. were only consulted in the texts extracted by w2.
- 'not_found' for item 1 and for the sweep means: not found by these 22 queries and these citation lists. The remaining risk is a paper from another community with another vocabulary (contractive compressors applied directly to SVRG / SAGA estimators, nonlinear or dual-space preconditioning with variance reduction, Frank-Wolfe with a strongly convex regularizer): one query each (Q22, Q16, Q7) returned nothing relevant, which is thin evidence.
- Wording of the base-method claim: by the w2 reading of Katyusha Section 6 (not re-opened here), the y-step of Katyusha2 in the spectral norm is the nuclear-scaled polar step of an SVRG estimator, with a certified linear rate for the accelerated scheme. 'Not found' for L-SVRG-Muon should therefore be worded 'not found as a stand-alone (non-accelerated) method, nor instantiated for the spectral norm', which is what the table says.
- Claim 2 has a boundary that the search made visible: the remedy 'geometric decay of the fixed length' is published for exact gradients (arXiv:2601.13474, arXiv:2606.30509) and was not found combined with a finite-sum estimator; the study's sentence 'the length rule must remove the floor' should mention that a geometric schedule is the published alternative to a gradient-dependent length.

## Second pass: attempts to refute the verdicts

### Angle: Frank-Wolfe / linear minimization oracle (18 searches)

**SAGA-Muon / L-SVRG-Muon: SAGA or loopless-SVRG estimator inside the nuclear-scaled polar step, linear rate at constant step (base methods; stage-1 verdict not_found)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* No Frank-Wolfe / LMO / matching-pursuit work was found with a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator inside the unconstrained nuclear-scaled polar step. Opened (PDF text extracted with pdftotext and read directly unless stated): (a) Combettes, Spiegel, Pokutta, 'Projection-Free Adaptive Gradients for Large-Scale Optimization', arXiv:2009.14114v3 (2021): Template 3, Algorithm 5 'AdaSVRF', Theorem 3.5, Section 4.1. (b) Song, Cui, Xia, Jiang, 'Accelerated Stochastic Greedy Coordinate Descent by Soft Thresholding Projection onto Simplex' (ASGCD), arXiv:1702.07842v2 (2017): Eq. (2)-(3), Algorithm 3, Theorem 2, Lemma 17. (c) Riabinin, Shulgin, Gruntkowska, Richtarik, 'Gluon', arXiv:2505.13416v1: Appendix D.1 (Assumption 4, Theorem 7). (d) Locatello, Raj, Karimireddy, Ratsch, Scholkopf, Stich, Jaggi, 'On Matching Pursuit and Coordinate Descent', arXiv:1803.09539v7: abstract, Algorithms 1-2, Section 2.1.2. (e) Garber, Kaplan, 'Fast Stochastic Algorithms for Low-rank and Nonsmooth Matrix Problems', arXiv:1809.10477v1: abstract, Algorithm 1, Theorems 1-2. (f) Nandhan, Khademi, Silveti-Falls, 'Boosted Stochastic Frank-Wolfe for Constrained Nonconvex Optimization', arXiv:2605.25255v1 (24 May 2026): abstract, contributions, Algorithm BSFW, Section 5. (g) Carlson, Cevher, Carin, 'Stochastic Spectral Descent for Restricted Boltzmann Machines', AISTATS 2015, PMLR v38 (proceedings.mlr.press/v38/carlson15.pdf): introduction, Section 3.4; Carlson, Collins, Hsieh, Carin, Cevher, 'Preconditioned Spectral Descent for Deep Learning', NeurIPS 2015 (PDF from papers.nips.cc, keyword scan only); the journal version 'Stochastic Spectral Descent for Discrete Graphical Models', IEEE JSTSP 10(2), 2016: abstract on the EPFL record page only, full text NOT readable. (h) Kim, Klabjan, 'Stochastic Variance-Reduced Algorithms for PCA with Arbitrary Mini-Batch Sizes', PMLR v108 (kim20e.pdf): Section 2, Algorithms 1-2.

*Explanation.* What this literature adds to the row; none of it is the configuration. (1) Length rule. The table describes the Frank-Wolfe neighbours (SVRF, SagaFw) as constrained steps with open-loop sizes. AdaSVRF is different: its update is y + gamma (v - y), v the LMO of the SVRG-based model gradient, gamma = min{eta_t <grad Q_t(y), y - v> / ||y - v||_H^2, gamma_t} (Template 3, line 8): a Frank-Wolfe short step computed from the SVRG estimator itself. The short step is the constrained form of the dual-norm (nuclear) scaling: the table's own reference arXiv:2506.01913 presents the short step and the clipped steepest-descent step as the same rule, and for K = 1, H = I on a spectral-norm ball of large radius the AdaSVRF step tends to -(eta / r) ||g||_* polar(g) (my computation, not in the paper). But AdaSVRF is constrained (convex combination towards a vertex of a fixed set), uses a clipped diagonal AdaGrad metric, an epoch SVRG estimator with batch sizes growing like t (Theorem 3.5), a cap gamma_t = 2/(t+2), has an O(1/t) rate and is run on l1 and l_infinity balls only (no occurrence of 'spectral' in its text). (2) Instantiated general-norm template. ASGCD puts the SVRG estimator inside the l1-norm steepest-descent step argmin_h <g, h> + (1/(2 eta)) ||h||_1^2 + lam ||x + h||_1 (Eq. (3); Algorithm 3, step (e)), smoothness being measured in the l1 norm (Eq. (2)): the l1 instance of Katyusha Section 6, only inside the accelerated scheme, with an O(1/S^2) rate. S12 returned no stand-alone (non-accelerated) greedy-coordinate SVRG / SAGA. (3) Stochastic dual-norm-scaled LMO step. Gluon's Theorem 7 analyses the step t_i = (1 - zeta) ||g_i||_* / (L0_i + (1 + zeta) L1_i ||g_i||_*) on stochastic gradients without momentum under 'bounded relative variance' ||g - grad f||_* <= zeta ||g||_* almost surely (Assumption 4, which the authors call strong): the published form of the condition of F5; nonconvex, sublinear, no specific estimator. (4) Deterministic. Generalized matching pursuit x - (<grad f, z> / L_A) z with z = LMO_A(grad f) and smoothness in the atomic norm has a linear rate for strongly convex objectives (Locatello et al.); with the orthogonal matrices as atom set it is the nuclear-scaled polar step (my reading: that atom set does not appear in the paper). S1 returned no stochastic or variance-reduced matching pursuit. (5) Garber-Kaplan run an SVRG estimator inside a generalized conditional gradient step and need O(log 1/eps) epochs on strongly convex finite sums, but the oracle is a Euclidean (weak) proximal step computed by a low-rank SVD, for nuclear-norm problems; the table's phrase 'sublinear or restart-based rates' for the Frank-Wolfe family should mention it. (6) BSFW (2026) feeds SAGA, L-SVRG and SAG estimators to (boosted) LMO steps; experiments on l1 balls, a nuclear-norm ball, and a constraint written ||U||_op <= tau on a single column (a Euclidean ball); open-loop steps, O(1/t) or O(1/sqrt(t)) rates. (7) The two stochastic-spectral-descent papers of 2015 that could be read (origin of the nuclear-scaled step on stochastic gradients) contain no variance reduction (no occurrence of SVRG, SAG, SAGA or 'variance reduction'). (8) Variance-reduced power iteration (Kim-Klabjan) is a k = 1 method: no orthogonalization of a matrix estimator. S18 found no linearly convergent variance-reduced Frank-Wolfe with a short step and an interior minimizer, which would have been the constrained twin of the configuration. Katyusha Section 6 (already in the table) stays the closest.

*Queries.* Quoted phrases of the real queries are written with single quotes. S1 [extended]: 'matching pursuit' OR 'steepest coordinate descent' stochastic variance-reduced SVRG OR SAGA finite-sum 'atomic norm' OR 'linear minimization oracle' unconstrained steepest descent step length 'dual norm' linear convergence strongly convex. S2 [extended]: Frank-Wolfe OR 'conditional gradient' over the 'spectral norm ball' OR 'operator norm ball' OR 'convex hull of orthogonal matrices' stochastic variance-reduced SVRG OR SAGA OR SPIDER, linear minimization oracle equals polar factor OR 'matrix sign' of the gradient estimator. S4 [extended]: stochastic Frank-Wolfe finite-sum variance reduction 'short step' OR 'adaptive step size' OR 'line search' step size from estimated Frank-Wolfe gap with independent samples, direction from linear minimization oracle of averaged gradient, 'linear convergence' strongly convex constant step. S7 [extended]: stochastic variance-reduced 'generalized power method' OR 'orthogonal iteration' OR 'block power method' SVRG finite-sum: next iterate is the polar factor OR QR orthogonalization of the variance-reduced gradient estimator, conditional gradient for convex maximization over orthogonal matrices, linear convergence. S12 [extended]: 'greedy coordinate descent' OR 'Gauss-Southwell' OR 'l1-norm steepest descent' combined with SVRG OR SAGA variance-reduced gradient estimator, finite-sum, coordinate selected from the variance-reduced estimator or from the snapshot full gradient, linear convergence strongly convex, non-Euclidean steepest descent step. S13 [extended]: 2026 arXiv new stochastic Frank-Wolfe OR 'conditional gradient' method finite-sum variance reduction SAGA OR 'L-SVRG' OR SARAH with spectral-norm OR Schatten norm ball linear minimization oracle, connection to Muon, strongly convex OR PL linear convergence. S17 [standard]: 'Stochastic Spectral Descent for Discrete Graphical Models' Carlson Hsieh Collins Carin Cevher. S18 [extended]: linearly convergent variance-reduced stochastic Frank-Wolfe for strongly convex finite-sum when the minimizer lies in the interior of the constraint set, step size from the Frank-Wolfe gap of the SVRG OR SAGA estimator (short step, no line search), constant batch size, linear rate in expectation.

**clipped length min(rho, gamma ||omega||_*) with a SAGA estimator, and mean-scaled length (stage-1 verdict published_close: SPIDER-SFO Option II, (L0,L1)-Spider, EPISODE, CGTVR in the Euclidean norm; generalized gradient norm clipping with momentum in the table)**: closer work found.

*Work.* Combettes, Spiegel, Pokutta, 'Projection-Free Adaptive Gradients for Large-Scale Optimization', arXiv:2009.14114v3 (15 Feb 2021) [opened; PDF text read: Template 3, Algorithm 5 AdaSVRF, Theorem 3.5, Section 4.1]. Supporting documents: Pethick, Xie, Erdogan, Antonakopoulos, Silveti-Falls, Cevher, arXiv:2506.01913 [the table's reference; Pokutta, Spiegel, Zimmer, 'Deep Neural Network Training with Frank-Wolfe', arXiv:2010.07243v2 [opened; PDF text read: Section 3.1 with Eq. (13)-(14), Section 2.2, experiment settings of the appendix]; Goldfarb, Iyengar, Zhou, 'Semi-Stochastic Frank-Wolfe Algorithms with Away-Steps for Block-Coordinate Structure Problems', arXiv:1602.01543v3 [opened; PDF text read: abstract, Algorithm 1, remarks after Theorem 1].

*Explanation.* Stage 1 reported that no paper combining a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator with a clipped LMO step in a non-Euclidean norm was found (its Q21). AdaSVRF is that combination in the constrained Frank-Wolfe form: the direction is v - y with v the LMO, over any compact convex set, of the SVRG-based model gradient, and the step is gamma = min{eta_t <grad Q_t(y), y - v> / ||y - v||_H^2, gamma_t}: proportional to the Frank-Wolfe gap of the estimator, capped by gamma_t. The correspondence with the lab's rule is stated in the table's own reference: arXiv:2506.01913 writes that 'the correct analog of clipping in the constrained setting corresponds to a clipped version of the Frank-Wolfe short step', and its Algorithm 2 (S3CG, 'Stochastic Short Step Conditional Gradient') applies that capped short step to a momentum estimator. So 'fixed length far from the optimum, estimator-proportional length near it, finite-sum variance-reduced estimator, LMO of an arbitrary norm ball' is published since 2020. Differences that matter: a Frank-Wolfe step towards a vertex of a fixed constraint set, not a trust-region step centred at the iterate; the pairing is the Frank-Wolfe gap in a clipped diagonal AdaGrad metric, not the nuclear norm; epoch SVRG with batch sizes growing like t instead of a SAGA table; a cap gamma_t = 2/(t+2) that decays instead of a constant rho; O(1/t) rate; experiments on l1 and l_infinity balls, no spectral-norm ball. Older Frank-Wolfe precedents of a capped, estimator-proportional step on a stochastic gradient: Goldfarb et al. 2016 (step min{-<g, d> / (L ||d||^2), gamma_max} on a minibatch gradient without control variate; linear rate on polytopes, and the paper explains why its proof fails on sets with a curved boundary); Pokutta et al. 2020 (Eq. (14), 'gradient rescaling': step min(alpha ||g||_2 / ||v - theta||_2, 1)(v - theta), stated for SFW; the SVRF and ORGFW runs whose settings I read are reported with Eq. (13), a division by the diameter). Mean-scaled half: nothing from this literature (the smooth step of Li-Sadeghi-Giannakis, see the radial entry, is the opposite assignment: direction from the memory, length from the current gradient).

*Queries.* S4 and S18 (texts in the first entry). S11 [extended]: Muon OR Scion OR 'spectral norm' LMO optimizer with Frank-Wolfe 'short step' OR 'Frank-Wolfe gap' adaptive step size: step length equals the dual pairing of an independent or fresh stochastic gradient with the LMO direction of the momentum, 2026 arXiv, variance-reduced finite-sum. AdaSVRF was returned by S4.

**radial placement: predictable direction polar(m), unbiased scalar length <omega, O_m> (stage-1 verdict published_close: GLyDER, arXiv:2311.13877)**: closer work found.

*Work.* Li, Sadeghi, Giannakis, 'Heavy Ball Momentum for Conditional Gradient', arXiv:2110.04243v1 (8 Oct 2021; venue not checked) [candidate recalled from memory, opened directly by identifier; PDF text read: Algorithm 2 (HFW), Eq. (4), Section 3.1, Section 3.3 with Eq. (9) and Theorem 2, experiment captions]. Also: Locatello et al., arXiv:1803.09539v7 [opened; Section 2.1.2 'random pursuit']; Braun, Pokutta, Zink, 'Lazifying Conditional Gradient Algorithms', arXiv:1610.05120v4 [opened; Algorithms 2-3, Remark 4.5, Section 5.1].

*Explanation.* HFW is the Frank-Wolfe form of the radial rule with exact gradients. Algorithm 2: g_{k+1} = (1 - delta_k) g_k + delta_k grad f(x_k) (a weighted average of all past gradients, delta_k = 2/(k+2)), v_{k+1} = LMO(g_{k+1}), x_{k+1} = (1 - eta_k) x_k + eta_k v_{k+1}; 'smooth step size' (9): eta_k = max{0, min{<grad f(x_k), x_k - v_{k+1}> / (L ||v_{k+1} - x_k||^2), 1}}. The direction comes from the LMO of a gradient memory, the length from the signed pairing of the current gradient with that direction; the paper notes that this pairing 'is no longer guaranteed to be positive' (hence the trimming at 0) and that the rule gives a non-increasing objective (its Appendix A.2): the deterministic counterparts of F9 and of its remark that a can be negative with a stale memory. On a spectral-norm ball the LMO is the polar factor, so the step is along polar(memory) with a length given by the pairing, up to the Frank-Wolfe shrinkage (my instantiation; the paper's experiments use l2, l1 and n-support norm balls). It has the two ingredients that GLyDER lacks (direction = LMO, in an arbitrary norm, of a gradient memory; signed pairing with a positive part) and lacks the one that makes the placement a statement about stochastic methods: the gradient in the pairing is exact, so there is no sampled estimator, no unbiasedness issue, no variance-reduced estimator, no added Euclidean step, and the rate is O(1/k) on a compact set; the memory also contains the current gradient (weight delta_k). The verdict stays published_close; the row should cite GLyDER (stochastic, Euclidean) and HFW (LMO of a memory, deterministic) together. Two weaker neighbours: random pursuit (Locatello et al.: the atom z is drawn independently of the gradient and the step is the projection (<grad f, z> / L) z, exact gradients) and lazy conditional gradients (Braun et al.: a cached vertex y is reused as the direction when its pairing with the current gradient, c(x - y), exceeds Phi / K; the step size is open-loop or min{1, Phi / (K C)}; online and stochastic versions exist, without variance reduction). S9 and S11 returned no stochastic method whose length is the pairing of a fresh or independent stochastic gradient with the LMO direction of a memory.

*Queries.* S9 [extended]: Frank-Wolfe with momentum or averaged gradients: direction from the linear minimization oracle of the gradient average (past information), step size set by the inner product of the current stochastic gradient with that direction ('smooth step size' OR 'short step' OR 'directional derivative'), stochastic 'heavy ball' conditional gradient unbiased step length. S11 (text in the previous entry). S3 [extended]: 'lazy' OR 'lazified' conditional gradient stochastic variance-reduced SVRF: cached vertex / previous linear minimization oracle output reused across iterations, accepted by inner product with the current variance-reduced gradient estimator, fewer LMO calls per epoch. HFW itself was not returned by S9; it was opened from memory.

**split placement: gamma sharp(m) + gamma_c c, map on the predictable part and sampled correction added linearly (stage-1 verdict not_found)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Nothing found. Documents in which the position of the correction relative to the oracle was checked: Nazykov et al., arXiv:2406.06788 (Algorithms 3, 7, 10; Eq. (10)-(12)) [text saved by the sampling-accel agent]; Nandhan et al., arXiv:2605.25255 (Algorithm BSFW) [opened]; Combettes et al., arXiv:2009.14114 (Template 3) [opened]; Akhtar, Rajawat, arXiv:2107.06534v2 (Algorithms 1 and 3) [opened]; Pethick et al., arXiv:2506.01913 (Algorithms 1-2) [w2 text]; Sfyraki, Wang, arXiv:2506.04192v4 (Algorithm 4) [opened].

*Explanation.* In every Frank-Wolfe / LMO method opened, the whole estimator (memory plus correction) is the argument of the oracle. The structures that come nearest are not the split: in the inner loop of AdaSVRF (as in conditional gradient sliding) the oracle argument is the estimator plus a linear proximity term (1/eta) H (y - x_t), still inside the oracle; in MOST-FW it is a STORM-tracked gradient plus a penalty gradient; Algorithm 4 of 'Lions and Muons' puts the STORM correction inside the LMO. BSFW and arXiv:2506.01913 state the obstacle (the LMO of an unbiased estimator is biased) and answer it with momentum or variance reduction inside the oracle, not with a linear correction outside. Two extended queries worded for this literature returned no update of the form 'LMO of the predictable part plus linear stochastic correction'.

*Queries.* S8 [extended]: hybrid update combining a 'linear minimization oracle' (Frank-Wolfe) direction computed from the snapshot full gradient or averaged gradient with an additive plain stochastic gradient-difference correction step (correction applied outside the oracle), projection-free variance-reduced method, one LMO call per epoch. S16 [extended]: update that adds a Frank-Wolfe OR 'linear minimization oracle' direction and a plain negative stochastic gradient step in the same iteration ('hybrid Frank-Wolfe gradient' OR 'Frank-Wolfe plus gradient step' OR 'conditional gradient with gradient correction'), LMO applied to the deterministic or averaged part and stochastic part kept linear, unbiased expected step.

**damped SAGA-Muon (polar step of m + theta c, theta < 1; theta = 1/n is the SAG direction) and the pathwise MISO / Finito reading of the joint step (stage-1 verdict not_found)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Shah, 'Frank-Wolfe variants for minimization of a sum of functions', arXiv:1805.10200v4 [opened; PDF text read: abstract, Section 3.2, Algorithm 4]; Nazykov et al., arXiv:2406.06788, Algorithm 10 'SAGA SARAH Frank-Wolfe' and Lemma B.27 [saved text re-read]; Negiar et al., arXiv:2002.11860, paragraph 'Comparison with Mokhtari et al.' and Appendix G [text saved by w2 re-read]; Nandhan et al., arXiv:2605.25255 [opened; abstract and headings of Appendix C.3: L-SVRG, SAGA, SAG estimators]; Lu, Freund, arXiv:1807.07680 [w2 text: abstract, contributions, statement of Lemma 2.1, Section 3.1, sampling rule].

*Explanation.* On the LMO side the two endpoints of the damping are published and nothing in between was found. theta = 1/n (SAG or incremental aggregated gradient memory inside an LMO): Negiar et al. and Lu-Freund (already in the row), plus Shah 2018 (Algorithm 4: cyclic aggregated gradient d_k = (1/m)[m d_{k-1} - grad f_[k](x_{k-m}) + grad f_[k](x_k)] fed to the oracle, step 2/(k+1)) and the SAG estimator of BSFW (2026). theta = 1 (SAGA inside an LMO): SagaFw, SAGA FW (Algorithm 3 of the zoo), BSFW. Negiar et al. discuss the two scalings as a bias-variance choice (their (1/n) f_i' against the unbiased f_i' of Mokhtari et al., which 'induces higher variance' and needs a vanishing momentum), but as two methods, not as a family in theta. The only mixing parameter found is the lambda of SAGA SARAH FW, which mixes a SARAH recursion with a SAGA-type table term (the ZeroSARAH construction): a different estimator from m + theta c. S6 returned SVAG / B-SAGA in the Euclidean setting only. MISO reading: S5 and S14 returned Lu-Freund (generalized stochastic Frank-Wolfe = randomized dual coordinate mirror descent; linear rate when the regularizer is strongly convex with respect to the l2 norm; uniform index) and Mairal's MISO papers, and nothing that identifies a normalized, steepest-descent or polar step of a SAGA estimator with MISO, Finito or SDCA.

*Queries.* S5 [extended]: 'generalized conditional gradient' OR 'generalized Frank-Wolfe' variance-reduced SVRG OR SAGA OR SAG finite-sum strongly convex regularizer 'linear convergence' unconstrained dual coordinate interpretation 'non-Euclidean' OR 'arbitrary norm' OR 'squared norm' regularizer. S6 [extended]: stochastic Frank-Wolfe finite-sum 'biased' gradient estimator interpolating SAG and SAGA inside the linear minimization oracle, damping OR weight on the control-variate correction, 'bias-variance' tradeoff conditional gradient. S14 [extended]: 'MISO' OR 'Finito' OR 'incremental majorization-minimization' equivalence with stochastic Frank-Wolfe OR 'linear minimization oracle' using SAG-type gradient memory, primal iterate is the minimizer of the aggregated surrogate, dual coordinate ascent, generalized conditional gradient finite-sum.

**bounded-gain polar map s / sqrt(s^2 + eps^2) with eps proportional to the innovation norm, on a variance-reduced estimator (stage-1 verdict published_close: PRISM, arXiv:2602.03096)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Li, Fan, Li, 'Variance-Adaptive Muon: Pre-Orthogonalization Variance Modulation for Efficient Language Model Pretraining', arXiv:2601.14603v2 (1 Sep 2026) [opened; PDF text read: Section 3 (Eq. (4) and (8)), Algorithm 1, Section 4]; this is the paper that two stage-1 reports listed as 'abstract only', to be opened by the owner of the bounded-gain row. Dubois-Taine, Bach, Berthet, Taylor, 'Fast Stochastic Composite Minimization and an Accelerated Frank-Wolfe Algorithm under Parallelization', arXiv:2205.12751v3 [opened; abstract, Definition 5 and Algorithm 2 located by keyword, not read in full]. Ma, Zhang, He, 'Schattor: Schatten-family methods for deep learning optimization', arXiv:2606.15702v1 [abstract only]. PRISM abstract page arXiv:2602.03096 [opened as a spot check of the stage-1 identifier].

*Explanation.* Muon-NSR applies, entrywise and before Newton-Schulz, the map M / sqrt(M^2 + alpha Gamma + eps) to the momentum, Gamma being an exponential average of the squared innovations (M_{t-1} - G_t)^2 (Algorithm 1, line 5); the paper writes the gain as (1 + NSR^2)^(-1/2). It is the lab's scalar map with an offset built from the innovation of the estimator, but applied to entries instead of singular values, with a running average of squared innovations instead of the norm of the current one, a momentum estimator, a fixed-length step and LLM experiments. PRISM (stage 1) acts on the spectral side with the current innovation and remains closer; Muon-NSR should be cited next to it. From the Frank-Wolfe side: replacing the oracle by an average of oracle calls on randomly perturbed arguments (randomized smoothing of the support function, Dubois-Taine et al.) gives a smoothed, bounded-gain oracle with a fixed smoothing scale and exact gradients. S10 returned nothing with a smoothing parameter tied to gradient noise other than works stage 1 already has (the '[Jia+26]' quoted by the search summary is, judging by its description, the Jiang-Mhammedi-Mohri-Mokhtari paper that stage 1 already reports, arXiv:2602.08232; I did not open the page that cites it). Schattor (Schatten-norm LMOs between SGD and Muon) was not read beyond the abstract.

*Queries.* S10 [extended]: regularized OR smoothed 'linear minimization oracle' over the spectral norm ball (soft polar factor, singular values s/sqrt(s^2+eps^2)) with smoothing parameter set adaptively from the stochastic gradient noise or variance, stochastic Frank-Wolfe OR Muon OR Scion, noise-adaptive regularization of the LMO.

**one-shot and lazily refreshed polar-metric SAGA / L-SVRG (stage-1 verdict published_close)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Combettes, Spiegel, Pokutta, arXiv:2009.14114v3 [opened; Template 3, Algorithm 5, Section 4.1].

*Explanation.* The projection-free literature has one family that combines a metric with a variance-reduced estimator: Template 3 of Combettes et al. minimizes, by K Frank-Wolfe iterations, the model <g, x - x_t> + (1/(2 eta_t)) ||x - x_t||_H^2 with g the SVRF, SPIDER or CSFW estimator and H_t a clipped diagonal AdaGrad matrix updated at every iteration. The metric is diagonal, accumulated from stochastic gradients and never frozen; nothing is built from the polar decomposition of a gradient. It is farther than PROMISE, OSGM-SVRG and Kereta et al. (stage 1). I ran no query dedicated to this configuration: Frank-Wolfe steps are affine invariant, so this literature has no natural counterpart of a frozen linear metric, and stage 1 spent 22 queries on it. This entry therefore adds little evidence.

*Queries.* None dedicated. AdaSVRF was returned by S4 (text in the first entry). Spot check of a stage-1 identifier: arXiv:2609.11751 abstract page opened (title and authors as reported).

**relaxed (dual-free-SDCA) table inside the polar step (stage-1 verdict not_found)**: closer work found.

*Work.* Nazykov, Shestakov, Solodkin, Beznosikov, Gidel, Gasnikov, 'Stochastic Frank-Wolfe: Unified Analysis and Zoo of Special Cases', arXiv:2406.06788 (PMLR v238 according to the URL returned by S2) [opened by the sampling-accel agent, which reports Algorithms 1-3; I re-read its saved text: introduction, Section 3.3 Eq. (11), Algorithm 7 'DIANA Frank-Wolfe', Lemma B.21, heading of Algorithm 9 'EF21 Frank-Wolfe', Algorithm 10, heading of Algorithm 13 'PP-L-SVRG Frank-Wolfe'].

*Explanation.* DIANA Frank-Wolfe keeps one memory h_i per term, moves it by a fraction, h_i <- h_i + alpha Q(grad f_i(x) - h_i), and feeds the unbiased estimator g = h + (1/n) sum_i Q(grad f_i(x) - h_i) (memory mean plus compressed innovation) to the LMO of an arbitrary compact convex set, followed by the convex-combination step. This is an unbiased estimator on a relaxed memory inside an LMO, which the stage-1 report gave as not found (it cites DIANA with a Euclidean proximal step and Lu-Freund with a biased aggregate). With a Bernoulli compressor (keep the difference with probability p, scaled by 1/p) the scheme is a relaxed-table SAGA with independently sampled indices (my remark; the paper uses RandK sparsification in its experiments and alpha = 1/(1 + omega) in Lemma B.21). Differences: the randomness is a compressor applied to every term rather than one sampled index; constrained Frank-Wolfe step with open-loop sizes, sublinear rates; no spectral-norm instance; no link with dual-free SDCA. It is closer than the row's references on the axis 'relaxed memory + unbiased estimator + nonlinear oracle'; the unconstrained nuclear-scaled step with such a table, and its relation to dual-free SDCA, remain not found.

*Queries.* No dedicated query: found by re-reading a text that stage 1 had saved. Related queries S5, S6, S14 (texts in the damped entry) returned nothing on relaxed tables beyond Lu-Freund.

**Katyusha-Muon: loopless Katyusha with the nuclear-scaled polar y step (stage-1 verdict not_found; closest: the general-norm template of Katyusha Section 6)**: closer work found.

*Work.* Song, Cui, Xia, Jiang, 'Accelerated Stochastic Greedy Coordinate Descent by Soft Thresholding Projection onto Simplex' (ASGCD), arXiv:1702.07842v2 (24 Jun 2017; venue not checked) [candidate recalled from memory, opened directly by identifier, title confirmed in the PDF; PDF text read: abstract, introduction, Eq. (2)-(3), Algorithm 3 and the paragraph after it, Theorem 2, Remarks 3-4, first paragraphs of Section IV, Lemma 17].

*Explanation.* ASGCD is a published and tested instance of Katyusha's non-Euclidean template, for the l1 norm. Each f_j is assumed L1-smooth with respect to the l1 norm (dual norm l_infinity, Eq. (2)). Algorithm 3 is Katyusha with (d) the SVRG estimator mu_s + (1/b) sum_{j in B} (grad f_j(x_{k+1}) - grad f_j(x~_s)), (e) the y step y_{k+1} = SOTOPO(estimator, x_{k+1}, lam, eta), the exact minimizer of <g, h> + (1/(2 eta)) ||h||_1^2 + lam ||x + h||_1, which without the l1 term is l1-norm steepest descent, i.e. greedy coordinate descent (as the paper recalls), and (f) a mirror step with a p-norm divergence; the paper states that all other parts are standard steps of the Katyusha framework. The error of the estimator is bounded in the dual (l_infinity) norm (Lemma 17). Experiments on Lasso with b = 1 and b = n against Katyusha, SVRG and accelerated full gradient. So an SVRG estimator inside the dual-norm-scaled steepest-descent step of a non-Euclidean norm, inside Katyusha, instantiated and tested, is published since 2017, whereas stage 1 found no instantiation of the template. What still differs: l1 norm on vectors instead of the spectral norm on matrices (a few largest coordinates instead of a polar factor); epoch-based, proximal, uniform sampling; O(1/S^2) rate for convex objectives, no linear rate; no loopless version. S15 did not return ASGCD nor any other instantiation (the engine returned the Katyusha paper itself); S12 returned no non-accelerated greedy-coordinate SVRG or SAGA.

*Queries.* S12 (text in the first entry). S15 [extended]: Katyusha framework instantiated with a non-Euclidean steepest descent (gradient) step: 'l1-norm' OR 'nuclear norm' OR 'trace norm' OR 'spectral norm' OR 'Schatten' smoothness, accelerated stochastic variance-reduced greedy / steepest descent, mirror descent step with p-norm divergence, experiments.

**lazy split L-SVRG-Muon (one factorization per reference point) and anchor-PolarGrad L-SVRG (stage-1 verdicts not_found)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Akhtar, Rajawat, 'Zeroth and First Order Stochastic Frank-Wolfe Algorithms for Constrained Optimization', arXiv:2107.06534v2 [opened; PDF text read: Algorithm 1 MOST-FW, Section II.D, Algorithm 3 T-MOST-FW, statement of Lemma 5]; Braun, Pokutta, Zink, arXiv:1610.05120v4 [opened; Algorithms 2-3, Section 5.1]; Lan, Pokutta, Zhou, Zink, 'Conditional Accelerated Lazy Stochastic Gradient Descent', arXiv:1703.05840v5 [opened; abstract and introduction lines only]; Osokin, Alayrac, Lukasewitz, Dokania, Lacoste-Julien, 'Minding the Gaps for Block Frank-Wolfe Optimization of Structured SVMs', arXiv:1605.09346v1 [opened; abstract, Section 3.1 lines]; Yurtsever, Sra, Cevher, 'Conditional Gradient Methods via Stochastic Path-Integrated Differential Estimator', PMLR v97 [opened; Algorithm 2].

*Explanation.* The Frank-Wolfe literature has several ways of not recomputing the oracle, none with a linear correction in between. T-MOST-FW: with a STORM-tracked gradient s_k, the LMO is called only if ||s_k - v_{k-1}|| >= delta_k; otherwise the previous oracle output z_{k-1} is reused unchanged (a residual-triggered refresh as in CacheMuon, here with a momentum-based variance-reduced estimator; the reused direction is not corrected). Lazy conditional gradients reuse a cached vertex when its pairing with the current gradient is large enough (weak separation oracle); stochastic versions exist (LOCG, CALSGD), and CALSGD only remarks that variance reduction could be added. Block-coordinate Frank-Wolfe caches oracle calls with a gap-based cache-hit rule. For the anchor step: in SVRF and SPIDER-FW the first step of an epoch is an LMO step on the exact (or large-batch) gradient, but so are all the following ones, on the estimator; no method was found whose only oracle call is at the reference point, with Euclidean variance-reduced steps in between. Nothing closer than CacheMuon and the works listed by stage 1; T-MOST-FW is worth one line in the lazy row as the Frank-Wolfe analogue of a residual-triggered refresh.

*Queries.* S3 (text in the radial entry) and S8 (text in the split entry).

**PAGE-Muon with the nuclear-scaled step (stage-1 verdict published_close: Sarah Frank-Wolfe, arXiv:2304.11737)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Yurtsever, Sra, Cevher, 'Conditional Gradient Methods via Stochastic Path-Integrated Differential Estimator', ICML 2019, PMLR v97 (yurtsever19b.pdf) [opened; PDF text read: Algorithm 2, parameter choices, conclusion; stage 1 had the abstract page only]; Beznosikov, Dobre, Gidel, arXiv:2304.11737 [text saved by the sampling-accel agent: discussion after Theorem 4.1, conclusion]; Nazykov et al., arXiv:2406.06788 [saved text: Algorithm 2 heading].

*Explanation.* Confirms stage 1. SPIDER-FW: v_1 is the exact or large-batch gradient at the start of an epoch, v_k = grad f_S(x_k) - grad f_S(x_{k-1}) + v_{k-1}, w_k = lmo(v_k), x_{k+1} = x_k + eta (w_k - x_k) with eta = 2/(s+1); its conclusion leaves the strongly convex case open, as does the conclusion of Sarah Frank-Wolfe ('in order to get fast rates in the strongly convex case'). No recursive estimator inside a dual-norm-scaled or short-step LMO update was found.

*Queries.* S13 and S18 (texts in the first entry).

**importance sampling inside the polar step (stage-1 verdict published_close: Katyusha2)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Osokin et al., arXiv:1605.09346v1 [opened; abstract, Section 3.1 lines]. Sampling rule checked by keyword in: Lu-Freund arXiv:1807.07680 (uniform index), Negiar et al. arXiv:2002.11860 (uniform), Reddi et al. arXiv:1607.08254 and Hazan-Luo arXiv:1602.02101 (uniform) [w2 texts]; Nazykov et al. (uniform); ASGCD ('with equal probability'); Shah (cyclic).

*Explanation.* A stochastic Frank-Wolfe method with non-uniform sampling of the index does exist: block-coordinate Frank-Wolfe with 'gap sampling' draws a training example with probability proportional to its block gap. It is a block method on the dual of a structured SVM, each block with its own oracle; there is no reweighted innovation inside a nonlinear step, so it is not closer than Katyusha2. No variance-reduced Frank-Wolfe with non-uniform sampling was met in the documents opened. No dedicated query (stage 1 ran two on Frank-Wolfe with importance sampling).

*Queries.* None dedicated.

**minibatch SAGA-Muon with a batch of the order of the rank (stage-1 verdict published_close: Phases of Muon, arXiv:2605.09552)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Carlson, Cevher, Carin, 'Stochastic Spectral Descent for Restricted Boltzmann Machines', AISTATS 2015, PMLR v38, first page 111 (proceedings.mlr.press/v38/carlson15.pdf) [opened; PDF text read: introduction, Section 3.4]. ASGCD, arXiv:1702.07842v2, Lemma 17 [opened].

*Explanation.* An earlier statement of the batch-versus-rank issue for the nuclear-scaled step, without variance reduction: Section 3.4 of the 2015 paper has a paragraph 'Effect of mini-batches smaller than min(M, J)' (with minibatches of size r << min(M, J) the gradient has rank at most 2r, the relation sign being lost in the extracted text; 'Learning is noisier but still provides competitive results') and a paragraph on noisy gradient estimation (under additive white noise the singular values are biased upwards, the subspaces are not). Phases of Muon (stage 1) is far more precise and remains the reference; the 2015 paper should be cited at F1 and in this row as the first occurrence. On the estimator side, ASGCD's Lemma 17 bounds the l_infinity-norm (dual-norm) error of a minibatch SVRG estimator with the factor (n - b) / (b (n - 1)): a dual-norm variance that does decrease with b in that geometry, to set against the remark of Katyusha Section 6.2 quoted by stage 1.

*Queries.* S17 (text in the first entry), used to locate the stochastic spectral descent papers.

**claim: fixed-length steps keep a floor even with a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator (stage-1 verdict published_close)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Pethick et al., arXiv:2506.01913 [w2 text: paragraph 'Weight decay & constrained problems' and the short-step paragraph]; Yang, He, Li, 'Convergence of Spectral Descent for Non-smooth Optimization', arXiv:2605.26977v1 (26 May 2026) [opened; abstract, lines of Theorem 4.1 and Remark 4.3, figure captions].

*Explanation.* This literature states the deterministic mechanism and stops there. arXiv:2506.01913 notes that with an open-loop step 'it is possible to step too far in the direction given by the lmo, since the stepsize does not decrease near a critical point', and introduces the short step for that reason. Yang-He-Li prove linear convergence of spectral descent (Muon without momentum) on non-smooth sharp convex problems with a geometrically decaying step proportional to the initial distance to the solution set, with exact subgradients: a third reference for the 'geometric schedule' remedy that stage 1 found in arXiv:2601.13474 and arXiv:2606.30509. No statement combining a fixed-length LMO step with a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator was found beyond Li-Tang-Zou and the SignSVRG remark already reported.

*Queries.* S13 (returned arXiv:2605.26977) and S11; no dedicated query.

**landscape: finite-sum variance reduction for Muon through its reading as stochastic Frank-Wolfe / LMO optimizer (stage-1 verdict not_found)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Sfyraki, Wang, 'Lions and Muons: Optimization via Stochastic Frank-Wolfe under Heavy-Tailed Noise', arXiv:2506.04192v4 (25 Aug 2026) [opened; PDF text read: abstract, the section introducing Algorithm 4, caption of Table 1, related-work appendix]; Sadiev, 'From Imperfect Information to Controlled Steps: Stochastic Optimization under Heavy-Tailed Noise and Compression', PhD dissertation, KAUST, arXiv:2611.11459v1 (8 Nov 2026, 698 pages) [opened; abstract, Section 1.3.4, comparison table of Chapter 8, keyword scan of the whole text]; Nandhan et al., arXiv:2605.25255 [opened]; Poirion, Pokutta, Takeda, 'Random-Subspace Frank-Wolfe over Strongly Convex Sets', arXiv:2605.24819 [abstract only].

*Explanation.* The variance-reduced variant of 'Lions and Muons' (Algorithm 4) integrates STORM into the stochastic Frank-Wolfe template (momentum-based); its related-work appendix lists the finite-sum variance-reduced Frank-Wolfe methods without instantiating any for Muon. The dissertation of Sadiev (four days old at the date of the check) extends a hierarchy of momentum estimators to LMOs over arbitrary norm balls (the table of its Chapter 8 lists LMO-IGT, LMO-MVR, LMO-SOM-V1 and LMO-SOM-V2), the spectral-norm ball being named as the Muon case; a keyword scan of its text shows no SAGA, SVRG or PAGE estimator inside an LMO. S2 and S13 returned no Frank-Wolfe paper with a finite-sum estimator on a spectral-norm ball; the 2026 Frank-Wolfe papers that do use SAGA / L-SVRG / SAG (BSFW) work on l1 and nuclear-norm balls.

*Queries.* S2 and S13 (texts in the first entry).

*Statements of the first reports found wrong or unsupported.*

- OVER-GENERAL [landscape report, item 1, field 'difference']: 'Every finite-sum variance-reduced method found inside a non-Euclidean or LMO-type step uses a FIXED-LENGTH step (unit-norm LMO, sign, or Euclidean normalization) with a decaying or accuracy-tied step size and a sublinear guarantee.' True of the works that report names, not of the literature: AdaSVRF (arXiv:2009.14114, Template 3 line 8) takes a gap-proportional short step computed from an SVRG estimator inside an LMO update; ASGCD (arXiv:1702.07842, Algorithm 3) takes the dual-norm-scaled l1 steepest-descent step on an SVRG estimator; Garber-Kaplan (arXiv:1809.10477, Theorem 2) need O(log 1/eps) epochs for a variance-reduced generalized conditional gradient on strongly convex finite sums (with a Euclidean proximal oracle).
- UNSUPPORTED [estimator report, item 3]: closer_than_table = false, with 'No work was found that feeds an SDCA-type or DIANA-type relaxed-table estimator to a polar or spectral-LMO step'. The sentence is literally true for a spectral LMO, but 'nothing closer than the row' does not hold: DIANA Frank-Wolfe (Eq. (11) and Algorithm 7 of arXiv:2406.06788) feeds a DIANA-type relaxed-memory unbiased estimator to a generic LMO, and the same paper has EF21 Frank-Wolfe (Algorithm 9). The sampling-accel report had opened that paper and listed 'MARINA FW, EF21 FW' among its variants without DIANA FW.
- SUPERSEDED, not an error of fact [sampling-accel report, item 4, and item 1 point (iii)]: 'Nothing closer was found' than the template of Katyusha Section 6, and Katyusha2 'is not tested in a non-Euclidean setting'. The second statement concerns the Katyusha paper and is correct, but the template has a published and tested non-Euclidean instance, ASGCD (arXiv:1702.07842, l1 norm). The verdict for Katyusha-Muon should be published_close with ASGCD as the closest instance; the spectral instance remains not found.
- TOO STRONG [sampling-accel report, item 1, field 'difference']: 'On the LMO / Frank-Wolfe, sign and Muon sides no importance sampling of the index was found at all'. Block-coordinate Frank-Wolfe with gap sampling (Osokin et al., arXiv:1605.09346) samples the index non-uniformly inside a stochastic Frank-Wolfe method. It has no reweighted variance-reduced estimator, so the verdict of that item is unchanged; the sentence should be restricted to variance-reduced estimators fed to an LMO.
- SUPERSEDED, not an error of fact [placement report, item 4, field 'difference']: 'no paper combining the two was found (Q21)' (a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator and a clipped LMO step in a non-Euclidean norm). AdaSVRF combines an SVRG estimator with a capped, gap-proportional LMO step over an arbitrary norm ball, in the constrained Frank-Wolfe form; the unconstrained trust-region form and the spectral norm remain not found.
- LEFT OPEN BY STAGE 1 [placement report and landscape report, lists of other papers]: both list arXiv:2601.14603 as 'abstract only', to be opened by the owner of the bounded-gain row; the estimator report, which owns that row, does not mention it. Opened here (see the bounded-gain entry): an entrywise bounded-gain map with a buffer of squared innovations, applied before Newton-Schulz; it does not change the verdict.
- It is in PMLR v38 (proceedings.mlr.press/v38/carlson15.pdf, opened); its Section 3.4 already discusses minibatches smaller than the matrix dimension, an earlier reference than Phases of Muon for the batch-versus-rank remark of the minibatch row.
