On the Muon side, two works do sample the training examples non-uniformly for a Muon step, both OPENED (PDF text): OPUS, arXiv:2602.05400 (Section 4.2, Section 5.3 Eq. (26)): candidates scored in the 'optimizer-induced update space' (AdamW or Muon, linearized as above) and drawn by Boltzmann sampling p(z) proportional to exp(U_z / tau); OptiSelect, arXiv:2610.03432: top-utility batch from an oversampled pool, with a theory of how the sign and polar maps limit the gain of selection ('discriminability collapse').

*Explanation.* Neither is the configuration: the selection is utility-driven and biased (no 1 / (n p_i) weight; the words unbiased or reweight do not occur in the OPUS method sections), the aim is data efficiency and not variance, and there is no finite-sum estimator. They do contradict, in a weak sense, the first-stage sentence that no non-uniform sampling of the index was found at all on the Muon side, and they are the natural citations for 'which samples to feed a polar step'.

*Queries.* W12 [extended], W13 [standard] (see above).

**Minibatch SAGA-Muon with a batch of the order of the rank**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Nothing closer than Phases of Muon (arXiv:2605.09552, confirmed in the NeurIPS 2026 list) and Li-Tang-Zou. Seen in the curated list only (NOT opened): 'The Best Optimizer Depends on Batch Size', arXiv:2610.08975 (ranking of Muon, SOAP, Shampoo changes with the batch size; noisy-quadratic analysis); 'VENUS: Decoupling the Samples in MARS Benefits Small-Batch Training', NeurIPS 2026 OPT workshop (neurips.cc/virtual/2026/161524, abstract read): a MARS-type estimator with two independent minibatches inside an LMO, batch-dependent comparison.

*Explanation.* No minibatch finite-sum memory inside a polar step other than Li-Tang-Zou's SVRG; the two additions concern streaming estimators.

*Queries.* No dedicated WebSearch call; curated list, OPT 2026 list, arXiv listings.

**PAGE-Muon with the nuclear-scaled step**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Nothing closer than Sarah Frank-Wolfe (arXiv:2304.11737) and Compressed Gluon (arXiv:2604.10689). Their Semantic Scholar citation lists (10 and 5 citing papers) contain no recursive finite-sum estimator inside a dual-norm-scaled step; FedCALM (opened) describes arXiv:2604.10689 as 'the VR technique of VR-MARINA' applied to Muon without local steps.

*Explanation.* No new PAGE / SARAH / SPIDER / MARINA estimator inside a Muon-type step in the curated list, in the arXiv listings of August to November 2026 or in the workshop lists.

*Queries.* No dedicated WebSearch call; citation lists of arXiv:2304.11737, 2406.06788, 2604.10689; listings as in the first entry.

**Katyusha-Muon (loopless Katyusha with the nuclear-scaled polar y step)**: nothing closer.

*Work.* Nothing closer than Katyusha2. Seen in listings only (NOT opened): 'A simple adaptive accelerated optimistic dual averaging method for unconstrained convex optimization in p-norm geometry', arXiv:2611.05334 (deterministic, p from 2 to infinity, curated summary); MONA and LMO-IGT (already known).

*Explanation.* No accelerated variance-reduced method with a spectral steepest-descent step in the curated list (searched for Katyusha, accelerated, Nesterov, negative momentum among Muon / LMO entries), the arXiv listings or the NeurIPS / ICML 2026 lists.

*Queries.* No dedicated WebSearch call (six were run by the first stage); listings as in the first entry.

*Statements of the first reports found wrong or unsupported.*

- Estimator group, item 3 (verdict not_found): 'No work was found that feeds an SDCA-type or DIANA-type relaxed-table estimator to a polar or spectral-LMO step' and '#22 returned EF21-Muon and its follow-ups only'. Contradicted by two October 2026 preprints that I opened: FedCALM (arXiv:2610.23434, Algorithm 1: NS_q(grad f_i - c_i + c) with control variates moved by EF21-compressed differences) and NBCLT (arXiv:2610.17913, Algorithm 1: c_i <- beta2 c_i + (1 - beta2) c_hat_i for sampled clients, estimator fed to a spectral-ball projection). The verdict should be published_close.
- Estimator group, item 1 (verdict not_found, 'nothing closer than the works of the row'): NBCLT (arXiv:2610.17913, Algorithm 1 line 9) uses the damped control-variate estimator (1 - beta1)(grad f_i - c_i) + m with a table over sampled clients inside a spectral map (projection, not polar); PAdaMFed (ICLR 2025, Algorithm 1) puts a weighted control-variate estimator inside a fixed-length normalized step. Both are closer than Negiar et al. / Lu-Freund on the damped-estimator axis; the statement stays true only for 'polar step with nuclear scaling'.
- Landscape group, item 4 (sweep): 'No finite-sum variance-reduced Muon-type method was found beyond the three already cited'. Incomplete: FedCALM (arXiv:2610.23434) and NBCLT (arXiv:2610.17913) are two more control-variate (SCAFFOLD-type table over clients) spectral methods; both are in the curated list that this pass used.
- Landscape group, caveat: 'Workshop and conference listings could not be checked: the OPT 2026 page (opt-ml.org) lists no accepted paper yet ... no HiLD 2026 nor NeurIPS 2026 listing was opened'. The lists are public and were read here: NeurIPS 2026 OPT workshop (neurips.cc/virtual/2026/workshop/137528, 150 entries), ICML 2026 HiLD workshop (icml.cc/virtual/2026/workshop/54077), NeurIPS 2026 papers (9,127 titles), ICML 2026 papers (6,628 titles). No finite-sum variance-reduced Muon paper in them; the OPT list contains the workshop version of FedCALM and the step-size papers quoted in the radial entry.
- Placement group, item 2: 'Linearizations of the polar map were found only in proofs and noise analyses' and 'No optimizer applying D polar(m) or D sharp(m) to a sampled correction ... was found'. OptiSelect (arXiv:2610.03432, Eq. (2), Appendix B.7) defines O(G) = D msign(A_t)[G] on per-sample gradients as its scoring operator and analyses it (Theorem 4, Appendices D.3-D.4). The second sentence remains true for the update step; the first is too strong.
- Placement group, items 3-4: 'No Muon variant with this pairing was found (Q15)' and the description of the Muon side as scaling by norms only (AdaGO, NAMO, OptMuon). Algorithm 2 of arXiv:2605.18999 (Scale-Calibrated Muon) sets the step along the Muon direction of the momentum to (||m||_* - ||g - m||_*)_+ / L, a certified lower bound of the pairing <g, u(m)> with a positive part and the descent bound a^2 / (2L); the landscape group listed this paper from its abstract only ('three adaptive rules for the length'). ExpertMuon-Compass (arXiv:2610.04140) uses [cos(O, G)]_+. The statement that no paper has a predictable polar direction with an unbiased signed length still holds.
- Placement group, item 4 (clipped half): 'no paper combining the two was found (Q21)' (non-Euclidean clipping and a variance-reduced estimator). NBCLT (arXiv:2610.17913) clips a control-variate estimator by projection onto a norm ball, the spectral ball included.
- Sampling group, item 1: 'On the LMO / Frank-Wolfe, sign and Muon sides no importance sampling of the index was found at all'. On the Muon side OPUS (arXiv:2602.05400, in the ICML 2026 list) and OptiSelect (arXiv:2610.03432) sample the training examples non-uniformly for a Muon step, with utilities computed in a linearized Muon geometry; they do not reweight, so the conclusion on the configuration is unchanged.
- Metric group, items 1-2: 'one-sided Shampoo (accumulated, never frozen, no variance reduction)'. True of the algorithm box of the theory paper only: the standard scalable Shampoo computes its preconditioners 'once every few hundred steps' and applies the stale ones in between (Anil et al., arXiv:2002.09018, paragraph 'Delayed preconditioners', PDF text read). A lazily refreshed gradient-built matrix metric applied linearly is standard practice outside variance reduction.
- Metric group, items 1-2: the list of closest works on the polar side stops at CacheMuon. Missing: Mime (arXiv:2008.03606, 2020: SVRG-corrected gradients through server statistics frozen during the round, update linear in the gradient by design), OPUS (Feb 2026: frozen Newton-Schulz operator applied linearly to candidate gradients, earlier than CacheMuon) and OptiSelect (Oct 2026: right-sided (A^T A + eps I)^(-1/2) formed once and reused for 10 scoring calls).
- Metric group, caveats, hit 'not opened' arXiv:2611.05075: its title in the arXiv listing is 'Variance Reduction Note 4: The Convergence of SVRG' (title only, not opened); it is irrelevant to the polar-metric rows.
- Checked and found correct (no error): OSGM-SVRG (arXiv:2609.11751v2: Algorithm 3, Theorem 4.8, 'Update P_k every m inner iterations', decay factor c <= 1; PDF text read); FedMuon of Liu et al. (arXiv:2510.27403: Eq. (3) and Algorithm 1 line 7; PDF text read); the six authors and the STORM section of arXiv:2605.11850; the venues quoted for Phases of Muon, Denoise First, Orthogonal Updates for the Win and Parshakova et al. (NeurIPS 2026 list) and for Spectral Clipping, LiMuon, Naganuma et al., Li-Tang-Zou and Vaswani et al. (ICML 2026 list).

*Other papers reported.*

- arXiv:2610.17913v1 - Linxuan Pan, Shucheng Yang, Junchi Yang, 'NBCLT: Parameter-Agnostic Norm-Ball Clipped Local Training' (13 Oct 2026). OPENED (PDF text: Sections 2.2-3.2, Algorithm 1, Theorem 5). Damped control-variate estimator (1 - beta1)(grad f_i - c_i) + m, EMA-relaxed table for sampled clients, projection onto a norm ball (spectral ball: singular-value clipping), predict-and-correct. Closest published work for the damped, relaxed-memory and clipped rows; cite also in the bounded-gain row.
- arXiv:2610.23434v1 - Bystrov, Chirkov, Medyakov, Bylinkin, Beznosikov, 'Bridging Compressed Communication and Local Steps via Spectral Method in Federated Learning' (FedCALM, 16 Oct 2026; workshop version 'Federated Learning via Spectral Methods with Compressed Communication and Local Steps' in the NeurIPS 2026 OPT list). OPENED (PDF text: Sections 2-4, Algorithms 1-2). SCAFFOLD-type corrected gradient inside Newton-Schulz, control variates updated by EF21; remark that the LMO update does not shrink near the optimum. Base row, relaxed-memory row, Section 5.1.
- arXiv:2610.03432v1 - Simin Fan, Alireza Abdollahpoorrostam, Martin Jaggi, 'OptiSelect: How Does the Optimizer Shape Data Curriculum?' (2 Oct 2026). OPENED (PDF text: Eq. (2), Theorem 4, Appendices B.7, D.3, D.4). Frechet derivative of the polar map at the momentum applied to per-sample gradients; right-preconditioned proxy G (A^T A + eps I)^(-1/2) reused across scoring calls. Tangent row, polar-metric row, F7, importance-sampling row.
- arXiv:2602.05400 - Wang, Ouyang, Xu, Hu, Liu, Chen, Zhang, Zheng, Yang, Ren, Liu, Zhang, 'OPUS: Towards Efficient and Principled Data Selection in Large Language Model Pre-training in Every Iteration' (Feb 2026; ICML 2026 list, poster 61926). OPENED (PDF text: Section 4.2, Section 5.3). Linearized Muon lookahead update with a frozen Newton-Schulz operator as a 'sample-independent left-preconditioner'; Boltzmann sampling of candidates. Polar-metric, split and importance-sampling rows.
- arXiv:2605.18999v1 - Demidovich, Chakraborty, Malinovsky, Nedic, Richtarik, 'Distance-Aware Muon: Adaptive Step Scaling for Normalized Optimization' (18 May 2026). OPENED (PDF text: contributions, Section 4, Algorithm 2). Scale-Calibrated Muon: length (||m||_* - ||g - m||_*)_+ / L along the Muon direction of the momentum, descent a^2 / (2L). Radial and mean-scaled rows, F9, and F5 (the inequality <g, u(m)> >= ||m||_* - ||g - m||_* is the one behind F5).
- arXiv:2008.03606v2 - Karimireddy, Jaggi, Kale, Mohri, Reddi, Stich, Suresh, 'Mime: Mimicking Centralized Stochastic Algorithms in Federated Learning' (2020; ICML 2021 according to the search summary). OPENED (PDF text: Section 3, Algorithm 1, Table 5). SVRG-corrected local gradient through optimizer statistics computed at the server point and frozen during the round; update linear in the gradient. Polar-metric rows and the rationale of the metric placement.
- ICLR 2025 - Wenjing Yan, Kai Zhang, Xiaolu Wang, Xuanyu Cao, 'Problem-Parameter-Free Federated Learning' (PAdaMFed). OPENED (proceedings PDF text: Algorithm 1, Section 3.2, ablation paragraph). Control variates over sampled clients plus momentum inside a fixed-length normalized step; its variance-reduced variant is STORM-type. Damped row, base row, Section 5.1.
- OPT 2020 workshop paper 61 (opt-ml.org/papers/2020/paper_61.pdf) - Xun Qian, Hanze Dong, Peter Richtarik, Tong Zhang, 'Error Compensated Loopless SVRG for Distributed Optimization'. OPENED (PDF text: introduction, Algorithm 1). Contraction compressor on the L-SVRG gradient difference with error compensation, reference full gradient linear, linear rates. Split row (mirror placement with a finite-sum estimator).
- arXiv:2609.14339v1 - Ramasinghe, Siriwardhana, Ajanthan, Dolatabadi, Koneputugodage, Avraham, Shevchenko, Snewin, Pajak, Xi, Long (Pluralis Research), 'Communication-Efficient LLM Adaptation over Decentralized GPU Meshes' (13 Sep 2026). OPENED (PDF text: Sections 1 and 3.2, Appendix E statements). Occasional clean anchor gradients define, through the SVD of their EMA, a two-sided linear filter sigma / (sigma + delta) applied to noisy gradients; predictability argument for unbiasedness. Polar-metric and anchor rows.
- arXiv:2610.01515v1 - Junkang Liu, 'FedMIX-P: Mixing Local and Global Preconditioners for Federated Vision and Language Model Training' (1 Oct 2026). OPENED (PDF text: Algorithms 2 and 7). Round-reference preconditioner frozen during the round, mixed with the local one and applied linearly; nonlinear Muon instance with two Newton-Schulz calls. Polar-metric and lazy-split rows.
- arXiv:2601.14603v2 - Jingru Li, Yibo Fan, Huan Li, 'Variance-Adaptive Muon: Pre-Orthogonalization Variance Modulation for Efficient Language Model Pretraining' (v2 1 Sep 2026). OPENED (PDF text, pages 1-7: Section 3, Algorithm 1, Eq. (11)). Entrywise gate M / sqrt(M^2 + alpha Gamma + eps), Gamma an EMA of the squared innovation, before Newton-Schulz. Bounded-gain row.
- arXiv:2602.06880v2 - Zitao Song, Cedar Site Bai, Zhe Zhang, Brian Bullins, David F. Gleich, 'Decoupling Variance and Scale-Invariant Updates in Adaptive Gradient Descent for Unified Vector and Matrix Optimization' (DeVA). OPENED (PDF text, pages 1-5: Sections 1-3, Theorem 3.1). Elementwise signal-to-noise gain on the matrix sign in the Shampoo eigenbasis. Bounded-gain row (optional).
- arXiv:2610.04140v1 - Vaidya, Vinod, Akbarian, Ellendula, Jerzak, Ho, 'ExpertMuon-Compass: Alignment-Guided Step Sizes for Mixture-of-Experts Training' (2 Oct 2026). OPENED (PDF text, pages 1-8: Section 3). Step multiplier from [cos(O, G)]_+ between the orthogonalized direction and the gradient. Mean-scaled row.
- arXiv:2002.09018 - Anil, Gupta, Koren, Regan, Singer, 'Scalable Second Order Optimization for Deep Learning' (2020). OPENED (abs page and PDF text: paragraph 'Delayed preconditioners'). Shampoo preconditioners computed once every few hundred steps. Polar-metric row (lazy refresh of a gradient-built matrix metric is standard practice).
- arXiv:2610.34158 - Ping Li, Vincent Y. F. Tan, 'Last-Iterate Stationarity of Muon with Finitely Many Newton-Schulz Iterations' (25 Oct 2026). ABS PAGE ONLY (fetch tool). Cycle on a convex quadratic without stabilization, convergence at a small constant step with stabilization, non-convergence under persistent noise at constant parameters. F2 and Section 5.1.
- NeurIPS 2026 OPT workshop (list neurips.cc/virtual/2026/workshop/137528, 150 entries read). ABSTRACTS ONLY on the NeurIPS pages: 'Can Muon Adapt Its Stepsize Without Knowing the Solution Distance?' (id 161530); 'A Radial Smoothness Model for LMO-Based Optimization' (161534); 'VENUS: Decoupling the Samples in MARS Benefits Small-Batch Training' (161524); 'NGN-Scion: Making Norm-Constrained LMO Optimizers Robust to the Learning Rate' (161553); 'The Local Geometry of Magnitude and Direction Decoupling' (161516). Length rules for LMO steps and MARS variants; none uses a finite-sum estimator.
- Curated list github.com/JiwenJ/Awesome-Optimizers (raw README and lists/muon-weakly-related.md, lists/muon-resources.md, lists/2022-2025.md; last entry 2026-11-05). OPENED and searched in full. A convenient census of the 2026 Muon literature for the report (about 450 Muon-related entries with summaries); it does not contain OPUS, so it is not complete for papers that are not presented as optimizers.
- Seen in the curated list only, NOT opened: arXiv:2608.01911 (continuous-time analysis of the smoothed polar map G (G^T G + eps I)^(-1/2), PL rates); arXiv:2610.28975 (expected gradient self-outer-products and Hessian structure at initialization, HiLD 2026; neighbour of F10); arXiv:2609.36600 (Muon, ASGO and Adam as second-moment preconditioning under different noise structures; convergence to a neighbourhood); arXiv:2605.26977 (linear convergence of spectral descent on convex, Lipschitz, sharp nonsmooth objectives, exact gradients); arXiv:2605.19811 (LionMuon); arXiv:2610.08975 (optimizer ranking versus batch size); arXiv:2610.18695 (placement of preconditioner refreshes in a curvature-augmented Muon); arXiv:2512.01930 ('SVRG and Beyond via Posterior Correction', ICML 2026 list; abstract page read: Newton-like and Adam-like extensions of SVRG).
- Opened and found not relevant to any configuration: arXiv:2610.27674 (HyMUON, downloaded, not analysed beyond the curated summary); arXiv:1703.07269 (Goldfarb, Iyengar, Zhou: linear convergence of away-step and pairwise stochastic Frank-Wolfe on polytopes with plain stochastic gradients, no variance-reduced LMO input); arXiv:1610.05120 (Braun, Pokutta, Zink: lazified conditional gradients with cached LMO answers, no variance reduction); arXiv:2408.01848 (mirror-descent methods with Markovian noise, abstract only); arXiv:2510.14009 (noise-adaptive layerwise learning rates for LMO methods, abstract only); arXiv:2606.23637 (Muown, abstract only).

*Limits stated by this agent.*

- Budget: 15 WebSearch calls (cap 18), 10 in extended mode and 5 in standard mode. The allowed_domains filter of W1 (github.com) and W3 (openreview.net) was visibly not enforced (results were mostly arXiv pages). The engine's own summaries were used as leads only.
- Most findings of this pass come from listings, not from keyword search: a curated GitHub list read as raw markdown, arXiv monthly listing pages (arxiv.org/list/<category>/2026-MM, nine categories, August to 12 November 2026), the NeurIPS 2026 and ICML 2026 virtual-site lists of papers and workshops, the modded-nanogpt README, and Semantic Scholar per-paper citation lists (graph API, 'citations' of a given arXiv identifier). None of these is a search engine or a keyword-search endpoint; I mention it because the orchestrator may want to judge this reading of rule (3).
- Listings were screened on titles (arXiv, conferences, citation lists) or on the curated summaries: a paper whose title or summary uses another vocabulary is missed. The curated list is selective (OPUS, a data-selection paper that linearizes Muon, is absent from it), and it stops at 2026-11-05; the days after that are covered by arXiv titles only. cs.CV and other categories were not listed. In my id-title tables some identifiers are shifted by one line, so I quote identifiers only from pages or PDFs I opened.
- OpenReview could not be read (browser-verification page, not circumvented). W3 returned two anonymous OpenReview PDFs (hashes c167e4f4... and 8b1da0bf...) that were NOT opened and could not be identified (W8); the search summary of W3, which had access to them, reported no SVRG / SAGA Muon paper, and W8 pointed to the two known momentum-based papers. ICLR 2027 submissions were therefore not checked.
- Semantic Scholar: 24 citation lists obtained; three requests were rate-limited and not retried successfully (arXiv:2605.08980, arXiv:2606.08783, arXiv:2509.26337, the last one already done by the first stage). The index lags for recent papers (no citing paper listed for arXiv:2610.30280, 2610.27747, 2610.19844, 2311.13877).
- Depth of reading. PDF text extracted with pdftotext and read by me for: arXiv:2610.17913, 2610.23434, 2610.03432, 2602.05400, 2605.18999, 2008.03606, 2609.14339, 2610.01515, 2601.14603 (pages 1-7), 2602.06880 (pages 1-5), 2610.04140 (pages 1-8), 2002.09018 (two paragraphs), 2609.11751, 2510.27403, 2605.11850 (header and section list), the PAdaMFed proceedings PDF and the EC-LSVRG workshop PDF. Formulas are partly garbled by the extraction (Greek letters lost): the hyperparameter choices of Theorem 5 of NBCLT, and the exact form of the EMA of Variance-Adaptive Muon, should be re-read in the PDF before being printed. Abstract only: arXiv:2610.34158, the OPT 2026 workshop papers, arXiv:2512.01930, 2408.01848, 2510.14009, 2606.23637. The HiLD 2026 list was read through the fetch tool (a small model), which may have missed titles.
- Derivations that are mine and come from no paper: FedMuon with one sampled client, one local step and exact gradients is the fixed-length joint SAGA-Muon over clients; the nuclear-scaled polar map divided by the rank is a contraction with delta = 1 / rank (sum of squared singular-value deviations from their mean), so that EC-LSVRG covers a mirror split; Mime-Adam with beta2 = 0 is the diagonal analogue of the polar metric; the reading of NBCLT line 9 as m + theta c with theta = 1 - beta1 and of line 15 as the relaxed table with delta = 1 - beta2; the reading of PAdaMFed step 7 as a predictable part plus a damped innovation. None was checked numerically (no Python allowed).
- Outcome labels: 'closer_work_found' means closer than what the positioning table and the first-stage reports cite, on at least one axis that defines the configuration; in no case was the same algorithm found. The judgement is debatable for the radial row (Scale-Calibrated Muon is deterministic and uses a certificate, GLyDER is Euclidean and unbiased) and for the lazily refreshed metric row (Mime has the variance-reduced structure with a diagonal metric, OptiSelect has the polar metric without variance reduction or step).
- The newest items (FedCALM 16 Oct 2026, NBCLT 13 Oct 2026, OptiSelect 2 Oct 2026, Li-Tan 25 Oct 2026, the OPT 2026 workshop papers) are a few weeks old: the area moves fast and this check is valid for 2026-11-12 only.
- dl/<arXiv id>/paper.pdf and paper.txt for the papers listed above; dl/awesome (curated list); dl/arxiv_listing (title tables); dl/conf (NeurIPS / ICML / OPT pages and title lists); dl/s2 (citation lists); dl/speedrun (README files); dl/ec_lsvrg; dl/padamfed). Nothing else in the repository was modified; no Python and no git command was run.

Each newly cited identifier was opened again by an independent agent. 'Supported': the claim made about the document by the search or refutation report is supported by what was read.

| arXiv:2610.30280 | yes | yes | Abs page: that title, the four authors, v1 Wed 21 Oct 2026, comment '47 pages, 13 figures. A shorter version was presented at the ICML 2026 Workshop on High-dimensional Learning Dynamics'. Text saved by the metric searcher, re-read by me: Theorem 1(iii) 'Assume rank(G) = d <= m. PolarGD is one-step exact at X if and only if E E^T = c I_d ... (a scaled orthogonal matrix if m = d)'; proof B.3: G G^T = H E E^T H = c H^2 and Q = (G G^T)^(-1/2) G = (c H^2)^(-1/2) H E = c^(-1/2) H^(-1) H E; Eq. (10)-(13), Proposition 2 (kappa(A(X)) = kappa(X)); Theorem 3 with f(X+)/f(X) = 1 - alpha (2 - alpha) p(X), alpha = gamma tr(H) (Eq. 17); Section 4.2 and F.2: Q_eps = (G G^T + eps I)^(-1/2) G, sigma -> sigma / sqrt(sigma^2 + eps), 'Tikhonov damping'; App. G: finite Newton-Schulz as damped polar, l_q = 3.4445^q; 'momentum and stochasticity are left out on purpose'. Objective f(X) = (1/2) tr((X - X*)^T H (X - X*)) with H on the row side: F10 is the transposed statement, and the paper's version is an equivalence valid for K >= d. Not in the paper: the metric used as a frozen preconditioner of a stochastic method. |

Precision: the isotropy assumption is on (W - W*)(W - W*)^T and cancels the left (output-side) factor H only; the input second moment is inverted explicitly, so msgn(G) alone is the Newton direction only under Corollary 1. Same mechanism as F10, not the same statement (that is Theorem 1(iii) of arXiv:2610.30280). |

*Limits stated by the checker.*

- Scope. This is a check of the documents cited by the five searchers and four refuters, not a new prior-art search. WebSearch was not used (0 of the 6 calls allowed): no identifier failed to resolve. No identifier points to a wrong paper. One author list is wrong (PMLR v84, shang18a: the sign-normalized report gives 'Shang, Liu, Zhou, Cheng, Tsang, Zhang, Tao, Jiao'; the authors are Shang, Liu, Zhou, Cheng, Ng, Yoshida). Smaller discrepancies: the arXiv listing of ASGCD orders the authors Song, Cui, Jiang, Xia (PDF header: Song, Cui, Xia, Jiang); the workshop version of FedCALM lists four authors, the arXiv version five; the authors of 'A Radial Smoothness Model for LMO-Based Optimization' are shown on the NeurIPS page (Laing, Shulgin, Orvieto, Richtarik); arXiv:1805.10200 (Shah) carries the arXiv comment 'False claim made in one of the theorems'.
- How identifiers were checked. For 166 arXiv identifiers I opened the arXiv abstract page with WebFetch and read title, authors, dates and comments. WebFetch returns the answer of a small model, which refused to quote long passages: abstracts are paraphrases, and an 'abstract level' entry means no more than that. Non-arXiv items: ICML, NeurIPS and PMLR pages and one blog page were opened; two DOIs (IEEE Access, Optimization Methods and Software) resolve but their publisher pages gave no text (empty page, HTTP 403), so only the Crossref metadata record of each DOI was read; OpenReview was not tried.
- How content claims were checked. I checked the header of the main ones against the abstract page, but this is a re-reading of their copies, not an independent download; (b) 25 PDFs that I downloaded myself from arxiv.org with curl (single documents) and converted with pdftotext, for papers the reports had read only through the fetch tool's summary (arXiv:2503.10537, 2503.20762, 2601.13474, 2509.02981, 2302.07155, 2609.34915, 2606.03899, 2305.18929, 2609.13677, 2602.19271, 1605.04131, 1703.00102, 2606.30509, 2410.14054, 2605.17027, 2611.11387, 2605.19619, 2603.28254, 2206.02702, 2608.09763, 2311.05589, 2601.14647, 2610.34158, 2610.31728, 2511.11466). Formulas are partly garbled in such extractions; constants and equation numbers should be re-read in the PDF before being printed.
- What 'claim supported' covers. 'yes' means that the passage the report relies on is in the document as described. It does not cover the derivations that the reports themselves label as their own: the one-worker reading of Theorem J.6 of arXiv:2010.12292 and its composition with Proposition 4.1 of arXiv:2605.08980 (rated 'partly': both ingredients are there, the corollary is in neither paper); Mime with one client and beta2 = 0 as SVRG in a frozen diagonal metric; DIANA Frank-Wolfe with a Bernoulli compressor as a relaxed-table SAGA; the spectral-ball limits of AdaSVRF and of heavy-ball Frank-Wolfe; the coincidence of the one-shot polar-metric iteration on ls-orth with the whitened SAG / SVRG of Yang et al. None was checked numerically (no Python allowed).
- Not verified. Venues that are not on the arXiv page (ASGCD at NIPS 2017, Mime at ICML 2021, Naganuma et al. and OPUS at ICML 2026); the full text of the workshop version of Shulgin et al. (only the ICML listing and its abstract); the full texts of Wang-Wang-Yuan (OMS) and of the IEEE Access paper; the two companion papers of stochastic spectral descent (NeurIPS 2015, IEEE JSTSP 2016); the counts and title screenings that the refuters drew from listings (curated GitHub list, arXiv monthly listings, conference lists, citation lists); every identifier that the reports themselves list as 'not opened'.
- Reading of the verdicts. No document contains one of the study's algorithms as such: the only published_same is the structural identity of F10. The rows whose verdict moves are those where a published work has the defining ingredient in another geometry or setting (NBCLT for the damped and relaxed tables, PRISM for the innovation-dependent offset, Katyusha2 and ASGCD for the general-norm template, Sarah Frank-Wolfe for the exact-refresh recursive estimator, OptiSelect for the Frechet derivative of the polar map, GLyDER / heavy-ball Frank-Wolfe / LBGM / Scale-Calibrated Muon for the radial step). Several of these documents are a few weeks old (NBCLT 13 Oct 2026, FedCALM 16 Oct 2026, OptiSelect 2 Oct 2026, arXiv:2610.30280 21 Oct 2026): the check is valid for 2026-11-12.
- Files and commands. No Python, no git command that changes anything (one read-only 'git status'), nothing else in the repository modified.

## Addendum: check of 2026-11-18 on the additions of M9, M5b and F12-F16

Added on 2026-11-19 from the report of the search agent (the record of 2026-11-12 above is unchanged). Section 3 of `findings.md` gives the conclusions (paragraph, bullet and table row marked "2026-11-19").

**Object.** The statements behind Section 5.11 of `findings.md` (experiment M9, "Muon's own recipe with a SAGA table") and the facts F12-F15 of `notes_theory.md`, in the labels of the search: S1 = the schedule law of Section 5.11 (SAGA table inside a fixed-length spectral step, geometric schedule, linear convergence above a critical decay; freeze without a table); T1 = F12, staleness lemma without rank factor; T2 = F13, recursion, floor O(lr^2), certified geometric schedules, sure orders, almost-sure convergence; T3 = F14, exact scale covariance on least squares; T4 = F15, Lyapunov function of the nuclear-scaled joint step; S4 = critical cooldown, memory rate of the table and reshuffling (Section 5.11, part D). F16 (frozen polar factor of the SAG direction) and the results of M5b (a correction of the tuning of M5) were not objects of this search.

**Method.** One agent, asked to refute the novelty of these statements: 27 web searches, 21 boolean abstract-level queries of the arXiv API, 16 term queries of the OpenReview search API, citation lookups for four identifiers, two blog pages; candidate papers downloaded and converted to text, then read at the depth stated for each. No numerical experiment was run by this agent.

**Verdict: close.** No paper was found that states the schedule law (SAGA/SVRG table inside a fixed-length spectral, sign or normalized step, geometric schedule, linear convergence above a critical decay). But its two halves are published separately, so T2 is close to a corollary. Half 1, the geometric-schedule law for fixed-length steps with a critical ratio and error tracking the step: Goffin 1977; Davis-Drusvyatskiy-MacPhee-Paquette 2018 Thm 5.1 (q^2 = 1-(1-gamma)tau^2); Li-Zhu-So-Lee 2019 for finite sums (dist <= rho^k e_0, rho >= rho_min); Ma et al., Rhee et al., Yang-He-Li 2026 for Muon with exact gradients. Half 2, the staleness-by-path descent inequality for a finite-sum variance-reduced estimator inside an LMO step with arbitrary step sizes: Qin-Liu-Xu SignRVR Thm 4.1 (sign + SVRG); Li-Tang-Zou Lemma F.1 (general norms including spectral, SVRG under reshuffling, which is Muon's recipe fed with SVRG). What remains unpublished as far as these searches go: the SAGA table instead of an SVRG anchor, the rank-free operator-norm constant, the exact scale covariance on least squares, the nuclear-scaled Lyapunov function, and the link between the critical cooldown, the table's memory rate and reshuffling.

### Verdict per statement

**S1 (the schedule law of Section 5.11 (SAGA table inside a fixed-length spectral step, geometric schedule, linear convergence above a critical decay; freeze without a table)).** CLOSE. Not found as stated.

- Evidence of absence: arXiv abstract-level API searches returned 0 relevant hits for Muon / spectral descent / sign / normalized crossed with SVRG / SAGA / finite-sum crossed with linear convergence. 'abs:muon AND (SVRG OR SAGA OR finite-sum)' returns only Huang-Pu-Johansson 2611.18124. Sixteen OpenReview title searches found nothing.
- Algorithm published in SVRG form: Li-Tang-Zou 2602.11557 Algorithm 1 (spectral LMO, reshuffling, optional momentum), with polynomial steps on separable data and no linear rate. Qin-Liu-Xu 2310.15976 do sign + SVRG with arbitrary step sizes, nonconvex.
- Geometric-schedule law published for exact gradients: Goffin 1977 (not read); Davis-Drusvyatskiy-MacPhee-Paquette 1803.02461 Thm 5.1; Kim 2508.13569; Garber-Hazan 1301.4666; for Muon, Ma et al. 2601.13474 (rho >= 1/2), Rhee et al. 2606.30509, Yang-He-Li 2605.26977. For finite sums without variance reduction on sharp problems: Li-Zhu-So-Lee 1907.11687 (dist <= rho^k e_0, rho >= rho_min).
- Chzhen-Schechtman 2305.13187 Section 4.2 argue that linear rates cannot be expected from sign + SVRG, at fixed step only.
- The half 'without a table the schedules freeze' is published in several forms: Huang-Pu-Johansson Thm 3.2; Qin-Liu-Xu Prop. 3.5; Li-Tang-Zou Prop. 4.2; Singh-Mishra-Raut 2607.12360 for additive noise.

**T1 (F12, staleness lemma without rank factor).** CLOSE.

- Path-length staleness of a finite-sum estimator inside an LMO step is published with dimension factors: Qin-Liu-Xu eqs. A.11-A.14 (4dL sum of gamma); Li-Tang-Zou Lemma C.13 with F.1 (max-norm path, factor m).
- For per-sample tables of linear-prediction models it is published in prediction-space norms: Negiar et al. 2002.11860 Lemma 2; Lu-Freund 1807.07680 substitute gradient.
- Euclidean SAG/SAGA: Zhu-Heath-Mitra 2602.05304 Lemma 3.3.
- The rank-free operator-norm / nuclear-norm form using rank-one per-sample gradients was not found; Huang-Pu-Johansson carry a factor r.

**T2 (F13, recursion, floor O(lr^2), certified geometric schedules, sure orders, almost-sure convergence).** CLOSE (corollary-level).

- Descent inequality 'first-order decrease + lr^2 x staleness constant' for LMO steps with a finite-sum estimator and arbitrary deterministic steps: Qin-Liu-Xu (A.15); Li-Tang-Zou (F.2); with momentum under shuffling, Huang-Pu-Johansson (10).
- Bounded-age view of SAGA: Zhu-Heath-Mitra Lemma 3.1. Bregman IAG: Zhang-Dai-Guo-Peng 1711.01136. Delayed gradients in LMO methods: Sadiev et al. 2605.18174.
- The deduction 'floor O(lr^2) at constant lr, linear rate under a geometric schedule with q^2 = 1 - const' is the Goffin / Davis et al. induction on the same recursion.
- Almost-sure convergence under Robbins-Monro steps: Bercu-Fredes-Gbaguidi 2410.03760 for Euclidean SAGA.
- Not found: the statement for a SAGA table in the spectral step with L_eff = L_op + O(A L).

**T3 (F14, exact scale covariance on least squares).** CLOSE.

- Floor Theta(eta^2), 'cooldown is necessary' and a scale-invariance lemma for sign / normalized SGD under noise vanishing at the optimum: Singh-Mishra-Raut (Lemma 2, Props. 2 and 6).
- Deterministic lattice confinement at scale lr: Gonon-Musat-Boumal 2602.11948.
- Normalized homogeneous Markov chain technique for linear convergence: Auger-Hansen 1310.7697.
- Not found: exact scale covariance of the SAGA-polar iteration on least squares in the joint state (W - W_*, table errors), and the time-homogeneous chain u_t/lr_t under a geometric schedule.

**T4 (F15, Lyapunov function of the nuclear-scaled joint step).** CLOSE.

- Euclidean 'gap + stale window' Lyapunov function with step of order 1/(L tau), tau about N: Zhu-Heath-Mitra Lemma 3.7. IAG Lyapunov analyses: Gurbuzbalaban-Ozdaglar-Parrilo 1506.02081 and Vanli-Gurbuzbalaban-Ozdaglar 1608.01713 (abstracts).
- Arbitrary-norm SAGA Lyapunov function for mirror steps: Xie et al. 2205.02273. Bregman IAG: 1711.01136.
- Katyusha Section 6 (general-norm SVRG steepest-descent step) is taken from the repository's earlier record, not re-read.
- Not found: function gap plus operator-norm staleness of the table for the nuclear-scaled polar step.

**S4 (critical cooldown, memory rate of the table and reshuffling (Section 5.11, part D)).** NOT FOUND as a statement (maximal admissible cooldown = memory rate of the table; reshuffling moving it from about e^-1 to e^-2).

- Neighbours: memory factor (1 - 1/n) in a table-error recursion (Negiar et al. Lemma 2, an upper bound); ages bounded by (8N/3) log(NK/delta) with high probability under i.i.d. sampling versus deterministically bounded under cyclic or reshuffled orders (Zhu-Heath-Mitra; the IAG literature).
- SAGA under reshuffling at constant step: Ying-Yuan-Sayed 1708.01383; Malinovsky-Sailanbayev-Richtarik 2104.09342. SAGA with decreasing step: Bercu et al.
- A critical per-epoch decay factor rho_min for incremental methods exists (Li-Zhu-So-Lee) but is set by sharpness, not by memory.
- 'Any finite-sum analysis of Muon under reshuffling': PUBLISHED. Huang-Pu-Johansson 2611.18124 (nonconvex, momentum must cover an epoch, failure for every fixed beta); Li-Tang-Zou (reshuffling + SVRG, separable data). Neither addresses the admissible decay speed.

*Identifiers.* every arXiv number given in the task was checked against the first page of the PDF or the arXiv API, and all exist with the stated content. Completed attributions: 2605.26977 = Yang, He, Li, 'Convergence of Spectral Descent for Non-smooth Optimization'; 2607.12360 = Singh, Mishra, Raut; 2611.18124 = Huang, Pu, Johansson; 2609.32861 = Xie; Vanli et al. = 1608.01713; Gurbuzbalaban et al. = 1506.02081; Mishchenko et al. = 2006.05988; Malinovsky et al. = 2104.09342.

### Limits stated by the checker

Scope: this step was the literature search only. I ran no numerical experiment and did not test whether S1-S4 are true. The proposers' scripts were not executed; I only looked at their prior-art notes.

Reading depth, precisely:
- Read myself from pdftotext output, relevant sections and proofs: Li-Tang-Zou; Qin-Liu-Xu; Huang-Pu-Johansson (statements only); Singh-Mishra-Raut; Zhu-Heath-Mitra; Negiar et al.; SignSVRG Section 4.2; Davis-Drusvyatskiy-MacPhee-Paquette Section 5; Li-Zhu-So-Lee Theorem 2; Garber-Hazan Algorithm 2.
- Keyword scan of the full text plus abstract and selected statements only: Ma et al.; Rhee et al.; Yang-He-Li; Xie 2609.32861; Gonon et al.; Davis-Drusvyatskiy-Charisopoulos 1907.09547 (abstract and finite-sum paragraph: 'the algorithms we develop here do not assume a finite sum structure'); Lu-Freund; MARS-M 2510.21800; 'On the Convergence of Muon and Beyond' 2509.15816v6 (abstract: nonconvex momentum variance reduction, no finite sum or linear rate); Jawanpuria et al. 2610.27747; SPIDER 1807.01695 (no gradient-dominated or restart extension found); Jiang-Li-Zhang 2609.18656; Ringmaster LMO; Bercu et al.; Xie et al. 2205.02273; Kim 2508.13569 (two-column extraction partly garbled).
- Abstract only: Gurbuzbalaban et al.; Vanli et al.; Ying et al.; Malinovsky et al.; Auger-Hansen; PLIAG; Aytekin et al. 1610.05507.
- Title and identifier only: Mishchenko-Khaled-Richtarik.
- Not read: Goffin 1977 (publisher page requires authentication). Its content is reported from a search summary of the abstract and from the descriptions in Davis et al. and Kim.
- Taken from the repository's earlier literature record, not re-opened: Do-Dereich-Jentzen 2608.04607, Katyusha Section 6, SSVR 2406.00489.
- OpenReview abstracts (for example 'When Muon Becomes Gradient Descent', 'Muon Does Not Converge on Convex Lipschitz Functions', both without finite-sum variance reduction) come from the search API payload. Forum pages and reviews were not read: the API began returning a verification challenge after 16 searches, which I did not try to pass.

Limits of 'not found':
- The web search engine honours boolean operators poorly.
- The arXiv API searches abstracts, not full texts. A result buried in an appendix (for example a PL corollary of a sign or LMO variance-reduction paper) would be missed.
- Citation lists of Li-Tang-Zou and Negiar et al. could not be retrieved (rate limit).
- Workshop papers without arXiv versions, theses, code hosts and most non-English blogs were not covered. Only two blog posts were opened; kexue.fm refused the fetch.

Judgement, not publication:
- The statement that T2 follows from Qin-Liu-Xu (A.15) plus a PL inequality and the Goffin / Davis et al. induction is my own composition; no paper writes it.
- It gives the sign / SVRG analogue with a dimension factor, not the rank-free SAGA spectral statement.
- The novelty verdict 'close' therefore rests on how much weight one gives to the SAGA table, the operator-norm constant, the exact scale covariance and the memory-rate threshold.

Housekeeping: the repository was not modified by the search; the downloaded papers and their text extractions were kept in a temporary folder and are not part of the repository. Section, theorem and equation numbers quoted above come from that reading and should be re-read in the papers before being quoted elsewhere; the attributions in F12, F13 and F15 of `notes_theory.md` are taken from this record.
